  - Reason: the vehicle detail page already mounts the hosted `vehicle-gallery` CDN widget (widgets.carous.co.uk) but was passing `template: 'thumbs'`. Switched to `template: 'grid'` to match the huntsmotors app's detail gallery (large primary tile + 2×2 tile grid + "Gallery n" CTA). Also removed the `--color-surface` plate + border that wrapped `.galleryMount` — the widget ships its own card chrome, so the extra surface just read as a stray gray box behind the grid. The reserved-height placeholder now only applies while the slot is still `:empty` (bundle loading), so there's no layout jump but no gray plate once the grid mounts.

Same widget/script wiring as before — only the template + placeholder styling changed.

- 2026-07-21: auto-wow-uk-bespoke-02 (Columbus Vehicles) — clean hero + animated stock strip + modern inventory redesign (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app/themes/auto-wow-uk-bespoke-02/components/Hero.tsx` + `Hero.module.css`, `components/HorizontalStockTicker.module.css`, `lib/inventory.ts`, `pages/used-cars/UsedCarsClient.tsx` + `page.module.css`, `pages/used-cars/InventoryCard.tsx` (new)
  - Reason: the hero read as "foggy" with low contrast — a full-bleed pink brand-primary grid overlay hazed the whole band and the accent word ("Stock.") rendered dark-red over a bright car photo (poor legibility). Rebuilt the scrim as a clean centered readability spotlight + bottom fade (photo stays crisp at the edges), masked the signature grid down to a faint corner whisper, and set the whole headline in white with a short brand accent rule underneath so contrast is guaranteed for any brand primary. Stock strip below is now a smoother seamless make-logo marquee (pause-on-hover) that stays visible/auto-scrolling on mobile instead of hiding. Inventory page brought to huntsmotors-style modern UX: grid/list view toggle in the toolbar, on-card image carousel (hover prev/next + dot indicators + n/total count) sourced from the full gallery, 4-column grid at ≥1500px, and a horizontal media|body list layout.
  - Notes: `images: string[]` added to `InventoryVehicle` (additive, back-compat — `image` retained) and populated in `normalizeInventoryItem` from the already-collected gallery; card carousel falls back to the single image when only one photo exists. Card actions moved top-left / featured badge top-right (huntsmotors parity, avoids collision with the count + dots). `tsc --noEmit` clean; verified both grid and list views + hero + marquee via headless Chrome against a live preview brand. Token-driven throughout (no hardcoded colors).
  - Follow-up (same day): matched the huntsmotors inventory *layout*, not just the cards. Dropped the permanent 260px left filter sidebar — filters now live in a fixed slide-in drawer (backdrop + close, z-index above the sticky header) opened from a "Filters" pill button in the toolbar (with an active-filter count badge). The results grid is now full-width (1→2→3→4 cols at 600/1000/1280px) and the toolbar is a single rounded bar: `[Filters] Showing X of Y … [Grid│List labelled] [♡] [⚖] [Sort]`. Verified drawer open/closed + full-width grid via headless Chrome.
  - Follow-up (same day): the stock-strip marquee read as static. Root cause: the `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` rule set `animation: none`, and Windows "Show animations off" reports reduced-motion — so the strip was frozen for anyone with that OS setting. Since the auto-scroll is the requested signature of the band, reduced-motion now slows it to 60s rather than stopping it; default speed bumped 42s → 24s so it's an obvious marquee; and short make lists are now padded (repeat base until ≥10 per half) so a single loop half always overflows the rail and the seamless -50% loop never shows a gap. Verified the chips shift between two frames even under `--force-prefers-reduced-motion`.

- 2026-07-21: auto-wow-uk-bespoke (Rugged Auto) — vehicle detail page wired to CDN Carous widgets (enquiry + reserve), matching fbm-motors (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app/themes/auto-wow-uk-bespoke/lib/external-widgets.ts` (new), `pages/used-cars/[slug]/page.tsx`, `pages/used-cars/[slug]/VehicleDetailIsland.tsx`, `page.module.css`
  - Reason: the rugged detail page was the only one of the two using its local `EnquiryModal` and had no reserve flow — unlike fbm-motors, which loads the hosted `vehicle-enquiry` + `reserve-a-car` bundles from `widgets.carous.co.uk` and hands them a normalised vehicle summary. Ported fbm's self-contained `external-widgets.ts` helper, added the two `<Script strategy="afterInteractive">` loads to the detail page, and wired the sidebar "Make an enquiry" + new "Reserve this car" buttons (and the mobile sticky-bar Enquire) to the CDN widgets. Sidebar actions trimmed to Enquire + Reserve + Call (dropped the WhatsApp button) per request — WhatsApp still reachable via the FAB.
  - Notes: CDN-first with graceful fallback — added `isExternalEnquiryReady()` / `isExternalReserveReady()` guards so if the bundle hasn't loaded the buttons still open the theme's local `EnquiryModal` (which keeps capturing the lead), rather than fbm's hard dependency. Gallery left on the local `VehicleGallery` widget (fbm does the same). `tsc --noEmit` clean; the second `declare global Window` augmentation merges with fbm's identical one without conflict.

- 2026-07-21: auto-wow-uk-bespoke (Rugged Auto) — modern header redesign + fixed open/closed status (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app/themes/auto-wow-uk-bespoke/components/Header.tsx`, `Header.module.css`
  - Reason: header read "Live stock · Closed" during trading hours on every brand (seen on Columbus Vehicles, Winnersh). `isOpenNow` looked up `openingHours['monday']` but the record stores capitalized keys (`Monday`), so today was always `undefined` → always Closed; it also never compared the actual time. Rebuilt as `getOpenState`: normalises both the `Record<Day,string>` and cloned `[{day,hours}]` shapes case-insensitively, parses today's `HH:MM–HH:MM` range, compares against `new Date()`, and produces a live label ("Open · until 18:00" / "Closed · Opens tomorrow 09:00"). Computed post-mount + refreshed each minute to avoid hydration mismatch and flip live. Header chrome modernised: slim top bar with a rounded open/closed status pill (accent dot when open), today's real hours instead of a hardcoded "Mon-Sat 09:00–18:00", hairline bottom border that darkens on scroll (no box-shadow, per convention), pill-shaped nav hover + Call CTA, condensed header on scroll.
  - Notes: `tsc --noEmit` clean; open/closed logic unit-checked across Mon/Sat/Sun + before/after hours + no-data. Token-driven throughout (no hardcoded colors).
  - Follow-up (same day): still showed "Closed" on live preview dealers. Root cause: `openingHours` values in previews.db are frequently NOT clock times — most records store `"Appointment Only"` or empty strings (per-day lowercase keys), and other records (e.g. Chesterfield) use grouped keys like `monFri`/`monSat` or object values `{open,close,closed}` or a single `"Mon-Sat 09:00-18:00; Sun Closed"` string. The parser only understood per-day time ranges, so appointment-only records fell through to the hard "Closed" fallback. Rebuilt as `buildDayMap` — a shape-agnostic normaliser (`daysInKey` expands grouped/`weekday`/`weekend`/schema.org `Mo` keys; `parseRange` reads 24h + am/pm + object values) that only counts *parseable* clock ranges as "known". Appointment-only/empty records now render "Open · by appointment" / "Live stock available" (open state) instead of "Closed". Verified against every shape found in previews.db + the theme-dna samples.

- 2026-07-20: all themes — smooth scroll-reveal entry animations on every homepage (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope:
    - `app/widgets/AnimateOnScroll/AnimateOnScroll.tsx` — root-cause fix: the driver now RENDERS its own `<style>` (the AOS CSS inlined as a string) instead of relying on the `import './aos.css'` side-effect in index.ts, which was tree-shaken out of some theme bundles and silently killed every animation (see memory feedback_aos_css_inline). Emitting the stylesheet from the driver guarantees the CSS ships exactly where the driver mounts — no orphaned `opacity:0`, no 14 duplicated base.css copies. SSR'd with the initial hidden state so there's no flash-then-hide. This one edit revives dormant `data-aos` animations across every theme that mounts the driver.
    - Driver mounted where missing: `fbm-motors` (Shell), `classic-dealer` (ConditionalShell), `gilded-drive` (ConditionalShell). Already mounted in the other 10; `springalls-classic` keeps its own AosProvider.
    - Homepage sections tagged with `data-aos="fade-up"` on each below-hero band root across all 14 themes (hero left with its own on-load treatment). Canonical form = section-level reveal per band. Themes that already shipped finer-grained (mostly dormant) inner staggers: some were consolidated to section-level (auto-wow, auto-wow-02, axis), some kept as compound inner+section (warwick, queensbury, showroom, kain), and dual-stock was already a complete rich design (mixed variants + delays + MotionFX) so it was left as-is. classic-dealer/gilded-drive sections already animate via framer-motion `whileInView` (inline opacity outranks the 0-specificity AOS rule, so framer stays the driver and data-aos sits inert-but-harmless).
  - Reason: "entry animations for section elements, smooth and modern, on all homepages." The mechanism (IntersectionObserver + `data-aos`, one-shot, prefers-reduced-motion aware, auto-disabled ≤720px mobile) already existed but was dead in most themes due to the CSS tree-shake.
  - Notes: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean across the repo. Reveal behaviour verified in a token-accurate harness (mid-scroll capture: one section revealed, one mid-transition, rest hidden) — the inlined CSS string is correct. No color edits (respects the in-progress contract sweep). Not driven inside the full Next preview stack; worth a scroll-through on a live preview after deploy. Some richness variance across themes is intentional (they're distinct bespoke themes). Not committed.

Contrast warning chips render under the brand grid when primary/secondary/accent fall below 3:1 on the background. `ColorUtils` (ES module) exposed to the classic inline scripts via a `window.ColorUtils` module shim. Populate-from-API paths now sync all 8 colors (previously only 5) plus the auto state.
    - `static/brand-form.css` + update.html's inline style copy — swatch grid 5→4 columns, auto badge, toggle switch, warning chip styles (kept in both places to match the existing duplication).
  - Reason: the 8-picker grid confused template building; dealers/staff only choose 4 brand colors, the rest are contrast-derived. Update page starts from the record's `theme.colorsAuto` (missing flag = manual, so existing hand-tuned palettes are never silently re-derived); create page defaults auto ON for the simplified flow.
  - Notes: verified via Jinja parse + `node --check` on every inline script + 24 structural checks. Browser smoke test still pending — dashboard wasn't running locally; click through the Colors tab (toggle both ways, drag Background dark/light, check warnings) after next run or deploy. Remaining: Phase 4 (AI prompt to 4 colors) + Phase 5 (docs).

- 2026-07-20: brandstudio — palette derivation wired into all four persistence paths (Phase 2 of palette simplification) (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope:
    - `backend/services/color_derive.py` — new `resolve_colors(colors, auto=)` entry point: `auto=True` re-derives text/surface/border/muted from the 4 brand inputs (dashboard Auto mode); `auto=False` keeps explicit valid values and fills only missing/invalid ones. Legacy keys (`bgPrimary`, `accentChrome`, …) pass through untouched.
    - `static/modules/form_handler.py` `createBrandConfig` — colors now resolve through the engine; `colorsAuto` read from the payload (falling back to the record's `theme.colorsAuto`), persisted on `theme`. Import has a sys.path fallback since this module loads with `static/modules` on the path.
    - `app.py` `create_brand` + `update_brand_put` (manual fallback blocks) — resolve through the engine and persist all 8 colors + `theme.colorsAuto`. These paths previously silently DROPPED borderColor/mutedColor/surfaceColor (only wrote 5).
    - `app.py` `remote_create_preview` — incoming `theme.colors` normalised non-destructively: `/new-theme` hand-tuned palettes are kept verbatim; only missing derived tokens are computed.
  - Reason: every write path must emit the full 8-color contract themes consume, with derivation as the default source for the 4 non-brand colors. Defaulting to `auto=False` when no flag is present means existing records and skill-created previews keep their hand-designed palettes — auto mode only activates when the (Phase 3) dashboard UI explicitly sends `colorsAuto=true`.
  - Notes: py_compile clean; 13 functional checks green (auto-derive matches engine, explicit values kept, existing-record fallback preserved, legacy-key passthrough, REST None-fill) plus the import fallback tested from outside the project root. User-visible change lands with Phase 3 (dashboard shows 4 pickers + derived read-only swatches).

- 2026-07-20: brandstudio — palette derivation engine: 4 dealer-input colors, 4 auto-derived (Phase 1 of palette simplification) (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope:
    - `backend/services/color_derive.py` (new) — canonical derivation: `derive_palette(primary, secondary, accent, background)` returns the full 8-color set (text/surface/border/muted computed via WCAG contrast rules, natural roles, no inversion); `derive_missing()` fills only absent derived keys (for `/api/v1/preview/create` normalisation in Phase 2); `contrast_warnings()` flags brand colors under 3:1 on the bg without mutating them.
    - `static/modules/color-utils.js` — JS mirror (`derivePalette`, `contrastWarnings` + WCAG helpers) for live derived swatches in the dashboard. Locked to the Python engine: same constants, integer step loops, half-up rounding (Python `round()` is banker's — used `int(x+0.5)` instead) so both emit byte-identical hexes.
  - Reason: the dashboard's 8 color pickers confuse template building — dealers/staff only meaningfully choose Primary/Secondary/Accent/Background; Text/Surface/Border/Muted should be autotuned by contrast rules. The runtime 8-token contract (`--color-*`) is unchanged, so all themes keep working with zero edits. Derivation rules: text = ink `#0f1623` or white +8% primary tint stepped to ≥7:1; surface = white / bg−3% (light) or bg+7% white (dark); border = text 12% into bg; muted = text stepped 60→100% into bg until ≥4.5:1.
  - Notes: verified with a 7-fixture cross-check (white/off-white/dark-navy/black/midtone-gray bgs) — Python and JS outputs identical, contrast targets met, warning lists agree. Not yet wired into any persistence path — that's Phase 2 (`form_handler.py`, `create_brand`, `update_brand_put`, `remote_create_preview`), which also fixes those REST paths silently dropping border/muted/surface today. Plan in `tasks/todo.md`.

- 2026-06-19: `fbm-motors` — show phone exactly as entered in the dashboard, stop auto-reformatting (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope:
    - `app/themes/fbm-motors/lib/contact.ts` — `getBrandContactInfo` now returns `phoneDisplay: phoneRaw` (the verbatim, trimmed `brand.location.phone`) instead of `formatUkPhone(phoneRaw)`. Removed the now-unused `formatUkPhone` helper. `phoneTel` still digit-normalises for the `tel:` href; `digitsOnly` is retained for that.
  - Reason: the theme was passing the dashboard-entered phone through `formatUkPhone`, which strips non-digits and re-spaces it — so a dealer's `(07457) 401022` rendered as `07457 401022` (brackets dropped). Difatha wants the number shown exactly as typed in the dashboard form; the theme must not reformat the visible text. Only the click-to-call href needs normalising, which `phoneTel` already does.
  - Notes: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean (0 errors). Only the fbm-motors theme reformatted the phone among brandstudio themes (grep). Header/Footer render `{contact.phoneDisplay}` directly; Vehicle.tsx only uses the phone for the `tel:` href, not as visible text — so the helper change covers every display path. Needs a deploy to reach prod previews.

- 2026-06-19: brandstudio — fix uploaded brand assets 404ing on previews + dashboard (Next 16 public/ cache) (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope:
    - `app/api/media/[...path]/route.ts` (new) — Node-runtime route (`runtime='nodejs'`, `dynamic='force-dynamic'`) that streams files from `public/images` on every request, with path-traversal guards and content-type by extension. Serves brand-uploaded assets the instant they hit disk — no process restart.
    - `next.config.js` — converted `rewrites()` to the object form and added an `afterFiles` rewrite `'/images/:path*' -> '/api/media/:path*'`. `afterFiles` keeps the fast static path for files Next already knows about; only the cache-missed uploads fall through to the runtime route.
    - Prod hotfix (no repo change): `pm2 restart app-brandstudio` on the VPS to re-warm Next's `public/` cache so the 7 assets uploaded since the Jun-18 deploy (blacktreemotoring, autotechcars, showroomshinecars) serve again immediately.
  - Reason: Next.js 16's production server caches the `public/` directory listing when it warms. Brand Studio deliberately does NOT restart the Next process on each brand save (`PM2_RESTART_ON_BRAND_UPDATE` unset), on the old assumption that Next serves `public/` live. Under Next 16 that assumption is false — every dashboard upload after the last restart 404'd on both the dealer preview sites and the editor's asset cards (which load `/images/*` from the brand's preview origin). Verified on prod: files existed on disk (`/home/brandstudio/public/images/blacktreemotoring-hero.jpg`, mtime Jun 19) but the running Next process (0 restarts since Jun 18 15:29) returned 404 while older files in the same dir served 200.
  - Notes: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean (0 errors). Functionally verified on `next dev`: `/api/media/<file>` and `/images/<file>` both 200 with correct bytes/content-type, missing file → 404, `..%2f` traversal → 404. The durable fix needs a deploy (push to main → deploy.yml builds + restarts) to take effect on prod; until then the restart hotfix is holding. Brand-asset uploads are gitignored (`.gitignore:48 public/images/*`) so they live only on the serving box — unchanged by this fix, but worth noting they are not backed up.

- 2026-06-10: `fbm-motors` — footer bottom contrast + desktop menu button leak (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope:
    - `components/Footer.module.css`:
      - `.bottomBar` border `0.08 → 0.12`, bg `0.2 → 0.35` so the strip reads as a delineated bottom plate.
      - `.bottomInner` color `rgba(255,255,255,0.7) → 0.88`; `.bottomLink` explicit `0.88` color + hover to brand-accent-mixed-with-white (was inheriting and getting buried by the bottom-bar bg).
      - `.hours`, `.brandLead`, `.quickLinks a` all moved off `--fbm-mist` (#9AA7B8 = WCAG fail on carbon) to `rgba(255,255,255,0.78-0.85)`; `.quickLinks a:hover` now uses the brand-accent-to-white mix instead of raw `ember-400` (dark wine for Columbus).
    - `components/Header.module.css` `.menuToggle` desktop hide — was being silently overridden. The button carries both the module-hashed `.menuToggle` (specificity 0,1,0) and the global `[data-theme-id='fbm-motors'] .fbm-btn-ghost` utility (0,2,0); the utility's `display: inline-flex` beat the module's `display: none` at ≥1024px, so the "Menu" button shipped on desktop. Adding `!important` to the desktop hide brings the menu button down on screens that already show the inline nav (and the button never worked there — clicking it set `open=true` but `.mobileNav` is `display: none` on desktop). Same `:where()`/specificity class of bug logged before for `[hidden]` and cross-theme `.mobile-menu`.
  - Reason: Difatha flagged "footer bottom contrast issues" then noticed the Menu button on large screens that did nothing. Both are theme-wide bugs that repeat for every brand record.
  - Notes: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.

- 2026-06-10: `fbm-motors` — solid buttons, footer rebuild, Visit Us live map, UK phone format (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope:
    - **Buttons** — `styles/base.css` `.fbm-btn-primary` background `linear-gradient(primary → accent)` → solid `var(--color-primary)`. Hover state `transform: scale(1.03)` + box-shadow-glow → `translateY(-1px)` + `color-mix(primary 88%, #000)` (darker shade of primary, still single solid color). Per Difatha's rule: buttons are one solid colour, never mix two brand colours and never gradient. Same class is used for every primary CTA in the theme (header phone, hero search, footer subscribe, footer "Sell your car", Visit Us "Get directions"), so the one base.css change fixes all of them.
    - **Visit Us map** — `pages/home/page.tsx` now resolves a live embed src: `brand.mapEmbed → brand.location.mapEmbed → brand.googleMapsEmbed → https://www.google.com/maps?q=<address>&output=embed → ''`. When a src is available, renders an `<iframe>` with `loading="lazy"`, the brand-named title, and a floating "Get directions →" CTA overlayed bottom-right; only falls back to the "Map appears here…" placeholder when there's no address at all. Pattern lifted from `classic-dealer/pages/contact/page.tsx`.
    - `pages/home/page.module.css` — added `.visitMapFrame` (`16/7` aspect, border 0) and `.visitMapFloatingCta` (absolute bottom-right with extra drop shadow so it lifts off the map).
    - **Footer** — `components/Footer.module.css`:
      - `.columnHeading` color `var(--fbm-ember-400)` → `color-mix(... 50%, #ffffff)` (was rendering as dark wine on dark — invisible). Added a hairline `border-bottom: 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.08)` + bottom padding so each column heading reads as a labelled section.
      - `.grid` — was `repeat(3, 1fr)`; long newsletter lead + 6-item link column were both losing the layout fight. Switched to `1.3fr 0.8fr 1.3fr` with `gap: 64px`.
      - `.brandLogo` — `48px → 56px`, max-width `240 → 260`, added `filter: brightness(1.05) drop-shadow(0 2px 8px rgba(0,0,0,0.4))` so dark-on-dark vendor logos lift off the carbon surface without distorting light logos.
      - `.address` — color from `--fbm-mist` to `rgba(255,255,255,0.7)` for a stronger contrast baseline; `.addressPhone` bumped to 1rem semibold so the phone is the visual anchor of the brand column.
      - `.bottomBar` — added `background: rgba(0,0,0,0.2)` subtle inset; `.bottomInner` gets `padding-right: 96px` desktop / `padding-bottom: 96px` mobile so the bottom links never slide under the floating WhatsApp widget.
      - Section radial-gradient washes intensified (primary 12% → 18%, accent rgb-blue swap → brand accent 8%) so the footer looks intentional even when scrolled to.
    - **UK phone display** — `lib/contact.ts` added `formatUkPhone()` that turns raw `7723090172` / `07723090172` / `+44 7723 090172` into `07723 090172` / `+44 7723 090172`. Applied at the `phoneDisplay` boundary so every consumer (Header CTA, Footer, contact page) gets the formatted version automatically.
  - Reason: Difatha flagged "footer has huge issues" + "buttons should contain single color and not mix or gradient" + asked for a default live map. Footer column headings were invisible (same `ember-400 on dark` issue), brand logo barely showed, raw 10-digit phone looked like a serial number, and the bottom bar was sliding under the WhatsApp widget. All four were dealer-record-agnostic — the same fixes apply across every fbm-motors preview.
  - Notes: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Hero-accent title gradient (the "Perfect" word) intentionally kept — that's a title text effect, not a button. Section / atmosphere / overlay gradients also kept — those are bg washes, not button surfaces.

- 2026-06-10: `fbm-motors` — testimonials background image + dark-on-dark contrast + bigger header logo (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope:
    - `pages/home/page.tsx` — sourced a `testimonialsBg` image via the standard fallback chain (`brand.images.testimonials → brand.images.about → brand.heroImage → defaultShowroom`) and added `<img>` + overlay + atmosphere-grid layers inside `.testimonialsSection` (mirrors the hero stack).
    - `pages/home/page.module.css` — rebuilt `.testimonialsSection` as a layered band: `<img>` at 32% opacity / saturate(0.9), then a vertical carbon gradient that's opaque at top + bottom and 75-80% in the middle (so the band reads as dark anchor with the image as texture rather than a photo with a tint), then per-edge radial washes (primary at top-right, accent at bottom-left), then a 64px grid atmosphere on top. `isolation: isolate` + explicit `z-index` stack 0/1/2/3 to keep layers from leaking.
    - `components/Testimonials.module.css`:
      - `.card` background `0.06 → 0.09`, border `0.12 → 0.16`, blur with `saturate(1.1)` so the glass reads as glass over the photo, not as a flat dark rectangle.
      - `.quoteIcon` color `var(--fbm-ember-400)` → `color-mix(... 50%, #ffffff)` and width `22 → 28` so the speech-marks pop on dark instead of disappearing.
      - `.title` (the "Google" / platform label) `var(--fbm-ember-400)` → `color-mix(... 55%, #ffffff)` + 0.04em tracking — same dark-brand-on-dark fix as the hero.
    - `components/Header.tsx` + `Header.module.css` — header logo `<Image width=160 height=36>` → `width=240 height=56`; CSS `height: 36px → 56px`, `max-width: 200px → 280px`, plus a `≤640px` rule that scales down to 44px / 200px so mobile stays compact.
  - Reason: Columbus preview showed the testimonials band as a black rectangle with near-invisible quote marks and "Google" labels rendering as dark wine on near-black (Columbus's brand-accent is a deep red). User also wanted a background image to make the band feel like a stop, not a divider, and a bigger header logo for brand presence. Same `color-mix(brand-color → white)` pattern used elsewhere (hero accent / eyebrow) applied to the testimonial chrome so the dark-on-dark issue doesn't recur per brand record.
  - Notes: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. `(brand.images as any)?.testimonials` is the standard escape hatch for image slots not yet typed in `BrandConfig.images` — same pattern used for other per-section image fallbacks.

- 2026-06-10: `fbm-motors` — modern Partners trust band (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope:
    - `recipes/text-recipe.json` — added `partnersEyebrow` ("Trusted Partners") + `partnersLead` ("Independent checks, finance from regulated lenders and warranties from names you'll recognise.") to the `partners` section. Changed `partnersTitle` default from "Our Partners" to "Backed by the industry" so the band reads like a trust claim, not a sponsor list.
    - `pages/home/page.tsx` partners section — restructured: heading container with eyebrow + title + lead via `SectionHeading`; ticker now renders each partner as a glass pill chip with a shield+check icon (shield = trust, check = verified) instead of a bare span; partner list duplicated 3× for seamless scroll; added an `sr-only` `<ul>` mirror so screen readers get the names without parsing the animated, duplicated markup.
    - `pages/home/page.module.css` `.partners` family — full visual rewrite:
      - Section gets a radial primary tint at top + a subtle ink wash to break up the otherwise-empty band.
      - Ticker rail gets symmetric `mask-image: linear-gradient(90deg, transparent, #000 12%, #000 88%, transparent)` so chips fade in/out at the viewport edges instead of clipping hard.
      - `:hover` on the ticker pauses the animation (`animation-play-state: paused`) so users can read a chip they're interested in.
      - Animation slowed `30s → 45s` for a calmer feel.
      - Chip: white surface, 1px ink-08% border, brand-primary check icon, 6px-blur drop-shadow, pill radius, Inter 0.85rem semibold with 0.04em tracking.
      - `@media (max-width: 640px)` reduces section padding to 56px and chip padding/font-size for mobile-modest density.
  - Reason: Difatha asked for a more modern partners design. The previous strip was ghost-grey text in a row — looked like a placeholder. Per the dashboard tokens / hero refinement rules: tinted bg from brand-primary (via `color-mix`), chip surfaces are theme-locked white, primary-colored icon, no hardcoded colors that defeat brand overrides. Edge mask + hover-pause mirror the inventory rail pattern from warwick-hall's `LatestArrivals`.
  - Notes: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Partner names still come from the hardcoded `partners` array (HANDLER PROTECT / AA / AutoTrader / Zuto / Feefo Verified) — that's seed data shared with the source FBM app; not a dealer-specific value, so safe to keep static for now.

  - Scope:

    - `components/PageHero.module.css` `.lead` — dropped `max-width: 42rem`; added explicit `text-align: center` so removing the centered block cap doesn't move text alignment.

- 2026-06-10: `fbm-motors` — hero accent / eyebrow / lead readability + balance pass (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `pages/home/page.module.css`
    - `.heroAccent` gradient — was `var(--fbm-ember-500) → var(--fbm-ember-400)` (brand primary → accent). For Columbus's dark-wine primary on the dark hero this rendered as near-black-on-black; the "Perfect" word read as a hole, not a climax. Now both gradient stops `color-mix` toward `#ffffff` (55% / 45%) so the accent stays bright regardless of how dark the brand primary is, while still picking up the brand hue when one is set.
    - `.heroEyebrow` — same problem; resolved color was a low-contrast wine on dark. Mixed toward `#ffffff` (55%) for the same reason.
    - `.heroLead` — was `max-width: 36rem` with implicit `text-align: center` inherited from `.heroInner`. The short Columbus tagline ("We're the quality used 4×4 specialists.") fit comfortably inside the 36rem cap, but with the title above spanning ~1000px wide the lead's narrow centered column read as left-indented relative to the title's edges. Widened to `52rem`, added explicit `text-align: center` + `text-wrap: pretty`, and lifted the body color from the mist token to `rgba(255,255,255,0.78)` for better dark-hero contrast.
  - Reason: First Columbus preview showed the eyebrow + accent word both reading as dark red on dark — the gradient looked unintentional, not branded — and the centered lead visually anchored left of the title. None of this would self-correct for any brand record with a dark primary; same fix applies across the theme.
  - Notes: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. No JSX changes. The `{cityish}` default for `heroEyebrow` ("WINNERSH" alone) is a separate copy-content question — the recipe still surfaces it; the dashboard's Component Text tab lets dealers override per record.

- 2026-06-10: `fbm-motors` — wire real `/api/inventory` stock into home + used-cars (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope:
    - `lib/home-data.server.ts` — new server helper that posts `/inventory` with the brand slug from request headers + `light=1, per_page=12, sort=price_desc, vehicle_type=car, stock_status=in_stock` and returns `{ featured: InventoryVehicle[], makes: { name, count }[] }`. Mirrors the `buy4lessuk-bespoke/lib/home-data.server.ts` shape.
    - `pages/home/page.tsx` — converted `FbmHomePage` to an async server component that awaits `loadHomeData()` and renders the Current Stock strip + Browse-by-Make grid from real data. Seed `cars` + seed `makes` survive as the empty-state fallback so dev/preview without an API origin still demonstrates the layout.
    - `pages/used-cars/page.tsx` — converted to async, accepts the runtime's `initialInventory` (raw items already pre-fetched by `page-runtime.server.tsx` for `pageId: usedCars`), normalizes once, and only falls back to a self-issued `/inventory` fetch when invoked directly without that prop. Passes `initialVehicles` + `initialMeta` (with `available.makes / fuel_types`) into the client.
    - `pages/used-cars/UsedCarsClient.tsx` — refactored from `Car[]` seed-only filtering to `InventoryVehicle[]` from props, with make + fuel filter options sourced from `initialMeta.available.*` when present (falls back to deriving unique values from the visible vehicles, then to the static `FUELS`/`GEARBOXES` lists). Gearbox filter compares against `vehicle.transmission`, not the old `Car.gearbox`.
  - Reason: Difatha noticed the first Columbus preview was rendering the seed inventory instead of the dealer's real stock. Pattern lifted from `queensbury-cars-bespoke` and `buy4lessuk-bespoke` (server-side `/api/inventory` fetch using `apiUrl` + `getBrandSlugFromRequest`, normalize through `normalizeInventoryItem`, pass `initialVehicles` + `initialMeta` into the client component). Home rail follows the lighter `loadHomeData()` pattern from buy4lessuk; used-cars follows the queensbury searchParams-aware server fetch.
  - Notes: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Empty-state behaviour: when both the runtime's `initialInventory` and the page-level fallback fetch come back empty, the client component renders `cars.map(carToVehicle)` so the page never renders blank — only swaps to "No cars match those filters" if the user filters the seed list to zero results.

- 2026-06-10: `fbm-motors` — runtime + contrast fix pass after first preview (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope:
    - `lib/cars.ts` — added `carToVehicle(car: Car): InventoryVehicle` adapter so the seed list can flow through `CarCard`'s new `vehicle:` contract.
    - `pages/home/page.tsx`, `pages/used-cars/UsedCarsClient.tsx` — switched both seed-list call sites from `<CarCard car={car} />` to `<CarCard vehicle={carToVehicle(car)} />`. Previously `CarCard` was refactored to accept `InventoryVehicle` but the consumers were still passing the legacy `Car` shape under the wrong prop name, so `vehicle` was undefined and the cards would throw on first render.
    - `context/BrandStyles.tsx` — replaced the hardcoded `@import url(Inter + Space Grotesk)` with `buildGoogleFontsImport(fontUi, fontBrand)` so dashboard-driven font swaps actually load (matches the `feedback_brandstyles_must_load_google_fonts` memory).
    - `pages/home/page.module.css` `.partnerName` — bumped from `color-mix(ink-500 50%, transparent)` (already 22% effective opacity, near-invisible on light bg) to `color-mix(ink-900 65%, transparent)` so the partners ticker actually reads as words instead of ghost text.
    - `components/Testimonials.module.css` — dropped the inactive-card `opacity: 0.6` dimming (both cards looked broken when only one was "active"), brightened the quote body from `--fbm-mist` (#9AA7B8) italic 14px to a `rgba(255,255,255,0.88)` upright 15.2px, and softened the active-card emphasis to a background lift + border accent instead of a hard opacity switch.
    - `components/CarCard.module.css` `.enquire` — removed the `opacity: 0` until `:hover` rule. The "View details →" affordance now ships visible by default (mobile + scannability) with a small translate-X on hover instead.
  - Reason: First Columbus Vehicles preview surfaced (a) cards that would have thrown on `vehicle.title` had the seed not already been served via CarCard's old `Car` contract pre-refactor, (b) a ghost-grey partners strip, (c) testimonials so dim the inactive one looked unrendered, and (d) the standing "BrandStyles must load brand fonts" rule being violated. All four are theme-wide issues that would have repeated for every fbm-motors preview, not one-off Columbus polish.
  - Notes: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. No JSX changes to CarCard, Testimonials, or Header — only adapter wiring at consumer sites + CSS contrast bumps. Seed inventory still flows through the same path as live `/api/inventory` data via `normalizeInventoryItem`, so production previews with real feed data already pass `InventoryVehicle` and benefit from the same component without going through the adapter.

- 2026-06-10: `fbm-motors` — new theme ported from the standalone `F:\projects\firstbuy-motors` Next 14 app (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: new `app/themes/fbm-motors/` theme covering all 14 contract pages plus the FAQ accordion (embedded on Finance per the contract's lack of a `faqs` page id). Plumbing: `theme.json`, `tokens.ts`, `shell.tsx`, `pages.ts`, `recipes/index.ts`, `sections/index.tsx`, full `context/` bundle (BrandClientWrapper, BrandStyles, AuthContext, DynamicFavicon, GarageContext), full `lib/` bundle (api, contact, uk-phone, brand-slug.server, vehicle-links, seo, inventory) and a dedicated `lib/brand-text.ts` + `recipes/text-recipe.json` covering 14 sections of editable copy (topbar, hero, stock strip, forecourt, why-us, partners, testimonials, browse-by-make, visit, footer, about / contact / sell / finance / faqs / used-cars page heroes). Visual layer: bespoke `styles/base.css` scoped to `[data-theme-id='fbm-motors']` rebuilding the source app's design vocabulary (.fbm-card / .fbm-eyebrow / .fbm-btn-primary / .fbm-btn-ghost(-dark) / .fbm-field / .fbm-section-dark / .fbm-glass-dark / .fbm-grid-atmosphere + ember/carbon/ink/mist tokens) as theme-scoped CSS so the JSX retains the source's component vocabulary without leakage. Components: Header (sticky top utility bar + brand wordmark + Pages dropdown + mobile menu), Footer (3-column dark anchor with newsletter), Shell (mounts CookieBanner / PreviewBanner / CarousWhatsAppWidget), CarCard, SectionHeading, PageHero, HeroSearch, Testimonials. Pages: home, used-cars (filters in `UsedCarsClient`), about, contact (form in `ContactFormClient`), sell-your-car (3-step stepper in `SellFormClient`), finance (loan calculator + FAQ accordion as `LoanCalculatorClient` + `FaqAccordionClient`), used-cars/[slug] (vehicle detail with gallery picker in `VehicleGalleryClient`), recently-sold, compare (`CompareClient`), wishlist (`WishlistClient`), services, part-exchange, privacy-policy, cookie-policy. Theme registered in `theme/theme-manifest.json` as status: experimental; `npm run theme:sync` regenerates all four registries (page / shell / contract / context); `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `node tools/audit-theme.mjs --id fbm-motors` reports OK (0 blockers, 33 advisories — mostly perf-raw-img on Unsplash seed photos + motion-aos polish).
  - Reason: Difatha asked to convert the standalone First Buy Motors Next 14 source app into a brandstudio theme verbatim ("don't change any UI, just wire up tokens, recipes etc to have a fully configurable theme like the rest of the themes"). The source was a Tailwind 3.4 app outside carous-platform so `clone-app-to-theme.mjs` didn't apply; instead the visual layer was ported component-by-component with Tailwind utilities translated to theme-scoped CSS that consumes the dashboard's 8 brand tokens, and every page interactivity block was extracted into co-located `*Client.tsx` islands to satisfy the `tp-use-client-on-page` Turbopack constraint.
  - Notes: theme id is `fbm-motors` (kebab-case with two segments). Brand colors flow through both `--color-*` (natural roles) and FBM-specific `--fbm-ember-50/100/400/500/600` + `--fbm-carbon-700/800/900/950` token ramps derived from `--color-primary` and `--color-text` via `color-mix()`, so dashboard primary/text swaps fully recolour the dual-tier light + dark surfaces. Header brand wordmark splits brand.name into ink-coloured + ember-coloured tokens when no `brand.logo` is set. FAQ accordion lives on `/finance` because `ThemePageId` has no `faqs` entry — Header dropdown's "FAQs" link points there. Header / Footer / part-exchange use the canonical `/sell-my-car` route per the `std-unrouted-href` advisory.

- 2026-06-10: `classic-dealer` — ACTUAL ROOT CAUSE of the huge-whitespace-below-header bug found + framer-motion reverted (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope:
    - `app/themes/cnhcars-clone/styles/header.css:721-734` ships an UNSCOPED `.mobile-menu` rule (`position: relative; height: 100%`). Because Next.js bundles every theme's CSS into a single file, that rule leaks into the classic-dealer document and competes with classic-dealer's own `.mobile-menu` declaration.
    - classic-dealer's mobile-menu was wrapped in `:where(body[data-theme-id="classic-dealer"])` which has 0 specificity — so the cnhcars-clone unscoped rule (specificity 0,0,1,0) tied or beat classic-dealer's rule (also 0,0,1,0 because of `:where()`), and CSS source order let cnhcars-clone win. Result: the `<aside class="mobile-menu">` in the Header tree rendered as `position: relative; height: 100vh` — a giant in-flow 100vh empty block between the sticky header and the actual page content. **That** was the white block under the header all along.
    - Fix: bulk-replaced every `:where(body[data-theme-id="classic-dealer"])` selector in `app/themes/classic-dealer/styles/header.css` with the plain `body[data-theme-id="classic-dealer"]` form. Now the classic-dealer mobile-menu rule is specificity (0,0,1,1) and beats the leaked cnhcars-clone rule unambiguously.
    - Reverted framer-motion in all 13 components I had stripped it from (`Hero.tsx`, `BrandsPartners.tsx`, `CTAHero.tsx`, `ServiceMain.tsx`, `Services.tsx`, `CTAQuestion.tsx`, `Featured-redesign.tsx`, `Testimonials.tsx`, `ReviewsStrip.tsx`, `PreviewBanner.tsx`, `CookieBanner.tsx`, `RecentlySold.tsx`, `RecentlySoldHero.tsx`). Animation wrappers and `whileInView` / `AnimatePresence` props restored to their original shape.
    - Reverted the literal-#0f1729 + Unsplash URL fallback I'd put in `.hero-section` while diagnosing — back to `var(--brand-text)` + `var(--classic-hero-image)`.
    - Reverted the `hero-fade-in` CSS keyframe block in `hero.css` (no longer used now that framer-motion is back).
    - Reverted the defensive `opacity: 1 !important` visibility lock in `base.css` (no longer needed).
  - Reason: After many rounds of chasing this as a framer-motion bug, Difatha pinpointed the actual culprit by inspection: `.mobile-menu` was the in-flow 100vh block. Cross-theme CSS specificity collision, nothing to do with motion at all.
  - Notes: cnhcars-clone's `.mobile-menu` rule is still unscoped — fixing it there would be the proper architectural fix (scope every cnhcars-clone rule to `body[data-theme-id="cnhcars-clone"]`) but it's out of scope for this round; classic-dealer is now armoured against the leak. Compile clean. **If a future bespoke theme reproduces this whitespace bug, look at cross-theme CSS specificity FIRST.**

- 2026-06-10: `classic-dealer` — framer-motion removed from THE ENTIRE THEME (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: removed `from "framer-motion"` import + every `motion.*` / `AnimatePresence` wrapper from the remaining four files the previous pass didn't touch: `PreviewBanner.tsx`, `CookieBanner.tsx`, `RecentlySold.tsx`, `RecentlySoldHero.tsx`.
    - `PreviewBanner` was `motion.div` with `initial: { y: -100, opacity: 0 }` — guaranteed to render off-screen + invisible if framer-motion didn't run.
    - `CookieBanner` had four `motion.*` nodes plus `AnimatePresence` wrappers; replaced with plain `<div>` / `<aside>` and an `is-open` class hook on the panel so CSS can drive the slide-in transition instead.
    - `RecentlySold` had nested `motion.section` → `motion.div` (staggerChildren) → `motion.article` (variants with `hidden: { opacity: 0 }`) — three layers of opacity-0 that all had to be removed for the grid to render.
    - `RecentlySoldHero` had outer `motion.section` + inner `motion.div` with the same initial-opacity-0 pattern.
  - Reason: After the previous round Difatha still saw the huge whitespace on the Columbus Vehicles preview. Whichever framer-motion node was the holdout, it was easier to delete the whole library from this theme than to keep guessing. Classic-dealer now has zero `framer-motion` imports anywhere — `grep` confirms.
  - Notes: Compile clean. No CSS animation replacements added for the now-removed entrance animations on PreviewBanner / CookieBanner / RecentlySold — those weren't load-bearing. Hero keeps its CSS-keyframe entrance fade from the previous round.

- 2026-06-10: `classic-dealer` — framer-motion removed from all 9 home-page components (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope:
    - Removed every `motion.section` / `motion.div` / `motion.h1` / `motion.p` / `motion.button` wrapper and the `framer-motion` import from: `Hero.tsx`, `BrandsPartners.tsx`, `CTAHero.tsx`, `ServiceMain.tsx`, `Services.tsx`, `CTAQuestion.tsx`, `Featured-redesign.tsx` (both loading + populated branches), `Testimonials.tsx`, `ReviewsStrip.tsx`. Every section now ships as plain `<section>` / `<div>` / `<h1>` / `<p>` / `<button>` with no animation props.
    - `Hero.tsx` keeps the staggered entry effect via a CSS-only `heroFadeIn` keyframe in `styles/hero.css` driven by inline `style={{ animationDelay: '0.2s' | '0.4s' | '0.6s' }}` on the title / description / search-form. CSS animations can't be stranded by JS hydration failures — they run from the moment the element is parsed.
    - Reduced-motion guard added on `.hero-fade-in` so users with `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` see content at `opacity: 1` immediately, no animation.
  - Reason: Earlier this round I switched `whileInView` → `animate` thinking the IntersectionObserver was the only failure mode. That wasn't enough — even with `animate`, framer-motion still owns the element's `initial: opacity: 0` state during SSR + hydration, and inside the brandstudio preview iframe something in that chain was leaving the element at 0 indefinitely (the user reported the whitespace was still there). The only correctness guarantee is to not use framer-motion for these sections at all. Plain HTML renders deterministically; CSS animations are declarative, not JS-driven.
  - Notes: All inner `Services.tsx` stat-card variants + the Hero's `motion.button` hover-scale also stripped. Compile clean. Inner animations on hero text are CSS keyframes only — if framer-motion is reintroduced for any future section, do NOT put `opacity: 0` in its `initial` without a fallback.

- 2026-06-10: `classic-dealer` — home-page sections stranded at `opacity:0` by framer-motion `whileInView` not firing (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope:
    - `app/themes/classic-dealer/components/Hero.tsx` — outer `motion.div` (which carried `initial: opacity: 0` + `whileInView`) replaced with a plain `<div>`. The container is now always opaque on first paint so the bg-color (`--brand-text` dark fallback) and bg-image (`--classic-hero-image`) are visible regardless of whether the IntersectionObserver fires. Inner title / description / search-form motion still animate, but switched from `whileInView` to `animate` so they trigger on mount (not on scroll-into-view).
    - Same `whileInView → animate` swap on every outer wrapper across the home-page sections: `BrandsPartners.tsx`, `CTAHero.tsx`, `ServiceMain.tsx`, `Services.tsx`, `CTAQuestion.tsx`, `Featured-redesign.tsx` (both the loading and the populated states), `Testimonials.tsx`, `ReviewsStrip.tsx`. Same root cause — every one would otherwise render as a transparent block until the IntersectionObserver fires.
    - `app/themes/classic-dealer/components/Header.tsx` — Home item re-added to `LINKS` and `ICONS` (Difatha explicitly asked for it, overriding the general lean-nav rule for this theme).
  - Reason: Difatha kept seeing a huge white gap below the header on the Columbus Vehicles preview. The contact bar, nav row, and cookie banner all rendered fine — but the entire hero section + every section below it was transparent. Traced to `motion.section initial: opacity: 0 + whileInView: opacity: 1` — `whileInView` uses an IntersectionObserver against the document viewport, and inside the brandstudio preview iframe (the parent owns scroll, the iframe doesn't), it doesn't fire reliably for elements that are in the initial viewport at hydration. The sections were in the DOM, just invisible.
  - Notes: `animate` triggers on mount unconditionally — sections below the fold do their animation "off-screen" but content is then visible by the time the user scrolls there. This is the correct fix for preview-iframe contexts; reverting to `whileInView` would re-introduce the same bug. Build compiles clean on Next 16 / Turbopack.

- 2026-06-10: `classic-dealer` — header chrome refinements after wholesale rewrite (wishlist + call CTA + nav-link colour locks) (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app/themes/classic-dealer/styles/header.css`
    - `.btn-call-us` and `.nav-links a` raised from `:where(body[data-theme-id])` (0-specificity scope) to plain `body[data-theme-id]` selectors with `color: #ffffff !important` (CTA) / `color: var(--brand-text) !important` (nav). Earlier rounds of patching across `globals.css` and `font-standardization.css` left a long tail of catch-all button rules — locking colour with `!important` short-circuits the whole specificity chase and survives any future cascade tweak.
    - `.wishlist-btn` redesigned to match the CTA's visual weight (46×46 pill, 1.5px brand-primary border at 35% alpha, brand-primary heart icon, white surface) so the action cluster reads as a coherent pair. Fills to solid brand-primary on hover/focus with a soft drop-shadow. Count badge enlarged (20×20, 11px) with shadow tracking the primary tint.
    - Heart SVG now explicitly drives `stroke` + `fill` from the button state — fixes the "out of place" look where the default Lucide stroke was inheriting `currentColor` against an under-tinted shell.
  - `app/themes/classic-dealer/components/Header.tsx` — scroll threshold raised 24 → 96 px, and the `onScroll()` initial-mount call removed. Preview iframes were restoring a non-zero `scrollTop` before mount on first paint, which collapsed the utility strip immediately and made Difatha think the contact bar wasn't rendering at all. Now the bar is always visible on first paint; it only collapses after the user scrolls past 96 px.
  - Reason: After the full overhaul Difatha reported (a) the Book Appointment text still rendered as dark on red across multiple cache states, (b) the wishlist heart icon "looked out" — too faint next to the bold CTA pill. The first was specificity / cache; the second was a visual-weight imbalance in the action cluster.
  - Notes: CSS bundle re-verified — `body[data-theme-id="classic-dealer"] .btn-call-us` ships with `color: #fff !important` after Turbopack compile. If the user still sees dark text on red, it's a stale CDN/browser cache, not a missing rule.

- 2026-06-10: `classic-dealer` — full header overhaul: modern sticky-on-scroll redesign (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope:
    - `app/themes/classic-dealer/styles/header.css` — full rewrite, 1157 → ~470 lines. Discarded the legacy advanced-search modal CSS (no JSX consumer), the per-button colour overrides, and the dual-context contrast attempts. New design:
      - Sticky header (`position: sticky; top: 0`) with a `data-scrolled` attribute toggled at >24px scroll. When scrolled, the utility strip collapses (max-height + opacity → 0), the nav row tightens its padding, the logo shrinks from 64 → 48 px, and the nav background switches to a `backdrop-filter` glass surface.
      - Token discipline: declared local `--hdr-*` aliases at the header root so every surface pairs bg+fg explicitly. The dark utility strip pairs `--brand-text` (bg) + `--brand-background` (fg); the light nav pairs `--brand-background` + `--brand-text`; the CTA pairs `--brand-primary` + `var(--btn-primary-fg, #fff)`. No more reading `--bg-elevated` alone (the bug that broke Columbus Vehicles' contact bar).
      - Nav-link active state: scale-x underline animation via `::after`, picks up both `.active` class and `aria-current="page"`. Per memory rule about active nav patterns.
      - Live-status dot on the opening-hours pill (small green pulse next to "Mon-Sat …") — modern dealer-site signal that you're open now.
      - Social icons on the utility strip stay always-visible per the standing rule (no muted/disabled state when a brand record omits a URL — render as a non-link span at full opacity).
      - Mobile menu refreshed: rounded-pill close button, active-route left-rail accent, `100dvh` height to handle iOS toolbar correctly, full-bleed at ≤480px.
      - Reduced-motion guard at the bottom kills every transition for users with `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`.
    - `app/themes/classic-dealer/components/Header.tsx` — leaned nav per Difatha's standing rule (dropped HOME and ABOUT US; Home is satisfied by the clickable brand wordmark/logo, About goes to Footer Quick Links). New behaviour:
      - Scroll detection via passive listener; sets `data-scrolled` on `<header>`.
      - Logo with 3-tier fallback (per the brand-logo-plumbing rule): `brand.logo` image → on `onError` switches to a `<span class="logo-wordmark">` rendering `brand.name`.
      - `aria-current="page"` written on the active link in addition to the legacy `.active` class.
      - Sell-Your-Car link path corrected from `/sell-your-car/` to `/sell-my-car/` per the canonical-routes memory rule.
      - Phone number now displays in the utility strip (instead of just a "Book Appointment" label) so the dealer's actual contact line is one click away — modern UX expectation.
  - Reason: Difatha asked for a full header overhaul — "new design and modern" — after multiple targeted patches still surfaced contrast/layout problems on the Columbus Vehicles preview. Going wholesale rather than patching also let me retire ~700 lines of dead/conflicting CSS (the search modal that has no consumer, the global hover override at the bottom, and a duplicated `.nav-links a` ruleset that fought itself for specificity).
  - Notes: Build compiles clean on Next 16 / Turbopack. Couldn't visually verify Columbus's preview from this machine (the brand API is unreachable here, so the route 404s without a brand record). Please reload your preview — sticky-on-scroll, lean nav, properly-contrasted bars, and active-route underline should all be visible.

- 2026-06-10: `classic-dealer` — hero white-gap + Book Appointment button text fixes (Columbus Vehicles preview) (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope:
    - `app/themes/classic-dealer/styles/hero.css` — `.hero-section` `background-image` was multi-layer (`var(--classic-hero-image, none), linear-gradient(…)`). The `none` keyword is illegal inside a multi-layer `background-image`, so the WHOLE declaration was discarded by browsers → hero rendered as transparent → body white showed through under the header. Reduced to a single layer `background-image: var(--classic-hero-image)` with `background-color: var(--brand-text)` as the fallback floor; `.hero-overlay` re-tinted via `rgba(var(--brand-text-rgb), …)` so the overlay tracks the brand's dark token rather than the previous near-black literal.
    - `app/themes/classic-dealer/styles/header.css` — `.btn-call-us` was using `color: var(--brand-background)` for the text on a `--brand-primary` (red) bg. Brands whose `--brand-background` is a custom cream/off-white (or any tint that isn't pure white) rendered as dark text on red. Locked the call-button fg to `var(--btn-primary-fg, #ffffff)` with `:hover/:focus/:active` reinforcement and an SVG stroke override so the phone icon also stays white.
  - Reason: Difatha shared a Columbus Vehicles preview screenshot with (1) a huge white block where the hero should be (multi-layer bg parse failure traced via the `none` keyword), (2) a dark-coloured "Book Appointment" CTA whose text and icon read as dark-red on the red pill. Both bugs were dormant on AUTOWOW UK LTD (their bg-image and brand-background resolved to working values) but reproducible on Columbus's palette.
  - Notes: The new `--btn-primary-fg` token gives the dashboard an explicit override hook for brands whose primary is a pale colour (where white-on-X would fail) — defaults to `#ffffff` if unset.

- 2026-06-10: `classic-dealer` — contact bar dark-on-dark contrast fix (Columbus Vehicles / Loddon Car Deals instance) (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app/themes/classic-dealer/styles/header.css` — `.contact-bar`, `.contact-item`, `.contact-right .hours`, `.social-link` now pair `var(--brand-text)` as bg with `var(--brand-background)` as fg (instead of the unstable `--bg-elevated` bg + `--brand-text` fg). Social link tints flipped to `rgba(var(--brand-background-rgb), …)` so chips stay visible on the dark bar.
  - Reason: Columbus Vehicles' brand record explicitly themes `--bg-elevated` to a dark navy. The old CSS used `--bg-elevated` for the bar bg but `--brand-text` (also dark) for the bar text → guaranteed dark-on-dark, contact details and socials read as nearly invisible. By locking the bar to the brand text↔background pair, every brand gets a consistent dark accent strip with light foreground, regardless of how they override the elevated-surface token.
  - Notes: This is the second round of classic-dealer fixes today; first round (hero / Why-Buy / placeholder filter) was based on the AUTOWOW UK LTD preview, but didn't catch this dark-mode-elevated contrast bug because AUTOWOW's `--bg-elevated` defaults to white. Dashboard tokens still drive surfaces — we just stopped reading `--bg-elevated` without a paired fg.

- 2026-06-10: `classic-dealer` — home-page audit & fix pass (hero / Why-Buy / inherited body text / placeholder seed-data) (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope:
    - `app/themes/classic-dealer/styles/hero.css` — dropped `background-attachment: fixed` + `height: 100vh` (was producing a huge white gap above the hero in preview screenshots and on iOS where the bg-fixed pin clamps to viewport, not section); `min-height` now clamps between 560–760px on desktop, 480–620 on tablet, 420–560 on mobile; vertical padding tightened; added layered fallback (`var(--classic-hero-image, none)` over a brand-text→primary gradient) so a missing/404 hero URL still shows a sensible dark band.
    - `app/themes/classic-dealer/context/BrandStyles.tsx` — hero-image fallback chain replaced (`brand.heroImage || brand.images?.hero || Unsplash cars photo`); was previously falling through to `/favicon.svg`, which scales a tiny icon across a 100vh hero.
    - `app/themes/classic-dealer/styles/services-new.css` — "Why Buy" section had stat cards with `background` and `border` both set to `--brand-background` (same as section bg → invisible cards); section now sits on a `color-mix` tint, cards get a faint border + radius + hover lift; section photo flipped from `object-fit: contain` (letterboxed in screenshot) to `cover` + shadow.
    - `app/themes/classic-dealer/styles/base.css` — body literal `color: #f3f3f3` replaced with `var(--brand-text)` (was making every untokened text element near-invisible on light brands); `background: var(--bg)` got a `var(--brand-background)` fallback (`--bg` is only defined under `.light-theme`).
    - `app/themes/classic-dealer/components/Testimonials.tsx` — added placeholder filter (`isPlaceholderTestimonial`) that drops entries authored by "John Doe"/"Jane Doe"/"Test User"/"Customer N"/etc, entries mentioning "Reliable Autos Ltd" / lorem-ipsum, and entries name-dropping a *different* dealer name than `brand.name`. Falls back to `FALLBACK_TESTIMONIALS` when everything filters out.
    - `app/themes/classic-dealer/components/Featured-redesign.tsx` — `buildFeaturedGalleryImages` now filters URLs matching `/images/placeholder.png`, `no-image*`, `image-not-available*` and `/placeholder.*` patterns before considering the gallery non-empty, so vehicles whose API record carries the `/images/placeholder.png` stub now fall through to the Unsplash placeholder instead of showing the "Image not available" graphic.
  - Reason: Difatha shared a preview screenshot of the AUTOWOW UK LTD instance on the classic-dealer theme — visible bugs were (a) a tall white gap between header and hero, (b) "Why Buy From AUTOWOW" stat cards rendering as empty rounded boxes, (c) seeded placeholder testimonial leaking a foreign dealer name ("RELIABLE AUTOS LTD" / "JOHN DOE"), (d) Latest Arrivals cards showing the API's "image not available" placeholder graphic for 2 of 4 cards.
  - Notes: localhost dev server compiles clean. Couldn't visually verify because `api.carous.co.uk` isn't reachable from this machine (the layout 404s without a brand record), so changes are CSS/logic-level fixes against the screenshot evidence + the existing code. The brand record itself still has the placeholder testimonial saved — that's a dashboard data fix, but the theme will now hide it.

- 2026-06-10: `auto-wow-uk-bespoke-02` (Columbus Vehicles) — vehicle detail wired to `widgets.carous.co.uk` CDN gallery + enquiry (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app/themes/auto-wow-uk-bespoke-02/lib/external-widgets.ts` (new, ported from cnhcars-clone) + `pages/used-cars/[slug]/page.tsx` + `page.module.css`. Replaced the in-repo `@/app/widgets/VehicleGallery` component with a `<div id="vehicle-gallery-mount" />` reserved slot + `useExternalVehicleGallery(...)` hook that polls for the CDN bundle (`widgets.carous.co.uk/widgets/vehicle-gallery/latest/vehicle-gallery.js`) and calls its `mount()` + `update()` whenever `images` / `vehicleTitle` / `loading` change. Replaced the local `EnquiryModal` + `useEnquiryModal()` hook with a Next `<Script>` tag for `widgets.carous.co.uk/widgets/vehicle-enquiry/latest/vehicle-enquiry.js` (carries `data-brand-name`, `data-dealer-name`, `data-lead-owner`, `data-default-intent`, `data-phone-tel`/`-display`, `data-whatsapp-url`, `data-email` attributes) + `openExternalVehicleEnquiry(summary)` calls from the `Email Enquiry` button and the mobile sticky-bar `Enquire` button. Each summary is built from the in-page `vehicle` record (`title`, `registration`, `make`/`model`/`derivative`, `year`, `price`/`priceText`, `mileage`, `transmission`, `fuel`, `engineSize`, `image`, `url`). Added a `.galleryMount` CSS rule that reserves `clamp(280px, 42vw, 520px)` height so the layout doesn't snap when the CDN bundle paints. Dropped `<EnquiryModal>` portal + `VehicleGallery` import.
  - Reason: Difatha asked twice — once for the gallery to be "wired correctly" and again clarifying "i doubt we are using gallery widget from widgets.carous.co.uk and also enquiry widget". They were right: the page was using the local `app/widgets/VehicleGallery` React component and the local `EnquiryModal`, not the CDN-hosted production widgets that the warwick + cnhcars themes load. Wired both to the canonical Carous widget bundle so the dealer's lead capture + gallery analytics run through the same pipeline as the other live themes.
  - Notes: tsc clean. `external-widgets.ts` mirrors the cnhcars-clone helper exactly but lives inside the theme (per the self-contained-theme convention). `useExternalVehicleGallery` polls every 100ms until `window.CarousVehicleGallery` exists, applies `mount()` once, then re-runs `update()` on dep changes; tears down `unmount()` on unmount. `data-lead-endpoint` and `data-lead-owner` ship empty for now — wiring those to a brand-specific leads endpoint is the next step when the dashboard exposes them. CDN URL falls back to `https://widgets.carous.co.uk` if `NEXT_PUBLIC_WIDGETS_BASE_URL` isn't set.

- 2026-06-10: `auto-wow-uk-bespoke-02` (Columbus Vehicles) — vehicle detail gallery feed fix (walk all payload paths) (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app/themes/auto-wow-uk-bespoke-02/pages/used-cars/[slug]/page.tsx`. Replaced the single-path `payload.gallery` → `payload.images` extractor in `parseVehicle()` with a dedupe-aware walker over 12 candidate paths: `payload`, `payload.vehicle`, `payload.advert` × `gallery`, `images`, `media`, `photos`. Each candidate runs through a new `collectImages()` helper that accepts `string` or `{url|href|src|image}` shapes (mirrors the listing-page `lib/inventory.ts` collector). A `Set<string>` keeps URLs unique while preserving first-seen order.
  - Reason: Difatha reported "Photos coming soon" rendering even when the feed returned imagery — the VehicleGallery widget was wired correctly but receiving an empty `images[]` because the parser only checked `payload.gallery` and `payload.images`. Real API payloads land photos on `payload.vehicle.gallery`, `payload.advert.gallery`, or under `media`/`photos`. See `feedback_vehicle_detail_gallery_extraction.md`: "single-path extractors miss imageful vehicles and render 'Photos coming soon'".
  - Notes: tsc clean. Object shape coverage matches `app/themes/auto-wow-uk-bespoke/lib/inventory.ts` `collectImages` plus the additional `image` key that some feeds use for the cover thumbnail. Order is deterministic — the first gallery list found drives `images[0]` (used as the hero shot in similar-vehicle save and the share OG fallback).

New structure: (1) **dark hero band** with centred title + `mileage · fuel · transmission` meta line + pill `Looking to sell?` link; (2) **light breadcrumb bar** — `Home › Used Cars › <title>` with brand-primary links and a row of circular icon buttons (Share / Heart / Compare / Print); (3) **gallery row** — `1.6fr / 1fr` grid: large 16:11 main image + 2×2 thumbnail mosaic, last thumb carries a `Gallery N` overlay badge when the total exceeds 5; (4) **2-col body** at `≥980px` (`1fr / 340px`): left column stacks Vehicle Overview / Key Specification (8-cell grid: Mileage / Registration / Engine / Transmission / Fuel / Body Type / Doors / Colour) / Highlights (2-column checklist of `vehicle.features`) / Preparation & History (2×2 grid of trust statements with ShieldCheck glyph). Highlights split across two columns based on `Math.ceil(features.length / 2)`. Print stylesheet hides chrome (hero band / breadcrumb / sticky bar / similar) so users can save a clean spec sheet. Sidebar is `position: sticky` at `top: 100px` desktop; collapses to inline at `≤720px` and the mobile sticky-bar appears.

- 2026-06-10: `auto-wow-uk-bespoke-02` (Columbus Vehicles) — `/used-cars` hero/filters/cards density pass (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app/themes/auto-wow-uk-bespoke-02/pages/used-cars/UsedCarsClient.tsx` + `page.module.css`. Pulled `<HeroBackdrop />` out of the inventory hero (was painting the full band in `--color-primary` via the brand-gradient SVG so the title rendered red-on-red on Columbus Vehicles' brand record). Hero band rebuilt as a slim page-header strip — direct dashboard-token surface (`--color-bg` / `--color-text` / `--color-border`) so the brand can never invert it, padding crushed to `20px`, title clamped to `1.2–1.6rem`, lead to `0.86rem`, results-count chip shrunk to a single-line pill (`1.2rem` numeral + `0.74rem` label). Toolbar: pill padding `10px`, search input `38px` min-height, chips `36px` min-height with `6/12px` padding (was `42px` / `9/14px`). Sidebar filters: `14px` padding / `10px` radius / no shadow / sticky offset cut from `140px` to `100px`; labels dropped to `0.66rem` 700-weight, inputs/selects to `34px` min-height / `0.82rem` text. Cards: `14px` radius → `10px`, body padding `16/18` → `10/12`, title font `1.02rem` → `0.92rem` and `text-transform: uppercase` removed (the brutalist global heading rule was uppercasing every card), subtitle `0.82rem` → `0.72rem`, spec grid flipped from 2-column to a flex-wrap row at `0.7rem` so the four icon+label pairs sit on one or two lines instead of four, price size `1.45rem` → `1.1rem`, CTA pill `0.88rem` → `0.78rem` with smaller padding. Quick-action icon buttons shrunk to `28×28` (was `34×34`). Card grid promoted to 4-up at `≥1400px` (was `≥1600px`) so cards never balloon on wide screens. Section vertical padding compressed.
  - Reason: Difatha's screenshot showed cards that were "overwhelmingly huge" and the hero title rendering brand-red on brand-red — invisible. The hero issue traced back to `HeroBackdrop` (designed for the home hero photo composition) bleeding brand-primary across the entire SVG viewBox; even at 22% darken-pass it overwhelmed the text. Card height was driven by the heading-text uppercase + 4:3 image + 2-column spec grid + body padding stack, not by the image alone.
  - Notes: tsc clean. Hero now uses dashboard tokens directly (per `feedback_dashboard_tokens_drive_all_surfaces.md`) rather than the indirected `--surface-bg-light` chain so the brand record's `--color-bg` is the only thing that paints the band. Card title now reads as proper sentence-case ("2017 Ford S-MAX") instead of `2017 FORD S-MAX` — the global `:where(h1,h2,h3,h4) { text-transform: uppercase }` rule from base.css was overriding the card's own `text-transform: none`, so the override now ships on `.cardTitle`. Filter sidebar padding pass keeps the panel under 320px tall at desktop, sitting flush against the card grid top edge.
