- 2026-05-25: Added `auto-wow-uk-bespoke-02` theme — bespoke rugged-archetype for Autowow UK Ltd (Mode A) (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app/themes/auto-wow-uk-bespoke-02/*` (full theme contract — 14 page bodies + 11 components + 14 CSS modules + paired-token base.css + per-theme cookie banner + brand-tinted `--brand-image-<slot>` plumbing). `public/themes/auto-wow-uk-bespoke-02/{favicon.svg,images/*.jpg}` (archetype-default imagery, 7 slots — classic-fallback for rugged slots not yet curated). `tools/.theme-dna/autowowukltd-02.json`, `tools/.palette/autowowukltd-02.json`, `tools/.theme-concepts/auto-wow-uk-bespoke-02.json`, `tools/.theme-fingerprints.json` (registry append). `theme/theme-manifest.json` (regenerated by `theme:sync`).
  - Reason: Generated via `/new-theme` from logo + dealer URL + primary hex `#b51c24` + context "used cars". Locked Signature Concept = "Performance-dealer-floor rugged with a brand-tinted grid-overlay hero, horizontal live-stock ticker, dense filtered-sidebar inventory, and full-bleed mosaic detail page in a brutalist voice". Fingerprint differs from both existing rugged peers (`auto-wow-uk-bespoke` + `showroom-shine-cars-bespoke`) on every axis the uniqueness gate checks: `signatureMove=grid-overlaid-photo`, `headerStructure=dark-accent-strip-top`, `inventoryListPattern=filtered-sidebar`, `vehicleDetailPattern=full-bleed-mosaic`, fonts `Anton + Inter`, `oneBigMove=decorative-density`. Sections composition Jaccard ≤ 0.33 vs both rugged peers.
  - Notes: All Phase 10 gates pass — `tsc --noEmit` 0 errors on theme files; `audit-theme.mjs` 0 blockers / 87 advisories (hardcoded neutral hexes in base.css token definitions are intentional per the paired-token color policy; max-width media queries on theme-specific surface refinements are intentional given mobile-first base styling already covers narrow viewports); `check-theme-uniqueness.mjs` no collisions vs 7 registered themes; `check-theme-similarity.mjs` baseline + peer passes all OK (highest score 0.107). WebFetch on the dealer URL returned 403 (Carous-hosted preview gate) — DNA carries `notes.urlFetchFailed=true` and defensible defaults; operator can enrich via `/update/<slug>` once a preview brand is registered. Theme imagery for 7 slots used the classic-archetype fallback pool (`fetch-theme-images.mjs` reported `used classic-fallback (no rugged/<slot> curated yet)` for all 7 slots) — operator swap recommended for production previews. Favicon: archetype-aware rugged SVG with glyph `A` at `/themes/auto-wow-uk-bespoke-02/favicon.svg`.

- 2026-05-25: `/new-theme` skill — Distinctiveness contract + fingerprint registry block "themes look the same" regression (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `.claude/skills/new-theme/SKILL.md` (new Quality Bar §"Distinctiveness contract", new Phase A2e between A2d and A2c, expanded Phase 8 §"Homepage section composition" with ~30-item section menu, expanded Phase 8 §"Required-widget placement varies per theme", new Phase 10d two-tool gate, Phase 11 §11b fingerprint append, restraint clauses gain `oneBigMove` exemption, new Pitfall row 39, new Tool reference entries for uniqueness + extended similarity); `docs/theme-archetype-specs.md` (archetype catalogue 5 → 8: split `classic` into `classic-trad` + `classic-warm` so the catch-all default gets a real redesign cycle; added `minimalist`, `industrial`, `editorial`); `tools/check-theme-uniqueness.mjs` (new — Phase 10d gate that reads the per-theme concept file + global registry and blocks on archetype-collisions); `tools/check-theme-similarity.mjs` (extended — peer pass at threshold 0.55 against the 3 most recent same-archetype peers, auto-discovered via the concept file); `tools/.theme-fingerprints.json` (new registry, backfilled with the 7 existing themes); `tools/.theme-concepts/auto-wow-uk-bespoke.json` (one example concept file written as a smoke-test artifact); memory entry `reference_theme_fingerprint_registry.md` + index update.
  - Reason: Difatha reported "themes created with /new-theme look similar to one another in feel and look". Side-by-side comparison of auto-wow-uk-bespoke / dual-stock-modern-bespoke / showroom-shine-cars-bespoke / kain-motors-bespoke showed the same chrome (top contact bar, WhatsApp FAB position, AOS animations, glow blobs, restraint-capped typography) and overlapping homepage section composition. Five structural causes diagnosed: (a) only 5 archetypes with `classic` as the catch-all "no redesign needed" default, (b) required-widget chrome identical across themes, (c) restraint rule pushing every theme toward "safe corporate calm", (d) cross-theme similarity check only compared vs the skeleton baseline (missed peer collisions entirely), (e) no fingerprint memory between runs. The fix is structural — themes must declare a Signature Concept upfront, the concept cannot collide with peers, and three quality gates enforce it.
  - Notes: The uniqueness check immediately caught a real collision in the backfilled data: `auto-wow-uk-bespoke` and `showroom-shine-cars-bespoke` (both rugged) ship `showroom-grid` + `gallery-forward` inventory pair — exactly the kind of sameness the gate is built to prevent. Existing themes are not retroactively rebuilt; the gate operates from this point forward via Phase A2e at `/new-theme` invocation. Concept files for the other 6 themes weren't created (registry entry is enough for peer comparison); generate them on demand if you want to re-run Phase 10d against an old theme. Smoke tests: `node tools/check-theme-similarity.mjs --id auto-wow-uk-bespoke` and `node tools/check-theme-uniqueness.mjs --id auto-wow-uk-bespoke` both run cleanly with the expected output.

- 2026-05-12: Shared `EnquiryModal` widget redesigned — theme-locked light chrome, single-column default, dropped L-bracket adornments + competing WhatsApp pill, inline contact rail (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app/widgets/EnquiryModal/EnquiryModal.module.css` rewritten end-to-end. Every previously-token-paired surface (`.dialog`, `.title`, `.fieldLabel`, `.input`, `.textarea`, `.sideCol`, `.contactPanelTitle`, `.contactItemValue`, `.successHeading`) had `background: var(--color-surface)` / `color: var(--color-text)` token pairs and was rendering dark-on-dark invisible per the screenshot — the dual-stock dealer record overrides `--color-surface` to navy without compensating `--color-text`. Locked all chrome to literal hex (`#ffffff` surface, `#0b1c3a` body text, `#52688c` muted, `#e3e7f0` borders) per `feedback_color_palette_policy.md`. Brand primary now flows only on accent affordances (eyebrow, input focus ring, submit button, chip hover, success heading checkmark). `.dialog` slimmed from `width: min(880px, 100%)` → `min(560px, 100%)` and gains a `data-has-side` attribute hook that widens it back to 880px + enables the 1.4fr/1fr split layout only when a caller passes a custom `sidePanel` prop. `border-radius` softened `8px → 14px`; `box-shadow` retinted from `rgba(0,0,0,0.45)` → `rgba(11,28,58,0.32)` for less brutal contrast against the backdrop. Backdrop opacity loosened `0.72 → 0.62`. Submit button restyled from sharp-cornered black brick to brand-primary pill (`border-radius: 999px`, brand-tinted shadow). New `.contactRail` + `.contactRailChips` + `.contactChip` + `.contactChipWa` rules render a slim horizontal pill row at the bottom of the form. Dropped these now-unused rules: `.bracket` (the L-shaped corner adornments), `.whatsappBtn` (the green pill that competed with Send Enquiry in the actions row), `.contactPanel`, `.contactPanelTitle`, `.contactItem`, `.contactItemIcon`, `.contactItemWhatsApp`, `.contactItemLabel`, `.contactItemValue` (replaced by the leaner contact rail). `.sideCol` retained but neutered for custom-content callers — soft light gradient backdrop + left border, no built-in styling for nested children. `app/widgets/EnquiryModal/EnquiryModal.tsx` updated: dropped the two `<div className={styles.bracket}>` divs; added `data-has-side={sidePanel ? 'true' : 'false'}` to `.dialog`; removed the in-actions WhatsApp pill (`<a className={styles.whatsappBtn}>`); restructured the layout so the default contact panel (Call / WhatsApp / Email) is no longer a full-color gradient side column but a slim post-form rail inside `formCol`, only rendering when no custom `sidePanel` is passed and at least one direct-contact channel exists; the `<aside className={styles.sideCol}>` only renders when a caller supplies custom JSX via `sidePanel`. All existing imports still used (Mail in email chip, Phone in rail + success Call CTA, WhatsAppIcon in WhatsApp chip, Send on submit, X on close, CheckCircle2 in success).
  - Reason: Difatha: "Constact enquiry modal look so poorly done and overcomplicated" with a screenshot showing — (1) "CONTACT [GENERAL]" title invisible, (2) "Full name / Email / Phone / Message" labels all invisible above their inputs, (3) the brand-primary teal gradient side panel taking equal weight to the form, (4) the green WhatsApp pill competing with Send Enquiry in the actions row, (5) decorative L-bracket corners sitting outside the dialog edge, (6) four field-required errors stacked under empty inputs after a submit attempt with red-tinted backgrounds. Root cause for the invisible text is the same `feedback_color_palette_policy.md` violation that hit Testimonials, BrandTrust, Contact page, and Finance page in this theme — the shared widget paired surface + text tokens and got bitten when the dealer record overrode one side. Beyond the contrast fix, the modal genuinely had too many decorative elements (brackets, full gradient sidebar, dual CTAs, dotted-pattern overlay) for what is a 4-field form — collapsing to single-column + contact rail removes the overcomplication.
  - Notes: `useLeadsForm` validation timing is unchanged — "This field is required" still surfaces post-submit-attempt, which is correct UX. Custom-sidePanel callers (none in the current codebase, but the prop is part of the public API) retain the dual-column layout via `data-has-side='true'` and bring their own panel styling. Success state still lives inside `formCol` so it renders single-column too. Backdrop click-to-close + Escape key + body-scroll lock + focus restoration all unchanged in the TSX. Contact rail's WhatsApp chip uses literal `#25D366` (WhatsApp brand green) on the icon + hover border — same brand-color convention used everywhere else in this codebase for WhatsApp.

- 2026-05-12: `dual-stock-modern-bespoke` Contact page redesign — theme-locked light cards, 2-col contact grid, fixed invisible labels + duplicated address bug (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `pages/contact/page.module.css` rewritten end-to-end. Every previously-token-paired surface (`.topicChip`, `.contactList a`, `.contactList li > span`, `.hoursPanel`, `.h2`, `.h3`, `.lead`) had `background: var(--color-surface)` + `color: var(--color-text)` — the dealer brand record overrides `--color-surface` to dark navy without compensating `--color-text`, so the `<strong>` labels ("Call" / "WhatsApp" / "Email" / "Showroom") were rendering dark-on-dark invisible per the screenshot, and the three inactive topic chips were dark-pills with dark glyphs. Locked all card chrome to literal hex (`background: #ffffff` / `border: 1px solid #e3e7f0` / body `#0b1c3a` / muted `#52688c`) per `feedback_color_palette_policy.md`. Contact list redesigned: removed the heavy `border-left: 3px solid var(--color-primary)` chunky-bar treatment (which combined with the dark surface made each row look like a brutalist banner), replaced with subtle hover lift (`border-color` shift toward brand-primary + `translateY(-1px)` + `0 6px 18px` brand-tinted shadow). Padding reduced from `14px 16px` → `12px 14px`, icon from `36px` → `32px`, grid switched from single-column `flex` to `grid-template-columns: 1fr` (mobile) / `1fr 1fr` (≥560px) so Call / WhatsApp / Email / Showroom render as a 2×2 grid instead of four tall full-width banners. `.contactList strong` now has explicit `color: #52688c` + smaller `0.72rem` uppercase tracking — labels read as quiet eyebrow metadata above the value (`color: #0b1c3a 0.95rem`) instead of inheriting dark-on-dark. Topic chips: inactive locked to white + navy text + light border with brand-primary hover; active stays brand-primary filled. Hours panel rebuilt with dashed `#eef1f7` day separators + explicit literal day/value colors. `pages/contact/page.tsx` — replaced the ad-hoc `[line1, line2, city, county, postcode].filter(Boolean).join(', ')` build with `contact.showroomAddress` from `lib/contact.ts` (which prefers `brand.location.fullAddress` and only falls back to the parts join when absent), fixing the duplicated "Winnersh, RG41 5TP, Winnersh, RG41 5TP" tail in the screenshot — caused by a dealer record that stuffs the full address into `line2` AND populates `city`/`postcode` independently. Single source of truth now matches what the footer renders.
  - Reason: Difatha: "contact page looks soo poorly designed" with a screenshot showing the four contact rows on dark navy surfaces with invisible labels, the three inactive topic chips invisible, a chunky brand-color left bar making each row feel like a banner, and the showroom address duplicating the city+postcode at the end. The root cause is the same color-pairing-policy violation that already bit Testimonials and BrandTrust earlier in this theme — fix follows the same pattern: theme-lock card chrome to literal light values, keep brand primary only on accent affordances. The address duplication is an orthogonal data-shape bug; routing through the helper picks up the dealer record's `fullAddress` first and side-steps the bad join.
  - Notes: `lib/contact.ts` not modified — its `buildAddressLine` already implements the fullAddress-first preference correctly; only the contact page was bypassing it. Phone-display formatting (`7723090172` rendering without a leading zero in the screenshot) is a brand-record data issue, not a theme issue, and was left alone — `phoneDisplay` is a verbatim passthrough of `brand.location.phone`. Form card on the right column was already locked to light literals so no change there. Topic-chip active state still uses `var(--color-primary)` for the filled state — that's the *value* side of a paired token, not the surface a brand record can subvert, so it remains correct. No JSX changes other than the address-source swap.

- 2026-05-12: `dual-stock-modern-bespoke` Finance "Three steps. One quote." redesign — light theme-locked cards, watermark numbers, desktop 3-up with dashed connecting rail (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `pages/finance/page.tsx` — `.steps` list items restructured. Old: 2-column grid `[ 56px circle | (h3 + p) ]` with a small solid-primary numbered pill on the left. New: each `<li class="step">` carries a watermark `.stepNumber` (positioned absolute top-right, `01` / `02` / `03`, semi-transparent primary at huge font size), a `.stepTag` chip ("STEP 01" etc, primary-tinted background with primary text — same chip language as Testimonials' badges), a `.stepTitle` h3, and a `.stepBody` p. Watermark numbers use a fresh 2-digit format (`01` not `1`) for editorial polish. `pages/finance/page.module.css` rewrite for the steps block: theme-locked light cards (`background: #ffffff`, `border: 1px solid #e3e7f0`, `box-shadow: 0 18px 40px rgba(11, 28, 58, 0.06)`, `border-radius: 14px`) replacing the prior `var(--color-surface)` + `var(--color-border)` + `border-left: 3px solid var(--color-primary)` chrome that was hitting the color-pairing bug (dark surface override + dark text token = invisible h3 in the screenshot). Top-edge gradient accent strip `::before` matches the BrandTrust / Testimonials pattern (`linear-gradient(90deg, var(--color-primary), color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-primary) 60%, #8fd2f3))`) for visual consistency across all light cards in the theme. Card hover: `translateY(-3px)` + deeper shadow + primary-tinted border. Watermark `.stepNumber` is `clamp(4.5rem, 7vw, 6rem)` in `color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-primary) 12%, transparent)` — visible but quiet, anchors the card without competing with the title. Title color hardcoded to `#0b1c3a` (was inheriting `--color-text`), body `#52688c` (was `var(--color-muted)`). `.intro` `max-width` bumped `880px → 1200px` so the desktop 3-up row has room to breathe. Desktop layout (`min-width: 768px`): `.steps` grid-template-columns goes from `1fr` to `repeat(3, 1fr)` with `clamp(16px, 2.4vw, 28px)` gap, and a `.steps::before` decorative dashed rail (`background-image: linear-gradient(90deg, color-mix(...primary...28%) 50%, transparent 50%); background-size: 12px 2px; background-repeat: repeat-x`) is drawn behind the cards at 50% vertical from 6% to 94% horizontal, with `.step { z-index: 1 }` lifting cards above the rail so it visually connects them. Cards still hide the rail where they overlap.
  - Reason: Difatha: "redesign this to a more modern design, too cheap design" (screenshot showed the prior version with invisible step titles caused by the `var(--color-surface)` + `var(--color-text)` dark-on-dark pairing, plus a basic dark-card-with-circle-number layout). The cheap feel came from three things: (1) the contrast bug — invisible titles read as a broken section; (2) the dark slab + neon circle felt like a generic CMS template, not a 2026 dealer finance page; (3) vertical-only stacking wasted desktop real estate. Fix all three: lock surface+foreground at the component level with literal hex values per `feedback_color_palette_policy` so brand-record overrides can't reintroduce the bug; redesign with watermark numbers + chip tags + clean title/body hierarchy + accent strip (the same vocabulary used by Testimonials / BrandTrust elsewhere in the theme — strengthens visual consistency); use a 3-up desktop layout connected by a dashed rail to communicate sequence visually rather than relying on numbers alone.
  - Notes: 2-digit numbering (`01 02 03`) is intentional — single digits look thin at the watermark size and 2-digit reads as more "design system" / editorial. The dashed connecting rail uses a CSS-only technique (linear-gradient with hard 50% stop tiled at 12px) — no SVG, no extra element, transparent to the printer. Rail is hidden on mobile because the cards stack vertically and a horizontal rail would float disconnected. `.stepTag`, `.stepTitle`, `.stepBody` all get `position: relative; z-index: 1` so they layer above the absolutely-positioned `.stepNumber` watermark (otherwise the watermark would render over the chip in the top-right corner). Products grid below the steps section was NOT touched — it has the same `var(--color-surface)` token usage and likely a similar contrast issue, but the user pointed only at the "Three steps" panel, so per the no-out-of-scope rule that's a separate ask. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

- 2026-05-12: `dual-stock-modern-bespoke` inventory page — type filter moved out of hero into modern underline tab bar; inventory hero gets a real background image (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `pages/used-cars/UsedCarsClient.tsx` — the in-hero `.typeTabs` segmented control (`<div role="tablist">` with three pill buttons inside `.dual-page-hero__inner`) deleted. Replaced by a new `.tabsBar` rendered at the very top of `.body` (above the toolbar) with the same three tabs (All stock / Cars / Bikes), each tab now structured as `<span tabLabel>` + `<span tabCount>` for cleaner typography control. Hero now carries the class `dual-page-hero dual-page-hero--used-cars` (was just `dual-page-hero`) so it picks up an actual background image instead of rendering as a flat dark band. Hero copy unchanged. `pages/used-cars/page.module.css` — `.typeTabs` / `.typeTab` / `.typeTabActive` / `.typeTabCount` rules removed. New rules: `.tabsBar` (horizontal flex with `border-bottom: 1px solid #e3e7f0`, horizontal scroll on overflow with hidden scrollbar, 20px margin-bottom); `.tab` (transparent button, `padding: 14px 6px`, Roboto Condensed uppercase 0.95rem, muted `#52688c` color, 48px min-height for tap target); `.tab::after` underline indicator (`height: 2px`, `background: var(--color-primary)`, `transform: scaleX(0)` default with center origin and a 220ms cubic-bezier transition); `:hover` previews a 50%-scale 50%-opacity underline; `.tabActive` darkens text to `#0b1c3a` and scales the underline to `scaleX(1)` at 3px tall; `.tabCount` is a soft pill badge (`#f1f5fc` bg + muted text, 22px tall, rounded-full) that flips to `color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-primary) 14%, transparent)` bg + primary-color text when the tab is active. `.hero` selector kept (empty rule body) as an anchor for future hero-local tweaks. `styles/base.css` — `.dual-page-hero--used-cars` added to the shared image-fallback selector group alongside `--contact`, `--compare`, `--wishlist`, `--privacy`, `--cookie`, mapping to `var(--brand-image-hero, none), url('/themes/dual-stock-modern-bespoke/images/hero.jpg')` so a dealer override (`--brand-image-hero`) wins and the theme default `hero.jpg` is the fallback. The shared dark-overlay rules on `.dual-page-hero::before` / text colors apply automatically.
  - Reason: Difatha: "instead of page-module__uy2KRW__typeTabs in hero section, redesign the inventory section as tabbed, clean and modern tabs, remove this from the hero section, add bg image in hero section in inventory page too modern and professionally." Three changes in one ask: (1) hero was carrying both branded copy AND an interactive type-filter — split responsibilities, hero is now pure context (breadcrumb + title + lead on a photo backdrop), the type filter belongs with the content it filters; (2) the previous segmented-pill tabs felt like form chrome, the user wanted "tabbed, clean and modern" — underline tabs with a sliding primary-color indicator are the canonical modern tab pattern (Material, Apple HIG, Stripe dashboard, GitHub all use this); (3) inventory hero was flat dark because no `--<slot>` modifier was applied, so neither the dealer override nor a theme default image had a hook to render — adding `--used-cars` to the shared group reuses `hero.jpg` (the main showroom shot) without needing a new asset.
  - Notes: Tab count badge for the "All stock" tab now renders `vehicles.length` directly (including zero) rather than the previous `vehicles.length || ''` truthy gate — a "0" badge is informative on an empty inventory and an empty string mid-tab looks like a broken element. Cars / Bikes counts get the same treatment. The shared dark-hero overlay (rgba(8,16,32,0.86 → 0.78)) sits over `hero.jpg` so the title/lead/breadcrumb white text stays legible regardless of dealer image (per `feedback_hero_overlay_dark_uniform`). `tabsBar overflow-x: auto` + `scrollbar-width: none` lets the tabs scroll horizontally on phones if the count badges push past viewport width without showing an ugly scrollbar. The 220ms cubic-bezier underline transition is the same curve used on the filter drawer — keeps the page's motion vocabulary consistent. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

- 2026-05-12: `dual-stock-modern-bespoke` inventory grid pinned to 1 / 2 / 4 columns at small / medium / large viewports (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `pages/used-cars/page.module.css` `.grid`. Reverted the previous-pass `repeat(auto-fill, minmax(260px, 1fr))` rule to explicit breakpoints — `grid-template-columns: 1fr` baseline (mobile, 1 col), `repeat(2, 1fr)` at `min-width: 768px` (tablet, 2 cols), `repeat(4, 1fr)` at `min-width: 1024px` (desktop, 4 cols). Gap unchanged at 18px.
  - Reason: Difatha: "display 4 cards in large screens, 2 in medium screens and 1 in small screens." Auto-fill was packing 3 columns at ~960-1100px viewports (between the tablet and desktop breakpoints) which Difatha didn't want — he wants a clean 1/2/4 step. Fixed breakpoints give predictable column counts and remove the in-between 3-col band the auto-fill produced.
  - Notes: At 1024px container width × 4 cols ÷ 18px gaps = ~230px per card — borderline tight but the VehicleCard layout (4:3 aspect ratio image, single-line title, 2-col specs grid) handles narrow widths. Above 1440px the cards widen with the 1fr fractional units rather than adding more columns, by design (Difatha specified exactly 4 at large, not "4+").

- 2026-05-12: `dual-stock-modern-bespoke` inventory filters converted to slide-in drawer; listing grid reclaims full container width (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `pages/used-cars/UsedCarsClient.tsx` + `pages/used-cars/page.module.css`. Filters `<aside>` is no longer an in-grid column on desktop — it's a fixed slide-in drawer (`position: fixed; left: 0; width: min(360px, 92vw)`) that translates in from the left edge via `transform: translateX(-110% → 0)` with a 280ms cubic-bezier transition, gated by the existing `filtersOpen` state. New backdrop `<button>` element (`.filtersBackdrop`, `position: fixed; inset: 0; rgba(11,28,58,0.5)` + 2px blur) renders only when open and closes the drawer on click. Drawer chrome now has three regions: sticky `.filtersHead` (title + Reset + new ✕ close icon button), scrolling `.filtersBody` (the field stack inside `overflow-y: auto`), and sticky `.filtersFoot` (full-width "Show N results" primary CTA that closes the drawer). New `useEffect` on `filtersOpen`: locks `document.body.style.overflow = 'hidden'` while open and listens for `Escape` to close; cleanup restores prior overflow and removes the listener. `aria-hidden={!filtersOpen}` on the aside; `aria-controls="dual-filters"` was already on the toolbar toggle. CSS: `.layout` collapsed from `display: grid; grid-template-columns: 1fr` (with a `1024px` rule that set it to `280px 1fr`) to `display: block` so the results column always spans the full container. Desktop sidebar override (`@media (min-width: 1024px) { .filters { display: flex } }`) deleted. Listing `.grid` switched from fixed breakpoints (`1fr` → `1fr 1fr` @768 → `1fr 1fr 1fr` @1024 → `repeat(4, 1fr)` @1440) to `repeat(auto-fill, minmax(260px, 1fr))` — naturally scales 1 col on phones up to ~5 cols at 1440px container with no breakpoint juggling, and stays graceful at any width in between.
  - Reason: Difatha: "redesign filters column as openable sidebar to create more space for listings in inventory page." Previous desktop layout had a 280px+28px-gap fixed sidebar eating ~308px of horizontal real estate on every screen ≥1024px, leaving only 3 cols of vehicles at 1024 and 4 cols at 1440. Drawer-on-demand pattern reclaims that space for the grid (now ~4 cols at 1024 and ~5 cols at 1440 via `auto-fill`) while keeping the entry point — the "Filters" button in the toolbar — visible at every viewport. Filters are still one click away and the count badge on the toggle still surfaces active-filter count. Auto-fill minmax was picked over manual breakpoints because the new layout has no sidebar to compete with — letting the browser pack as many 260px+ cards as fit is more robust than hand-tuned breakpoint values for the dealer-photoshoot-dependent card width.
  - Notes: `useEffect` body-scroll-lock saves the prior `document.body.style.overflow` and restores it on cleanup rather than hard-coding `''` — important because other components (search overlay, mobile drawer, lightbox) also lock scroll, and stacking them shouldn't trample each other's state. Backdrop element is a `<button>` not a `<div>` so it's keyboard-focusable and screen-reader-announceable as "Close filters". Drawer z-index 80 sits above the header (z-index 50 in `Header.module.css`) but below the search overlay (z-index 70) and the mobile drawer (z-index 60) — wait, mobile drawer at 60 is below the filters drawer at 80, that's correct: filters take precedence when invoked from the inventory toolbar; backdrop at z-index 75 sits between header and drawer so the drawer rises above the dimmed page. `min(360px, 92vw)` width keeps the drawer narrow on phones (full-screen would be too aggressive — the user can still see what they're filtering) and capped at 360px on desktop (wider than that wastes drawer space and feels modal-heavy). All filter field controls untouched — the `<Field>` helper and the seven select/input fields render identically inside the new `.filtersBody`. `aria-hidden={!filtersOpen}` paired with `visibility: hidden` (in CSS) means assistive tech doesn't see the drawer content when closed, even though it remains in the DOM. `tsc --noEmit` clean on the changes.

- 2026-05-12: `dual-stock-modern-bespoke` Footer re-flipped to DARK / BLACK canvas (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `components/Footer.module.css` rewritten end-to-end (header comment swung back from "LIGHT THEME" to "DARK / BLACK THEME"). Three tonal steps re-established: upper CTA band `linear-gradient(180deg, #0e131b → #0a0e14)` with a brand-primary radial glow at `38%` opacity (top-right) plus an inset corner radial at `22%` for depth; 4-col mega body sits on solid `#0a0e14` with a `rgba(255,255,255,0.04)` hairline top divider; legal bar steps down to `#06090e`. Foreground palette locked to literals so brand-record overrides of `--color-text` / `--color-muted` cannot break contrast on near-black: titles `#ffffff`, primary body `#f0f3f8`, secondary body `#b6c1d1`, muted/address/legal `#9aa6b8`. Social pills converted from white-with-light-border to dark-card chrome (`rgba(255,255,255,0.06)` fill + `rgba(255,255,255,0.12)` border + `#f0f3f8` glyph) with brand-primary hover; `.socialDisabled` gets `opacity: 0.55` so absent socials read as disabled rather than identical to active ones on the dark canvas. Dividers between bands use `rgba(255,255,255,0.04-0.08)` instead of `#e3e7f0`. Footer top border `rgba(255,255,255,0.06)` instead of `#e3e7f0`. `.upperTitle` `#0b1c3a` → `#ffffff`; `.upperLead` `#52688c` → `#9aa6b8`; `.colTitle` `#0b1c3a` → `#ffffff`; `.colBody` `#52688c` → `#b6c1d1`; `.linkList a` / `.contactList a` `#52688c` → `#b6c1d1`. `.contactCta` (outline button) keeps brand-primary fg/border — reads well on near-black via the base `dual-btn--outline` pairing.
  - Reason: Difatha: "redesign the footer to use dark theme like before" (replaying the earlier black-canvas design). The intermediate "all pages to LIGHT surfaces" pass had reverted the footer to white/grey-blue along with the rest of the theme; that wasn't his actual intent — the footer was meant to stay as the dark anchor at the bottom of the page (analogous to the home Hero / CtaBand / inner-page hero band moments that all remained dark). Net effect on the dual-stock canvas: photo-backed dark hero up top → light content sections in the body → black footer at the bottom = the dealer-signage rhythm the theme was built for.
  - Notes: All foreground colors are literal hex values, not `var(--color-text)` / `var(--color-muted)` — paired surface+foreground at component level per `feedback_color_palette_policy.md` so the WCAG AA contrast holds regardless of brand-record token overrides. Brand primary still flows through the eyebrow rule, brand mark badge, hover states, contact CTA, and Carous credit link — single chromatic accent, multiple touchpoints. `.dual-btn--ghost` (used by "Sell yours" CTA in the upper band) reads `color: #ffffff` + 50%-white border via base.css so it pairs cleanly with the dark band — no module-level override needed. Brand mark badge (`width: 28px` `--color-primary` square with white initial) pops against black. No JSX changes in `Footer.tsx`. File is still untracked in git, so this is a clean re-write rather than a revert through the index.

- 2026-05-12: `dual-stock-modern-bespoke` inner-page hero band re-flipped to DARK overlay — fixes white-on-white captions caused by an earlier light-flip vs. white-text mismatch (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `styles/base.css` `.dual-page-hero` block. `::before` overlay swapped from the diagonal light wash (`linear-gradient(115deg, rgba(245,248,255,0.94) → 0.78 → 0.45)`) to a uniform dark overlay (`linear-gradient(180deg, rgba(8,16,32,0.86) → 0.78)`). `.dual-page-hero` `color: #0b1c3a` → `color: #ffffff`; `border-bottom: 1px solid #e3e7f0` removed (looked like a glitch with the dark band against the section below); `::after` `background-color: #f5f8ff` → `#0b1c3a` so the band has a dark fallback floor when both the dealer override and the theme default jpg fail. Inner text rules (`__breadcrumb` `rgba(255,255,255,0.72)` / `__title` `#ffffff` with shadow / `__lead` `rgba(255,255,255,0.9)`) were already white from the earlier compact-hero pass — they now pair with the dark overlay instead of fighting it.
  - Reason: Difatha: "all hero section redesign are poor, use dark themed and captions be visible" (screenshot showed a Finance hero on a snow scene where the breadcrumb / title / lead were rendering effectively invisible). Two prior commits had each individually been correct but conflicted with each other: (1) the "compact hero" pass made the section dark + captions white; (2) the later "all pages to LIGHT surfaces" pass flipped the overlay back to a white wash but kept the captions white — so on every inner page the captions were now white on a near-white overlay. Bright underlying images (snow, sky, white car bodies) made this catastrophic; even mid-tone images left text barely legible. Fix is to commit to ONE direction at the component level — the photo-backed inner page heroes are a strong dark moment intentionally separate from the surrounding light surfaces of the rest of the theme (analogous to the home Hero and CtaBand which the earlier light-pass also kept dark).
  - Notes: Uniform dark overlay was deliberately picked over a diagonal/asymmetric gradient — a diagonal gradient's low-opacity edge always fails on bright images, which is what bit the first compact-hero pass. Memory `feedback_hero_overlay_dark_uniform.md` saved with the rule (uniform dark + white captions, never diagonal or light wash on photo-backed heroes). `.dual-page-hero--<slot>::after` image rules untouched (dealer override + theme default fallback contract per `feedback_brand_image_plumbing`). Used-cars list typeTabs container is white-on-white-card so still legible against the dark hero. Vehicle detail's `heroBand` (different structural pattern — title strip above gallery, no bg image) and the homepage Hero left out of scope.

- 2026-05-12: `dual-stock-modern-bespoke` — all inner pages + inventory flipped to LIGHT surfaces; dark blue panels eliminated (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `pages/used-cars/[slug]/page.module.css` (vehicle detail) — `.heroBand` from dark navy gradient → `linear-gradient(180deg, #ffffff 0%, #f1f5fc 100%)` with brand-primary radial glow + dark text; `.crumbs` / `.title` / `.titleMeta` text from white to `#0b1c3a` / `#52688c`; `.actionPill` from translucent white-on-dark to white pill with `#0b1c3a` text + brand-primary hover; gallery placeholder `#181d27` → `#eef1f8`; thumb hover border from translucent-white to brand-primary tint; `.specsStrip` from `#14181f` → light tinted band with dark labels/values; `.sidebar` from `#0a0e14` → `#ffffff` card with brand-primary top border + dark text, `.contactPill` from translucent-white-on-dark to `#f1f5fc` chips with hover-to-primary; `.keyFacts` borders/colors flipped to light; `.stickyMobile` from `rgba(10,14,20,0.97)` → `rgba(255,255,255,0.97)` with `#e3e7f0` top border. `pages/about/page.module.css` `.statBlock` from dark gradient → light gradient with `#e3e7f0` border, inner `.stat` cards on white, label colors dark/muted. `pages/contact/page.module.css` `.formCard` from dark gradient → white-to-tinted gradient with brand-primary top border + dark text. `pages/part-exchange/page.module.css` `.stepsCard` same treatment + `.h3` flipped to dark. Small `.badgeCar` chips in `services` + `Testimonials` from `#0a0e14`/`#0b1c3a` → `#52688c` (still differentiated from brand-primary `.badgeBike`). `pages/used-cars/page.module.css` (inventory listing) — `.typeTabs` overlay from `rgba(0,0,0,0.4)` dark backdrop → white with light border (the page hero is now light too); `.toolbar` / `.filters` / `.pagination button` / `.compareCount` / `.wishlistCount` / skeleton gradient all locked to literal light values (`#ffffff`, `#e3e7f0`, `#f1f5fc`, `#eef1f8`) instead of `var(--color-surface)` / `var(--color-bg)` / `var(--color-border)` so brand-record overrides of those tokens can't push surfaces back to dark navy. `styles/base.css` `.dual-page-hero::before` overlay flipped from dark diagonal gradient (`rgba(11,28,58,*)`) to light diagonal wash (`rgba(245,248,255,0.94 → 0.78 → 0.45)`); `.dual-page-hero` color `#ffffff` → `#0b1c3a` with `border-bottom: 1px solid #e3e7f0`; `::after` solid fallback `#0b1c3a` → `#f5f8ff`; `__breadcrumb` / `__title` / `__lead` text colors all flipped to dark; legacy `.dual-header` / `.dual-footer` fallback rules (inactive but kept for stragglers) also flipped from dark navy to light. `components/Footer.module.css` rewritten end-to-end as a light footer (was re-darkened by a concurrent pass): `.footer` `#ffffff`, `.upperBand` white-to-`#f5f8ff` gradient with primary-tinted glow, `.mega` `#f1f5fc`, `.legal` `#e9eef9`; all text colors `#0b1c3a` / `#52688c`; social pills white with light border + brand-primary hover.
  - Reason: Difatha: "redesign all other pages including inventory page to remove dark blue surface colors." Earlier passes mixed designs — VehicleCard + HeroSearchStrip were light, but vehicle detail / about-stats / contact-form / part-exchange-steps cards stayed dark navy, the inner page heroes used a dark diagonal overlay (re-introduced by a concurrent design pass), and the footer was re-darkened. Net effect on Columbus preview: roughly half the pages still showed dark navy panels. This pass uses literal light-tier values (`#ffffff`, `#f5f8ff`, `#f1f5fc`, `#e9eef9`, `#e3e7f0`, `#0b1c3a`, `#52688c`) at component level so brand-record overrides of `--color-surface`/`--color-bg`/`--color-border` can't reintroduce dark blue (Pitfall-38 immunity, light direction).
  - Notes: Intentional dark moments preserved: home Hero + CtaBand (photo+overlay rhythm), mobile drawer (`Header.module.css:295` — off-canvas takeover), search overlay (`Header.module.css:362` — modal backdrop), lightbox (`pages/used-cars/[slug]/page.module.css:641` — image-viewing modal). `.dual-section--dark` helper in `base.css` left alone — unused by any current component but kept for legacy stragglers. `tsc --noEmit` clean on the changed files.

- 2026-05-12: `dual-stock-modern-bespoke` inner-page hero band redesign — compact height, image-revealing diagonal overlay, light text (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `styles/base.css` — shared `.dual-page-hero` rules rewritten in one place since every inner page (`about`, `services`, `finance`, `part-exchange`, `sell-your-car`, `recently-sold`, `contact`, `compare`, `wishlist`, `privacy-policy`, `cookie-policy`, `used-cars` list) renders the band via `<section class="dual-page-hero dual-page-hero--<slot>">`. Padding cut from `clamp(3.5rem, 10vw, 8rem) … clamp(3rem, 8vw, 6rem)` (56-128px top / 48-96px bottom) to `clamp(2rem, 4.5vw, 3.5rem) … clamp(1.75rem, 4vw, 3rem)` (32-56px top / 28-48px bottom) — roughly half the prior height. Overlay (`.dual-page-hero::before`) swapped from a near-opaque white wash (`rgba(245,248,255,0.94)` over `rgba(241,245,252,0.88)` plus a primary-tinted radial blob) to a diagonal dark gradient `linear-gradient(115deg, rgba(11,28,58,0.78) 0%, rgba(11,28,58,0.55) 45%, rgba(11,28,58,0.28) 100%)` — text-side anchored at 78% opacity for legibility, right side bottoms out at 28% so the actual brand image (e.g. `/themes/dual-stock-modern-bespoke/images/about.jpg`, or the dealer-supplied `--brand-image-*` override) shows through clearly instead of being washed out. `.dual-page-hero::after` gets a solid `#0b1c3a` fallback `background-color` so the band still has a dark-navy floor if both the dealer override AND the theme default image fail to load (previously the white wash hid that failure mode). Inner-content `color: #0b1c3a` flipped to `color: #ffffff` on the section; breadcrumb dropped from `#52688c` / `#0b1c3a` to `rgba(255,255,255,0.72)` / `#ffffff`; title from `clamp(2rem, 4.4vw, 3.4rem)` `#0b1c3a` to `clamp(1.5rem, 3vw, 2.25rem)` `#ffffff` with `text-shadow: 0 1px 14px rgba(11,28,58,0.35)` to keep legibility against busy image areas; lead from `clamp(1rem, 1.4vw, 1.15rem)` `#52688c` to `clamp(0.92rem, 1.2vw, 1.05rem)` `rgba(255,255,255,0.9)`. `.dual-page-hero__inner` `gap` tightened from 14px → 8px and breadcrumb font shrunk `0.85rem → 0.72rem` with tighter `letter-spacing` to match the more condensed scale. Bottom border (`border-bottom: 1px solid #e3e7f0`) removed since text is now white and the band sits on dark — a hairline light border looked like a glitch at the seam with the section below.
  - Reason: Difatha: "redesign all other hero sections apart from homepage, use small height and less detailed, with image in the bg more revealing but still captions not overshadowed." Three constraints in one ask. (1) Smaller height → cut padding ~50% on top and bottom. (2) Less detail → drop the primary-tinted radial blob layer and the secondary gradient stop, keep one clean diagonal overlay, shrink the title and lead. (3) Image more revealing but captions not overshadowed → diagonal overlay is the key — solid dark would defeat purpose (image hidden), a flat low-opacity overlay would lose caption legibility; the diagonal anchors text-side dark (78%) for guaranteed readability and ramps to 28% on the opposite side so the photo dominates the right half. Single-source change in `base.css` means every inner page picks up the new look without per-page edits.
  - Notes: The `dual-page-hero--<slot>::after` image rules (`background-image: var(--brand-image-X, none), url('/themes/.../images/X.jpg')`) were left untouched — those are the dealer-override + theme-default fallback contract per the `feedback_brand_image_plumbing` memory and aren't part of the visual redesign. The used-cars list's local `.hero { padding-bottom: clamp(2rem, 5vw, 3.5rem) }` override in `pages/used-cars/page.module.css` was left as-is — its purpose is to add bottom breathing room for the inline `.typeTabs` segmented control, and the new base bottom padding (28-48px) plus that override still yields a sensible band. `.typeTabs` itself uses an opaque white container (`background: #ffffff`, `border: 1px solid #e3e7f0`) so it remains a legible card floating on the new dark overlay — no contrast issue with the inner control. Vehicle detail's `heroBand` was intentionally left out of scope: it's a different structural pattern (title strip ABOVE the full-width gallery mosaic, no background image, soft brand-tinted blob) per the `feedback_vehicle_detail_modern_structure` memory, not the same `dual-page-hero` family. Homepage hero similarly untouched (out of scope per the user's "apart from homepage" carve-out).

- 2026-05-12: `dual-stock-modern-bespoke` Footer flipped to deep-black canvas with contrast-tuned foreground tokens (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `components/Footer.module.css` rewritten end-to-end (header comment updated from "LIGHT THEME" to "DARK / BLACK THEME"). Three tonal steps: upper CTA band `linear-gradient(180deg, #0e131b → #0a0e14)` with the existing brand-primary radial glow remixed via `color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-primary) 14-38%, transparent)` so the glow follows the dealer's brand colour; mega 4-col body sits on `#0a0e14`; legal bar steps down to `#06090e` for tonal separation. Foreground palette locked to literals so a dealer record overriding `--color-text` cannot crash contrast: column titles + upper title `#ffffff`, primary body copy `#f0f3f8`, secondary body `#b6c1d1`, muted/address/legal `#9aa6b8` (cool greys with AA on near-black). Dividers `rgba(255,255,255,0.08)` between bands; social pills converted from white cards w/ light border to dark-card chrome (`rgba(255,255,255,0.06)` fill + `rgba(255,255,255,0.12)` border + `#f0f3f8` glyph) with brand-primary hover unchanged. `.upperTitle` `#0b1c3a` → `#ffffff`; `.upperLead` `#52688c` → `#9aa6b8`; `.colTitle` `#0b1c3a` → `#ffffff`; `.colBody` `#52688c` → `#b6c1d1`; `.address` → `#9aa6b8`. `.contactCta` override simplified — the base `dual-btn--outline` in `styles/base.css` (lines 205-213) already paints the outline button in brand-primary which reads well on the new black canvas, so the module-level override now just confirms that pairing (no white-on-dark variant, avoids losing the specificity battle to the `[data-theme-id]` attribute selector anyway).
  - Reason: Difatha: "for footer implement black bg and update the foreground elements from contrast". The previous footer was light (#f1f5fc / #ffffff bands with navy text) which clashed with the rest of the dual-stock surfaces where the hero, CtaBand, and several testimonials/recognition bands lean dark/dramatic. A black footer anchors the page bottom, matches the dealer-signage aesthetic that inspired this theme, and lets the brand-primary radial glow + accent links carry more weight visually.
  - Notes: All foreground colors are literal hex values, not `var(--color-text)` / `var(--color-muted)` — paired surface+foreground at the component level per `feedback_color_palette_policy.md` so the WCAG AA contrast holds regardless of brand-record token overrides. Brand primary still flows through the eyebrow rule, brand mark badge, hover states, contact CTA, and Carous credit link — single chromatic accent, multiple touchpoints. `.dual-btn--ghost` (used by "Sell yours" CTA in the upper band) reads `color: #ffffff` + 50%-white border per base.css so it already works against the dark band — no change needed there. Brand mark badge (`width: 28px` `--color-primary` square with white "D") still pops against black. No JSX changes in `Footer.tsx`.

- 2026-05-12: `dual-stock-modern-bespoke` VehicleCard — Car/Bike type badge dropped entirely from card chrome (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `components/cards/VehicleCard.tsx` — removed the `{!hideTypeBadge && <span typeBadge…>}` block from `.mediaBadges` and collapsed `.mediaBadges` to render only when `vehicle.featured` is set (was always rendered, since at least one of the two badges always existed). `hideTypeBadge?: boolean` prop dropped from `VehicleCardProps` and the function signature. `components/cards/VehicleCard.module.css` — `.typeBadge`, `.typeBadgeCar`, `.typeBadgeBike` rules deleted. `components/sections/FeaturedStock.tsx` — removed the `hideTypeBadge` prop from the inner `Rail` helper's signature, the two `<Rail … hideTypeBadge />` call sites (cars + bikes rails on the homepage), and the `hideTypeBadge={hideTypeBadge}` pass-through on the inner `<VehicleCard>`.
  - Reason: Difatha: "remove car/bike badges from sold section in homepage and all other pages that contains vehicle cards". Follows the same intent as the earlier `hideTypeBadge` opt-out on featured rails ("if a car appears in cars section, don't display car badge, ofcourse is a car") but expanded to every card surface — the badge is now globally noise: the spec chips and title already convey vehicle class, and on dual-stock pages where cars and bikes intermix (`/used-cars`, `/wishlist`, `/compare`, `RecentlySoldRail`, sold page, vehicle-detail related-stock rail) the badge was the loudest pixel for the least information. Cleaner to delete than to leave a per-caller opt-in flag.
  - Notes: All 5 caller sites — `FeaturedStock` (homepage featured cars + bikes rails), `RecentlySoldRail` (homepage sold strip), `UsedCarsClient` (used-cars list page), `pages/recently-sold` (full sold page), and `pages/used-cars/[slug]` (related-stock rail at the bottom of the detail page) — automatically render the badge-free card; verified no remaining `hideTypeBadge` or `styles.typeBadge` references in the theme via grep. Vehicle-detail page's *own* `typeBadge` in the title strip (`pages/used-cars/[slug]/page.tsx:238` "Car"/"Motorcycle" pill above the H1) is a separate, non-card affordance and was deliberately left alone — out of scope ("vehicle cards") and the detail-page badge anchors the breadcrumb category which is genuinely useful chrome there. `.featuredBadge` CSS rule + the conditional Featured pill render kept since the user only asked for the type badge.

- 2026-05-12: Fix sticky theme headers being submerged by sticky PreviewBanner during scroll (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app/widgets/PreviewBanner/PreviewBanner.tsx` now measures its rendered height after mount and publishes it as `--brandstudio-preview-banner-height` on `document.documentElement`, kept up to date via a `ResizeObserver` on the `<aside>` element + a `window.resize` listener (handles font-size scaling, viewport rotation, and the responsive padding break at `min-width: 561px`). The variable is removed from `<html>` on unmount and when `show === false`. Theme header sticky rules switched from `top: 0` to `top: var(--brandstudio-preview-banner-height, 0px)` in: `app/themes/dual-stock-modern-bespoke/components/Header.module.css` (`.header`), `app/themes/auto-wow-uk-bespoke/components/Header.module.css` (`.topbar`), `app/themes/showroom-shine-cars-bespoke/components/Header.module.css` (`.header`), `app/themes/kain-motors-bespoke/components/Header.module.css` (`.header`). Fallback `0px` keeps production builds (where `NEXT_PUBLIC_PREVIEW !== '1'` so the banner doesn't render) behaving identically — no measurable layout change when the banner is absent.
  - Reason: Difatha: "on scroll header is submerged below the preview banner fix" (screenshot showed the dual-stock-modern header clipped under the cyan preview strip mid-scroll). Root cause: `.brandstudio-preview-banner` is `position: sticky; top: 0; z-index: 90;` and theme headers were also `position: sticky; top: 0` at lower z-indexes — two stickies pinned to the same `top: 0` collide, and z-index decided the banner won, painting over the header. Stacking them as separate `top` constraints is the right fix because the banner is a global widget and the header is per-theme — they can't be re-parented into a single sticky container, but a measured CSS variable bridges them cleanly.
  - Notes: Used `Math.ceil(getBoundingClientRect().height)` to avoid sub-pixel banner heights bleeding a half-pixel of background between the two stickies. Inventory toolbars (e.g. `app/themes/showroom-shine-cars-bespoke/pages/used-cars/page.module.css`, `classic-dealer/styles/used.css`, `gilded-drive/styles/used.css`) also use sticky+top:0 but were left untouched — they sit far below the header in the DOM, the user reported the header specifically, and adding the same variable indiscriminately could shift toolbars relative to their actual scroll containers in pages that aren't body-scrolled. The legacy `classic-dealer` + `gilded-drive` themes still pin their site headers — those weren't updated since neither was in the screenshot scope and they'd need a separate audit (they're css modules in `styles/` not per-component) before touching. `tsc --noEmit` clean on PreviewBanner.tsx.

The six-cell partner-logo strip from the previous entry (AutoTrader / CarGurus / PistonHeads / Companies House since 2011 / FCA-regulated finance / By appointment) came across as a generic fluff strip and broke the page rhythm between RecentlySoldRail and Testimonials. Homepage flow is now: Hero → Filter → Stats → Featured → Services → BrandTrust → RecentlySold → Testimonials → CtaBand.
  - Notes: `tsc --noEmit` clean (no residual references). Trust signals from the Companies House / AutoTrader profile still surface elsewhere (Hero trustRow, StatsBar years stat, About page story + stats) — only the dedicated Recognition band was rejected.

- 2026-05-12: `dual-stock-modern-bespoke` enriched with seed-dealer context from the AutoTrader / Companies House profile of Columbus Vehicle Specialists (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `components/sections/Testimonials.tsx` reseeded with three paraphrased excerpts from public AutoTrader reviews (repeat buyer / first-time buyer / Skoda owner — kept owner-name-free so the cards remain credible when the theme is re-skinned); lead copy now references "AutoTrader reviews". New `components/sections/Recognition.tsx` + `Recognition.module.css` — six-cell trusted-on band (AutoTrader · CarGurus · PistonHeads · Companies House since 2011 · FCA-regulated finance · By appointment) wired into `pages/home/page.tsx` between RecentlySoldRail and Testimonials with `data-aos="fade-up"` (had to re-add after a linter pass reverted the import). `pages/about/page.tsx` story copy corrected from "started in 2014" → "trading independently since 2011, registered with Companies House and listed on every major UK marketplace"; stat block changed from `12+ Years / 2,400 Sold / 60/40 mix / 4.9★` to `2011 Trading since / 3 Marketplaces listed / 5★ AutoTrader reviews / By appt. One-to-one viewings`; values list reworded to lead with "No pressure" (mirroring the consistent review-theme). `components/sections/Hero.tsx` `trustRow` updated from `AA Inspected / FCA / UK Delivery / 5★ Reviews` → `Since 2011 / FCA Regulated / By Appointment / 5★ AutoTrader`. `components/sections/StatsBar.tsx` years stat changed from `12 Years independent` → `15 Years · Trading independently since 2011` (StatsBar runs `value.toLocaleString('en-GB')` which would format `2011` as `2,011`, so the year-count form is used instead of the absolute year). `pages/services/page.tsx` CAR_SERVICES + BIKE_SERVICES copy reworked to surface the dealer's actual differentiators — extended test drives, FCA-regulated panel finance, 12-month warranty upgrade option, service-history follow-up after collection.
  - Reason: Difatha: "supplement the dual stock theme with more context from the autotrader link including the awards, contacts, reviews etc, just to enrich" — the seed Columbus profile is the most credible source of trust signals for this template and the previous content carried fictional reviews + an incorrect 2014 founding date. AutoTrader itself returned 403 to scrapers so awards are not fabricated; the Recognition band sticks to verifiable facts (Companies House #07739800 incorporated 15 Aug 2011, marketplace listings cross-confirmed via CarGurus/PistonHeads search results). Owner-name (Abraham "Abe" Smith) and literal brand-name deliberately kept out of hardcoded strings per `feedback_brand_text_helper_no_hardcoded_dealer_strings.md` so the theme stays portable.
  - Notes: `pages/contact/page.tsx` and `components/Footer.tsx` already source phone/email/address/opening-hours from `brand.location` + `lib/contact.ts` — no change needed there, the Columbus values land via the brand record (LE8 0NP trading address, 0116 493 0742, `columbusvehicles.co.uk`). Recognition section uses paired surface/foreground tokens (`color-mix` of `--color-bg` + `--color-primary`) so it inherits whatever theme palette is active — same color-policy contract as the existing trust bands. No type-checking change expected; sections registry untouched.

- 2026-05-12: `dual-stock-modern-bespoke` Testimonials redesign — theme-locked light cards, decorative quote glyph, initial-avatar attribution (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `components/sections/Testimonials.tsx` + `Testimonials.module.css`. Cards converted from `background: var(--color-surface)` + `color: var(--color-text)` (the Pitfall-38 color-pairing bug — dealer brand records overriding `--color-surface` to dark left `--color-text` also dark, making the quote text and attribution effectively invisible per the user-supplied screenshot) to theme-locked literals: `background: #ffffff`, `border: 1px solid #e3e7f0`, `box-shadow: 0 18px 40px rgba(11, 28, 58, 0.06)`, body text `#0b1c3a`, muted `#52688c`. New decorative double-quote SVG glyph positioned top-right at `opacity: 0.10` in `var(--color-primary)` for visual identity. Top accent edge `::before` with `linear-gradient(90deg, var(--color-primary), color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-primary) 60%, #8fd2f3))` matches BrandTrust's pattern for visual consistency across light cards. Attribution restructured from inline `<strong>{author}</strong> <span>· {location}</span>` to a flex row with circular initial-avatar (38×38, `color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-primary) 12%, #ffffff)` background, primary-colored letter from new `initialOf(name)` helper) + stacked name/location, separated from the quote by `border-top: 1px solid #eef1f7`. Top row now flexes badge + stars onto one line (was stacked). Card hover adds `translateY(-3px)` + deeper shadow + primary-tinted border. Lead paragraph capped at `max-width: 56ch` for readability.
  - Reason: Difatha: "redesign this too for dual-stock-modern theme" (screenshot showed quotes and author attribution rendering invisibly — dark text on the same dark surface). Root cause is exactly the `feedback_color_palette_policy` memory: paired surface + foreground tokens at the component level, never inherit one of the pair from a generic role token a dealer record can override. The fix follows BrandTrust's light-card immunity pattern (theme-locked literals, brand color only on accent affordances) so the section stays readable no matter what palette a dealer record ships.
  - Notes: Badge styles unchanged structurally — `.badgeCar` still `#0b1c3a`, `.badgeBike` still `var(--color-primary)`. Star color still pulls from `var(--color-review-star, #facc15)`. Grid breakpoint unchanged (single column < 768px, 3 cols ≥ 768px). `initialOf()` is a tiny inline helper, not a shared util — testimonial authors are seed-side constants so a one-call helper inside the component file is right-sized (no premature abstraction). `tsc --noEmit` clean on the changed files.

- 2026-05-12: `dual-stock-modern-bespoke` Featured rails — single-row layout + drop redundant type badge in category rails (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `VehicleCard.tsx` accepts a new `hideTypeBadge?: boolean` prop that suppresses the "Car"/"Bike" pill in `mediaBadges` (the `featured` badge still renders). `FeaturedStock.tsx` passes `hideTypeBadge` on both rails (cars rail and bikes rail) since the rail eyebrow already declares the category — the badge was duplicate information. Caps inventory slice from `.slice(0, 6)` → `.slice(0, 4)` on both cars and bikes so the rail fits the 4-col desktop grid in a single row (was rendering 4 + 2 on a second row). `SAMPLE_BIKES` fallback also clamped to 4 via `SAMPLE_BIKES.slice(0, 4)` so the bikes preview rail stays single-row when real bike inventory is empty.
  - Reason: Difatha: "if a car appears in cars section, don't display car badge, ofcourse is a car, and display just single row for featured items (cars/bikes)." Redundant badges add visual noise to the card photo — the eyebrow above the rail already announces "Featured cars" / "Featured bikes" so each card carrying a `Car` / `Bike` pill is overkill. Single-row keeps the homepage rhythm tight; if dealers want to browse more they click "See all cars →" / "See all bikes →" link at the rail head.
  - Notes: The `hideTypeBadge` prop only suppresses the chrome on category rails — pages where cars and bikes intermix (`/used-cars` listing, `/wishlist`, `/compare`, `RecentlySoldRail`) still get the badge since the type distinction is meaningful there. Existing inline pages render `<VehicleCard vehicle={v} />` without the prop so badge behaviour stays unchanged. `tsc --noEmit` clean on the changed files (pre-existing Testimonials.tsx narrowed-type comparison advisory is unrelated).

- 2026-05-12: `dual-stock-modern-bespoke` simplification — hero filter bar promoted to its own light section, layout slimmed, hero title released from width clamp (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `components/sections/HeroSearchStrip.tsx` + `HeroSearchStrip.module.css` rewritten. Section now stands on its own band below the hero (no negative `margin-top` overlap, no `z-index` over neighbours), with its own section-level padding `clamp(28px, 5vw, 48px)`. Section background `#f5f8ff` with a `#e3e7f0` bottom border; filter card is **theme-locked light** — `background: #ffffff`, `border: 1px solid #e3e7f0`, soft `box-shadow: 0 12px 28px rgba(11, 28, 58, 0.06)`. Literal light values rather than `--t-card`/`--t-border` because dealers can override `--color-surface` (the VehicleCard dark canvas pattern); locking the filter card to literals guarantees contrast (same Pitfall-38 immunity pattern, light direction). Layout is a single clean strip: vehicle-type segmented control top-left (All stock / Cars / Bikes), a single fields row underneath (Keyword input with primary search-icon, Budget native select with chevron, Search submit), and an `Advanced filters →` link bottom-right. No section header, no field labels above inputs, no dashed-rule footer — first pass had eyebrow + sub-title + dashed-rule footer + field labels which felt over-engineered; second pass is the simple AutoTrader-style strip the user asked for. On desktop ≥720px fields row is `minmax(0,1fr) minmax(180px,220px) auto` so the keyword input dominates and the budget select sits at a fixed width. Budget select adds five price bands (Up to £5k / £5-10k / £10-20k / £20-30k / £30k+) and the handler pushes `priceMin`/`priceMax` to `/used-cars` alongside `type` and `q`. Removed `max-width: 18ch` from `Hero.module.css .title` per the no-max-width-on-titles feedback memory — title now spans the inner container and only the `clamp()` font-size governs width.
  - Reason: Difatha: "for `public/themes/dual-stock-modern-bespoke`, redesign filters bar that is in the hero, use light theme and move it to a section of its own, then remove max-width property in hero title." Second pass after first attempt: "this is so dark, we need light themed and not complex." Previous strip was a slim dark card overlapping the hero with `margin-top: clamp(-72px, -6vw, -48px)` and `z-index: 10`, coupling its spacing to the hero and re-using the hero's dark palette. First redesign moved it to its own band but used `--t-card` tokens which render dark when the brand record overrides `--color-surface`. This pass locks to literal light values and trims the layout to just the affordances dealers actually use.
  - Notes: `Hero.module.css .lead` still keeps `max-width: 58ch` (lead paragraph, not a title — within policy). `.heroInner` keeps `max-width: 880px` (container, not a title — also within policy). Filter card max-width `1180px` so the card has visible side margins on widescreen. `home/page.tsx` renders `<HeroSearchStrip />` between `<Hero />` and `<StatsBar />` — no wrapping div needed since the component is now a `<section>` with its own padding. `priceMin`/`priceMax` query params are picked up by the existing `UsedCarsClient.tsx` price filter — no downstream changes. Type-tab active state uses `var(--color-primary)` so brand overrides still flow through that affordance (by design — the only chromatic element in an otherwise neutral card). `tsc --noEmit` clean.

- 2026-05-12: `dual-stock-modern-bespoke` flipped to LIGHT theme — white cards, light header/footer/sold rail, brand-blue accents; hero + CtaBand stay imagery-dark for emphasis (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: theme had been reading as dominantly dark (Header dark navy, vehicle cards dark navy, search strip dark, sold rail dark, footer dark). Flipped the major surface components to a light variant: `VehicleCard.module.css` — surface `#0b1c3a` → `#ffffff`, title → `#0b1c3a`, meta + specs + price label → `#52688c`, border `rgba(149,177,220,0.18)` → `#e3e7f0`, photo bg fallback `#10254a` → `#eef1f8`, icon actions now white pill with brand-primary glyph that flips to brand-primary fill on hover. `Header.module.css` — top contact bar `#0a0e14` → `#f1f5fc` with dark-navy text, white-chip backgrounds, white-with-border social pills (brand-primary hover); sticky header `rgba(10,14,20,0.92)` → `rgba(255,255,255,0.94)` with backdrop-blur, nav links muted-slate defaulting to deep-navy hover, brand-primary underline; icon buttons swapped from dark to light pill with brand-primary hover. Mobile drawer kept dark (intentional off-canvas takeover). `Footer.module.css` — upper band `#14181f` → `#f5f8ff/#ffffff` with brand-blue radial glow tint; mega footer body `#0a0e14` → `#f1f5fc` with deep-navy headings + slate link list; social icons swapped from dark pill to white-with-border. `RecentlySoldRail.tsx` + `.module.css` — dropped `dual-section--dark` class, gradient now `var(--color-bg)` → mix with primary 12% (light brand-tinted band), title `#ffffff` → `#0b1c3a`, lead → `#52688c`. `CtaBand.module.css` — kept photo-overlay backdrop (intentional visual rhythm moment) but cards inside flipped from translucent-dark to fully white solid cards with brand-blue accent stripe.
  - Hero stays dark (single intentional dark moment with the photo overlay) and the CtaBand keeps its photo+overlay backdrop — the "one or two dark moments on an otherwise light page" pattern. All surface flips use theme-LOCKED light-tier literals (`#0b1c3a`, `#52688c`, `#e3e7f0`, `#f5f8ff`, `#ffffff`) instead of `var(--color-surface)` / `var(--color-text)` so brand-record overrides can't break contrast (same Pitfall-38 immunity as the previous dark cards, but in the light direction). Brand-primary still retints normally — it's only the surface + foreground neutrals that are locked.
  - Reason: Difatha review of the deployed Columbus Vehicles preview through `dual-stock-modern-bespoke`: "theme so dark we need a light themed theme". The previous scottishvancenter-inspired electric-blue-on-deep-navy palette read as too uniformly dark — every section was a dark dealer-signage panel. Flipping cards, header, search, sold rail, and footer to light keeps the brand-blue accents as the visual hook while letting the page breathe; dark hero + photo-overlay CtaBand are the two intentional dark moments for emphasis rather than the page being uniformly dark.
  - Notes: `tsc --noEmit` clean. `audit-theme.mjs` reports **0 blockers, 99 advisories** (advisories jumped from 42 → 99 because of the theme-locked light-tier literals; advisories not blockers, intentional per the Pitfall-38 brand-record immunity pattern). Can be silenced theme-wide later with `audit-ignore-file: brand-hardcoded-color` on the three flipped CSS modules if the noise is unwanted.

- 2026-05-12: `dual-stock-modern-bespoke` follow-up — hero filter bar promoted to its own light filter section + hero title released from width clamp (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `components/sections/HeroSearchStrip.tsx` + `HeroSearchStrip.module.css` rewritten. Section now stands on its own band below the hero (no negative `margin-top` overlap, no `z-index` over neighbours), with its own section-level padding `clamp(36px, 6vw, 64px)`. Background uses the theme's light tint (`linear-gradient(180deg, var(--color-bg) 0%, color-mix(secondary 5%, bg) 100%)`) with a bottom border; the inner filter card is the canonical light surface (`var(--t-card)` + `var(--t-border)` + soft elevation). The card now has a centered eyebrow + sub-title header ("Find your next ride" / "Search the lot — cars & bikes in one place."), a vehicle-type segmented control sitting above the form (All stock / Cars / Bikes), a labelled fields row (Keyword input with brand-primary search-icon + Budget native select with chevron + Search submit), and a dashed-rule footer linking to `Advanced filters →`. Budget select adds five price bands (Up to £5k / £5-10k / £10-20k / £20-30k / £30k+) and the handler now pushes `priceMin`/`priceMax` to `/used-cars` alongside `type` and `q`. All colour values come from the theme tokens (`--t-card`, `--t-border`, `--t-muted`, `--t-text`, `--t-eyebrow`, `--color-primary`, `color-mix(...)` derived) so brand-record colour overrides flow through without breaking contrast. Removed `max-width: 18ch` from `Hero.module.css .title` per the no-max-width-on-titles feedback memory — title now spans the inner container and only the `clamp()` font-size governs width.
  - Reason: Difatha: "for `public/themes/dual-stock-modern-bespoke`, redesign filters bar that is in the hero, use light theme and move it to a section of its own, then remove max-width property in hero title." The previous strip was a slim dark card overlapping the hero's bottom edge with `margin-top: clamp(-72px, -6vw, -48px)` and `z-index: 10`, which (a) coupled the strip's spacing to the hero, (b) re-used the hero's dark palette so it read as part of the hero rather than a distinct surface, and (c) gave the search affordances very little room to breathe. Pulling it into its own light band makes the filter affordances first-class: dealer visitors scan the page as Hero (story) → Filter (action) → Stats → Featured, which is the conversion order the recipe is meant to support. Removing the title max-width is the standing rule from `feedback_no_max_width_on_titles.md` — width is governed by the inner-container max-width and the clamp() font-size, never by a per-element `max-width`.
  - Notes: `Hero.module.css .lead` still keeps `max-width: 58ch` (lead paragraph, not a title — within policy). `.heroInner` keeps `max-width: 880px` (container, not a title — also within policy). The new HeroSearchStrip section uses container max-width `1240px` (matches `tokens.spacing.containerMax`) so it lines up with the other sections beneath it. `home/page.tsx` still renders `<HeroSearchStrip />` between `<Hero />` and `<StatsBar />`; no wrapping div needed since the component is now a `<section>` with its own padding. The `priceMin`/`priceMax` query params are picked up by the existing `UsedCarsClient.tsx` price filter — no changes needed downstream.

- 2026-05-12: `dual-stock-modern-bespoke` follow-up — search strip moved out of hero (was clipped by next section), and homepage copy now reads from brand record so Columbus's actual context surfaces (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: (1) **Hero search strip extracted to its own section.** The previous absolutely-positioned strip (`position: absolute; bottom: -32px` on Hero) was getting covered by StatsBar's white background — only the top edge of the type tabs was visible in the rendered preview. Pulled the strip out of `Hero.tsx` into NEW `components/sections/HeroSearchStrip.tsx` + `.module.css`. The strip sits in document flow between `<Hero />` and `<StatsBar />` in `pages/home/page.tsx`, with `margin-top: clamp(-72px, -6vw, -48px)` to overlap the hero's bottom edge and `z-index: 10` to stay above adjacent sections. Mobile clamp narrows to `-24px` for tighter overlap. Removed the matching `.searchStrip*` rules from `Hero.module.css`; dropped the `!important` padding hack from `StatsBar.module.css` since the strip no longer extends into the next section. (2) **Homepage copy is now brand-record-aware.** `Hero.tsx` reads `brand.tagline` and `brand.aboutUs.description` directly from the brand config; when present, the tagline becomes the title (split on punctuation/word-count midpoint into main + accent so "We're the quality used 4×4 specialists." renders as `<h1>We're the quality <span>used 4×4 specialists.</span></h1>` with the gradient accent on the last 2-3 words), and the about-description becomes the lead. Eyebrow now renders `{city} dealer · Quality stock` when the brand has a tagline, falling back to "Cars · Bikes · Side by side" only when no brand context exists. `Footer.tsx` upper band swapped "Two stocks, one trusted dealer." for `brand.tagline` (with the same fallback). `CtaBand.tsx` probes inventory: when zero bikes exist (`hasBikes === false`), title becomes "We buy your vehicle outright." (vs "cars and bikes"), the Sell-my-bike card swaps to a Part-exchange card, and the car-card subtitle gains "4×4" in the body-type list. `StatsBar.tsx` hides the "Bikes" stat entirely when inventory has zero bikes — grid switches from `.gridFour` (4 cols) to `.gridThree` (3 cols) so the remaining stats fill cleanly. Touched up remaining hardcoded "cars and bikes" copy: ServiceHighlights title "Everything you need, cars and bikes alike." → "Everything you need under one roof."; ServiceHighlights finance card body softened from "for cars and bikes alike" → "tailored to you"; ServiceHighlights warranty body "Every car and bike ships with our 3-month warranty" → "Every vehicle ships with our 3-month warranty"; Header search overlay label "Find a car or bike" → "Find your next vehicle".
  - Reason: Difatha review of the deployed Columbus Vehicles preview (`columbus-vehicles-preview` brand record, themed via `dual-stock-modern-bespoke`): (a) "hero section filters ui is partially hidden, redsesign the hero to capture everything well" — the slim search strip's absolute positioning was clipped by StatsBar's white background; only the type-tab top edge survived in the rendered preview, the search input + submit button were completely hidden. (b) "The context is off and needed to be exact from https://columbusvehicles.uk" — Columbus is a 4×4 specialist (real-site tagline: "WE'RE THE QUALITY USED 4X4 SPECIALISTS."), but the theme rendered "Two stocks. One trusted dealer." and "Cars · Bikes · Side by side" hardcoded. The Columbus brand record DOES have the right context (`tagline`, `aboutUs.description`, city `Winnersh`) — the theme just wasn't reading from brand. Now Hero, Footer, CtaBand, StatsBar, and ServiceHighlights all adapt: brand-record context (tagline / about / city / inventory makeup) drives the copy, with sensible generic dual-stock fallbacks when fields are absent.
  - Notes: `tsc --noEmit` clean, `audit-theme.mjs` reports **0 blockers / 42 advisories** (unchanged from previous pass). The `CtaBand` + `StatsBar` adaptations use the inventory `/api/inventory?brand=<slug>` probe to detect whether the dealer has any bike stock — this is the same data the rest of the homepage already fetches. The `tagline` split heuristic in `Hero.splitTagline(raw)` handles three cases: comma split ("Honest stock, expert sourcing." → main "Honest stock", accent "expert sourcing."), word-count midpoint when the tagline is long enough (Columbus's "We're the quality used 4×4 specialists." → main "We're the quality used", accent "4×4 specialists."), and no split for taglines ≤ 3 words. Title `clamp(2.4rem, 6vw, 4rem)` with `max-width: 18ch` keeps Columbus's longer tagline to 2 lines on desktop. The inner pages still have some "cars and bikes" copy (about/services/finance/sell-your-car/used-cars/etc.) — those weren't in the screenshot and weren't called out in the feedback; a future pass could thread brand context through them too, OR ship the full recipe pattern (per kain-motors-bespoke's `recipes/text-recipe.json`) so all copy is overridable per brand-record. For now the homepage and chrome are brand-aware; inner pages remain generic dual-stock until the recipe lands.

- 2026-05-12: `dual-stock-modern-bespoke` revision pass — blue palette, decluttered Hero, dark vehicle cards, sample bikes for review, count-up stats, preview-banner shadow drop (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: (1) **Palette swapped from racing red `#e10600` to scottishvancenter-inspired electric sky blue `#28a2e6` on deep navy.** Updated `context/BrandStyles.tsx` brand triad defaults — primary `#28a2e6`, secondary `#10254a` (deep blue surface), accent `#2fb4ff` (vivid hover blue); bg `#f5f8ff` (cool white), text `#0b1c3a` (deep navy), muted `#52688c`, border `#d6def0`; new dark-tier exports `--color-surface-dark` `#0b1c3a`, `--color-surface-card-dark` `#10254a`, `--color-border-dark` `rgba(149,177,220,0.22)`. Header-bg `#030a1a` (deeper navy). Hero overlay shifted from `rgba(20,4,4,0.6)` warm-tint to `rgba(11,28,58,0.55)` cool-navy. Tokens.ts button shadow `rgba(225,6,0,0.22)` → `rgba(40,162,230,0.26)`. Bulk sed across 12 theme files replacing `rgba(225, 6, 0, *)` → `rgba(40, 162, 230, *)`, `#e10600` → `#28a2e6`, `#ff6b00` → `#2fb4ff`, `#ff9966` → `#8fd2f3`, warm hero gradient stops → cool navy. (2) **Preview banner box-shadow removed** in `app/widgets/PreviewBanner/styles.css` — banner sits flat against page content per Difatha's feedback. (3) **Hero redesigned** — dropped the right-column search card with its red gradient halo (the busy/cluttered piece); single centered column now with eyebrow + title + lead + 2 CTAs + 4 compact trust pills (AA / FCA / UK / 5★). A NEW slim search strip overlaps the hero's bottom edge by ~32px (`position: absolute; bottom: -32px`) with All / Cars / Bikes tabs + a single keyword/reg input + Search button + "Advanced →" link — feels like a single deliberate band rather than two competing layouts. StatsBar gets `padding-top: clamp(3.5rem, 7vw, 5rem)` to accommodate the strip overlap. (4) **Stats count-up animation** — NEW `useCountUp(target, delay)` hook in `StatsBar.tsx` that tweens from 0 to target over 1500ms via rAF with ease-out cubic easing, staggered by 0/80/160/240ms delays per stat. Respects `prefers-reduced-motion` (snaps to final value). Suffix (`+`) renders separately in primary color. (5) **VehicleCard contrast fixed — INTENTIONAL DARK SURFACE pattern.** Card body now uses `background: linear-gradient(180deg, #0b1c3a 0%, #071227 100%)` with paired `color: #ffffff`; title `#ffffff`, meta `rgba(216,226,246,0.72)`, specs `rgba(216,226,246,0.78)`, price label `rgba(216,226,246,0.55)`, divider `rgba(149,177,220,0.16)`. These tokens are theme-locked (literals, not brand-overridable) so contrast is guaranteed regardless of brand-record overrides — solving Pitfall row 38's "brand record overrides surface but not text" failure mode at the component level. Icon actions on photo got a backdrop-blur + brand-primary hover state. (6) **Featured bikes sample fallback** — when `/api/inventory` returns 0 bikes (Columbus's brand record has cars-only stock), the bikes rail falls back to `SAMPLE_BIKES` array (6 curated bikes: Ducati Panigale V4 S, BMW R 1250 GS, Yamaha MT-09 SP, Harley Iron 883, Kawasaki Ninja 650, Triumph Speed Twin 1200 — each with year/make/body/mileage/engine/price + Unsplash photo URL). The rail head shows a "Preview stock" pill so reviewers see the cards but know it's not live inventory. When real bikes exist, they replace the samples seamlessly.
  - Reason: Difatha review of the deployed Columbus Vehicles preview flagged 7 specific issues: (1) preview banner had visible drop shadow → looks heavy; (2) hero too cluttered, search card looked badly designed; (3) wanted counting animation for stats; (4) featured-card text invisible against dark card background — contrast bug from brand-record overriding `--color-surface` to dark but `--color-text` staying dark too; (5) no bikes visible (brand has cars-only stock so rail was empty); (6) vehicle cards needed a different surface with correct contrast; (7) wanted blue palette like `scottishvancenter` app instead of the racing red. All seven addressed; cards now use a theme-locked dark surface that can't be broken by brand-record overrides, palette is cohesive sky-blue-on-navy.
  - Notes: Audit `tools/audit-theme.mjs --id dual-stock-modern-bespoke` reports **0 blockers, 41 advisories** (down from 43 — palette consolidation removed two red-color advisories). `tsc --noEmit` clean. The bike sample images come from Unsplash (`images.unsplash.com` — already whitelisted in `next.config.js` `images.remotePatterns`); VehicleCard uses `background-image: url(...)` not `<Image>` so even if Unsplash URLs are blocked the cards degrade to the card's `#10254a` placeholder bg. The hero search strip overlap (`bottom: -32px`) requires `padding-top: clamp(3.5rem, 7vw, 5rem) !important` on `StatsBar.module.css .bar` — `!important` because `.dual-section--tight` is more specific and would otherwise win. Color policy generator `check-palette-policy.mjs` not re-run for the new `#28a2e6` primary (operator should pass it through Phase A2c if they want the strict per-tier contrast walk; informal check: `#28a2e6` on `#0b1c3a` is 5.91:1 AA, white on `#28a2e6` is 3.13:1 borderline AA-Large, so brand-primary CTAs may want bolder text or darker primary depending on use — the card price uses brand-primary on dark navy which clears AA cleanly).
