- 2026-05-11: /new-theme SKILL — three new Quality Bar rules + Pitfalls 35/36/37 from auto-wow preview review (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `.claude/skills/new-theme/SKILL.md` — three new Quality Bar must-have sections: §"Hero title sizing — fit-in-2-lines max", §"Top contact bar — required composition" (PreviewBanner above + location chip + live-stock pulsing chip + social icons from `brand.socialLinks` + phone CTA in canonical order), §"Brand-uploaded images must render — `var(--brand-image-*)` plumbing" (every Hero/PageHero/CtaBanner/decorative image must consume the CSS var, never hardcode paths — dashboard uploads silently drop otherwise). Three new Pitfall rows (35, 36, 37) documenting the failure modes from auto-wow preview review. Three new feedback memories under `.claude/projects/.../memory/` (hero title clumpsy, topbar essentials, brand image plumbing) so cross-session work picks up the rules.
  - Reason: Difatha review of an auto-wow preview screenshot — "the hero title is so big in large screen spanning multiple rows, too big", "forgetting to add preview banner at the top of topbar and also no social icons in the top bar, these are some important sub components missing", "when we have images uploaded through brand studio, they should be visible". All three issues are pattern-failures that future themes will repeat without explicit SKILL rules.
  - Applied: documented the rules in SKILL.md Quality Bar (where Phase 8 reads them) + Pitfalls catalogue (where pattern-recognition lives) + feedback memories (where cross-session continuity lives). Each rule names the wrong pattern explicitly so future-Claude recognises it. Audit rules `lib-hero-title-too-big` and `lib-image-not-brand-driven` documented as deferred — SKILL clauses are the contract until they ship.
  - Notes: No code changes — this is documentation/policy only. The auto-wow-uk-bespoke Hero still uses `clamp(2.8rem, 6.4vw, 5.6rem)` which violates the new rule; deferred to a follow-up alongside applying the topbar social-icons + image-plumbing patterns. The infrastructure (`brand.socialLinks` shape, `--brand-image-*` CSS vars, PreviewBanner widget) already exists — what was missing was the SKILL.md prose telling Phase 8 to USE them.

- 2026-05-11: Promote PreviewBanner to a brandstudio-global widget (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: new `app/widgets/PreviewBanner/{PreviewBanner.tsx, styles.css, index.ts}` — single brand-token-driven implementation that themes consume with `<PreviewBanner brand={brand} />`. Replaces the per-theme `components/PreviewBanner.tsx` pattern used by older themes (classic-dealer / gilded-drive / springalls-classic). Reads `NEXT_PUBLIC_PREVIEW === '1'` to gate display; renders null in production (safe to always mount). Optional `force={true|false}` prop overrides env-var check for dev. Sticky-top, mobile-first responsive, pulsing dot indicator (animation skipped under `prefers-reduced-motion`). `tools/scaffold-theme-skeleton.mjs` Shell stub now imports and mounts `<PreviewBanner brand={brand} />` BEFORE `<Header />` by default so every future theme inherits it. `app/themes/auto-wow-uk-bespoke/components/Shell.tsx` updated to mount it as the reference implementation. SKILL.md `Required widgets` section expanded with the contract + Pitfall row 34 + Tested defaults updated. Memory pointer at `reference_new_theme_skill_docs.md` updated to list the now-7 brandstudio-global widgets.
  - Reason: Difatha caught a gap during the SKILL audit: "forgot to add preview banner for skill new theme". The PreviewBanner existed as a per-theme component in older themes but was pruned by the skeleton scaffolder, and Phase 8 was skipping it consistently because it felt like a "preview-deploy concern, not a theme concern". Result: every new bespoke theme (columbus, ele, auto-wow) shipped without the strip that tells dealers/prospects "this is a preview, not the live site" — which led to dealers sharing preview URLs as if they were production.
  - Applied: Built the global widget with brand-token-driven styling (`var(--color-primary)` background, `#ffffff` text, white pulsing dot) so it retints per brand automatically. Two render modes: env-gated (default — only shows when `NEXT_PUBLIC_PREVIEW=1`) or `force`-overridden (dev-only). Sticky-top + flex-column on mobile, flex-row on desktop (min-width 561px breakpoint). All wrapped in `.brandstudio-preview-banner-*` class prefix so the CSS doesn't conflict with theme-internal classes.
  - Verified: tsc clean against baseline. Audit `0 blockers / 46 advisories` (the +3 vs. the previous run come from the existing kept-inventory-page advisories surfacing more consistently — PreviewBanner itself is in `app/widgets/` so it's not theme-scanned). theme:sync clean. Memory reference updated.
  - Notes: Older themes (classic-dealer, gilded-drive, springalls-classic) still ship their per-theme `components/PreviewBanner.tsx` files — those keep working but new themes should NOT replicate the pattern. Migration of older themes to the global widget is deferred work; they're functionally equivalent.

- 2026-05-11: Motion & light language — new MotionFX + ScrollProgress widgets, expanded AnimateOnScroll, SKILL Quality Bar + audit rules (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: new `app/widgets/MotionFX/{MotionFX.tsx, styles.css, index.ts}` (CSS-only neon-keyframe library with 12 utility classes — `.mfx-glow-pulse`, `.mfx-glow-orbit`, `.mfx-pulse-dot`, `.mfx-shimmer` / `.mfx-shimmer-loop`, `.mfx-scan`, `.mfx-text-glow`, `.mfx-border-glow`, `.mfx-float`, `.mfx-float-large`, `.mfx-tilt`, `.mfx-grid-drift`, `.mfx-rotate-slow`, `.mfx-fade-loop`); new `app/widgets/ScrollProgress/{ScrollProgress.tsx, index.ts}` (rAF-throttled scroll-tied progress driver writing `--mfx-progress` 0→1 onto `[data-mfx-scroll]` elements with built-in `parallax-slow|medium|fast` + `fade-out-on-exit` + `blur-on-exit` + `zoom-on-enter` variants); `app/widgets/AnimateOnScroll/aos.css` expanded from 7 variants to 18 (added flip-up/down/left/right, slide-up/down, fade-up-right/up-left/down-right/down-left, zoom-in-up, zoom-out-down, blur-in) plus per-element `data-aos-duration` / `data-aos-easing` overrides; `tools/audit-theme.mjs` adds 3 new advisory rules (`motion-aos-min-count`, `motion-no-animated-glow` — theme-wide scan via `fsSync.readdirSync`, `motion-no-scroll-tied`); `tools/scaffold-theme-skeleton.mjs` Shell stub now mounts `<MotionFX />` + `<ScrollProgress />` alongside the existing `<AnimateOnScroll />` so every future theme inherits the motion plumbing; SKILL.md adds new Quality Bar §"Motion & light language — REQUIRED, not optional" with 6 concrete must-haves (animated glow blobs, pulse-dots on every status chip, 4+ AOS per page with varied variants, scroll-tied effects, shimmer on primary CTAs, text-glow on hero highlights), expands the "Required widgets" section to document the 18 AOS variants + MotionFX classes + ScrollProgress variants, adds Pitfall row 31 (themes feel "static and dead" — palette swap without motion), updates Pitfall row 11 (the Windows emoji-encoding guard) to note it's reapplied; `app/themes/auto-wow-uk-bespoke/components/{Shell, Hero, PageHero, CtaBanner, ServicesBand, Footer, LatestArrivals}.tsx` + matching .module.css files demonstrate the patterns (hero glow + orbit + grid-drift + text-glow on title highlight + shimmer on primary CTA + parallax-slow on background image; CtaBanner zoom-on-enter scroll + glow-pulse + text-glow + shimmer; ServicesBand + Footer animated glows; LatestArrivals pulse-dot on Available chip with green `--mfx-dot-color` override, shimmer-loop on Featured chip; AOS sprinkled across 12 page bodies with varied variants).
  - Reason: Difatha feedback after the auto-wow-uk-bespoke first build — "themes lacking neon lighting designs with animations and entry and scroll animations to make themes lively, implement rules and policies to capture such e.g. use https://cdnjs.com/libraries/aos etc, we need more lively themes". Static radial-gradient glows + 0-3 data-aos attrs per page made every theme feel inert on real devices regardless of how well the static design landed.
  - Applied: Built the MotionFX widget as a CSS-injector server component (zero runtime JS overhead; the 12 utility classes + 9 keyframes are pure CSS) so themes consume motion by adding className strings, not by importing components. Built ScrollProgress as a rAF-throttled client widget that observes `[data-mfx-scroll]` elements and writes a CSS variable — themes consume the variable via the built-in utility variants OR directly in their own CSS modules. Expanded AnimateOnScroll's CSS variants to match the AOS library's naming so the data-aos contract is closer to the de-facto standard (still our own implementation, but the contract is interchangeable). New audit rule `motion-no-animated-glow` does a theme-wide directory walk (sync fs) checking every .tsx/.css for any of: `mfx-*` references, `data-mfx-scroll` attributes, or `@keyframes` definitions — fires only if the entire theme is motion-free. New rule `motion-aos-min-count` requires 4+ AOS attrs on the homepage and 2+ on inner pages (kept inventory pages excluded). New rule `motion-no-scroll-tied` requires at least one `data-mfx-scroll` on the homepage.
  - Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean against baseline. Audit `0 blockers / 42 advisories` on auto-wow-uk-bespoke (down 11 from 53 once motion attributes were sprinkled in — the new motion rules don't fire any more). Theme:sync clean (6 themes registered). All MotionFX classes honour `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`; ScrollProgress no-ops entirely when reduced-motion is set.
  - Notes: The 12 utility classes are intentionally additive — themes apply them alongside their CSS-module classes (e.g. `className={[styles.primaryCta, 'mfx-shimmer'].join(' ')}`). All animations are brand-token-driven so the same markup retints per brand automatically; `.mfx-pulse-dot` accepts a `--mfx-dot-color` CSS var for semantic-color overrides (e.g. green for "Available" status). The skeleton scaffolder's Shell stub wires the three widgets so Phase 8 designers only sprinkle attributes — no motion plumbing per theme.

- 2026-05-11: Port `@carous/sell-your-car` widget to brandstudio + mount in ELE theme (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: new `app/widgets/SellYourCarWidget/` (7 files — `SellYourCarWidget.tsx`, `DefaultInfoPanel.tsx`, `types.ts`, `format.ts`, `lookup.ts`, `payload.ts`, `styles.css` ~910 lines, `index.ts`); new `app/api/lookup/route.ts` (POST handler for vehicle lookup with synthesized-fallback for prospect previews); new `app/themes/ele-car-sales-bespoke/pages/sell-your-car/SellYourCarMount.tsx` (client island); replaced `app/themes/ele-car-sales-bespoke/pages/sell-your-car/page.tsx` to mount the widget via the island and deleted the hand-rolled `ValuationFormIsland.tsx` + per-theme `page.module.css`; SKILL.md §"Required widgets" expanded with the widget contract + Pitfalls row 28.
  - Reason: Difatha clarification — "the sell your car page should be exactly like used in carous-platform apps" (huntsmotors et al). The skill was generating per-theme single-step valuation forms instead of mounting the carous-platform monorepo's `@carous/sell-your-car` 3-step wizard (Identify → Valuation → Details), missing the UK plate flag, vehicle lookup, guide trade-price reveal, and contact panel that buyers expect.
  - Applied (widget port): Copied `types.ts` / `format.ts` / `lookup.ts` / `DefaultInfoPanel.tsx` / `styles.css` verbatim from `F:/projects/carous-platform/packages/sell-your-car/src/`. Rewrote `payload.ts` to use a local `SellYourCarLeadMeta` type instead of `@carous/hooks`'s `LeadFormMeta`. Rewrote `SellYourCarWidget.tsx` to import brandstudio's `useLeadsForm` (slightly thinner meta shape — `buildPayload` callback now enriches meta with `leadType` / `leadSource` / `leadOwner` from props); `onSubmitSuccess` callback receives `undefined` since brandstudio's submit doesn't expose the response body.
  - Applied (API route): `/api/lookup` POST accepts `{reg, mileage}`, tries the existing `/api/vehicles/lookup?registration=...&mileage=...` GET, and falls back to a synthesized minimal vehicle record when the upstream lookup misses (which it will for most user-typed regs in a prospect-preview context — the dealer's MySQL inventory only contains their own stock). Synthesized response shape matches the widget's expected `{ vehicle, valuations: null }`, so the wizard advances to step 2 with "On request" as the trade price.
  - Applied (ELE mount): `pages/sell-your-car/page.tsx` is a clean Server Component composing `<PageHero>` + `<SellYourCarMount />`. The mount is a co-located client island that calls `useBrand()`, derives contact info via `getBrandContactInfo()`, and passes everything (brandName, infoPanel, contact, copy) to `<SellYourCarWidget />`. Pattern mirrors carous-platform/apps/huntsmotors/app/components/SellYourCarMount.tsx.
  - Applied (skill): SKILL §"Required widgets" now mandates mounting `<SellYourCarWidget />` for the sell-your-car page — Phase 8 must NOT hand-roll valuation forms. Pitfalls Catalogue row 28 documents the failure mode + fix.
  - Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean delta (zero new errors on widget, route, or theme); `audit-theme.mjs --id ele-car-sales-bespoke` → 0 blockers / 65 advisories (down 7 from 72 — the per-theme valuation form CSS had the hardcoded error/success hex codes that were flagged previously; removed with the file).
  - Notes: Posted to Slack channel `C0B2VGLD9K6` (claude-skills) with the widget contract.   - Reason: Earlier today's parity pass only touched `/update/<slug>` (templates/update.html + the `update_brand` route). Difatha flagged that `/create` (renders `templates/index.html`, posts to `/api/brands` → `create_brand`) was still on the old field set, which meant a freshly-created brand couldn't have its per-page images, structured address detail, extra colors, or per-page hero copy set until the dealer pivoted to the update page. The new BrandConfig surface area has to be in BOTH the creation and the edit forms or operators flip between pages.
  - Applied:
    - **`templates/index.html`**: identical changes to the ones added to `update.html` — new Page Images tab with 6 uploaders + URL-fallback per slot, Contact tab line1/line2/county fields with auto-joining full-address, Theme tab 3 extra swatches (border/muted/surface) that self-wire via the generic data-color JS, Content tab Page Heroes section with 8 page hero blocks (contact also gets a subtitle field).
    - **`app.py` `create_brand` route**: new `PAGE_IMAGE_SLOTS` constant + image-save loop after inventory upload; `brand['images']` layered from `load_preview(slug)` → URL fields → uploads after the FormHandler call; `images` and `themeId` added to `overwrite_keys`; the prior home-hero-only patch loop replaced with the full `PAGE_HERO_FIELD_MAP` covering 9 page keys (home/about/services/contact/used-cars/finance/sell-your-car/recently-sold/part-exchange).
  - Verified: Python `ast.parse` clean on `app.py`; Jinja `env.get_template('index.html')` and `env.get_template('update.html')` clean. Image slots audited against `BrandImageSlots` in `brands/types.ts` — `{hero, about, services, finance, partExchange, sellYourCar, recentlySold}` is the complete set; all 7 covered (hero via top-level `heroImage` field, 6 via the new tab). Theme components (Columbus, ELE) consume `--brand-image-*` CSS vars set by each theme's `BrandStyles.tsx` from `brand.images.*` — verified parity by grepping `var(--brand-image-...)` references.
  - Notes: The same `PAGE_HERO_FIELD_MAP` dict appears in both `create_brand` and `update_brand` routes. Inlined rather than module-level constant per the global "no premature abstraction" rule — 9 keys × 2 callsites = future-me has to update both, but a module-level constant for a 9-key dict reads as over-engineering for the gain. If a third route grows the same need, that's the trigger for promoting. The existing `email` (SMTP) and `api` (Ollama) blocks in `BrandConfig` are still not exposed in the editor; intentional — those are infrastructure config Carous sets, not dealer-facing knobs. "All configurables" in this context means all dealer-relevant fields, not the infrastructure plumbing FormHandler hardcodes.

- 2026-05-10: Update Brand editor — expose every BrandConfig configurable in the dashboard (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `templates/update.html` (new Page Images tab + 6 uploaders, Contact tab line1/line2/county fields, Theme tab 3 extra color swatches, Content tab Page Heroes section), `app.py` (`update_brand` route: 6 new image-file branches, `brand.images` propagation, per-page hero override loop, `images` added to `overwrite_keys`), `static/modules/form_handler.py` (FormHandler reads `address2`/`county` and `borderColor`/`mutedColor`/`surfaceColor` from form data and writes them into the canonical `location.address.{line1,line2,city,county,postcode}` and `theme.colors` blocks).
  - Reason: Previous fix exposed the underlying gap — `/new-theme` ships themes with empty `location.{city,postcode,email,phone}` and an `images` block the dashboard could not edit. Difatha's directive: "update brandstudio to have all theme configurables including images and all other configs." Editor schema parity is the priority so non-technical operators can complete a preview entirely through the dashboard rather than hand-editing brand JSON.
  - Applied:
    - **Page Images tab**: 6 uploaders + URL-fallback inputs for `about / services / finance / partExchange / sellYourCar / recentlySold`. Uploaded files land at `/public/images/<slug>-<slot>.<ext>` via the same `save_image_file()` helper used for logo/favicon/hero. Uploads win over URL inputs which win over previously-stored values.
    - **Contact tab**: 3 new fields — `address1`, `address2`, `county`. `address1` and `county` were already in the FormHandler's read list; `address2` is new. The legacy single-line `address` (display) input was kept but is no longer required-via-pattern: when blank, FormHandler joins the structured fields automatically.
    - **Theme Colors tab**: 3 new color swatch cards — `borderColor`, `mutedColor`, `surfaceColor`. The swatch JS uses generic `querySelectorAll('.color-swatch-card')` so no JS changes were needed; the new cards self-wire via `data-color="..."` and matching hidden-input/picker IDs.
    - **Page Content → Page Heroes section**: title + description inputs for `about / services / contact / used-cars / finance / sell-your-car / recently-sold / part-exchange`. Contact also gets a subtitle input. Server-side, `update_brand` loops a `PAGE_HERO_FIELD_MAP` after the legacy services-title block so an explicit `servicesHeroTitle` wins over the services-section title fallback.
    - **`update_brand` route**: new `PAGE_IMAGE_SLOTS` constant, file-by-file `save_image_file` calls inside an exception-guarded loop (matches the logo/favicon/heroImage pattern, returns `400 {error,details}` on per-slot save failure), `brand.images` built by layering existing → URL fields → uploads, `images` added to `overwrite_keys` so the merge keeps the layered set.
    - **`form_handler.py`**: `address_line2`, `county_value`, and `full_address_value` read from data with structured-field fallback; `border_color`, `muted_color`, `surface_color` read from data with theme-colors fallback; both written into the returned `BrandConfig` (location.address.line2/county, theme.colors.{borderColor, mutedColor, surfaceColor}).
  - Verified: Python `ast.parse` clean on `app.py` and `form_handler.py`; Jinja `env.get_template('update.html')` clean. Color-swatch JS is generic so the 3 new swatches auto-wire. File-upload JS (`initializeFileUpload`) iterates over all `.file-upload-area` elements so the 6 new uploaders auto-wire. The full-config JSON branch needs no extra handling — `deep_merge` covers any new fields. Smoke test on `columbus-vehicles-preview` requires Flask restart + hard-refresh of `/update/columbus-vehicles-preview` (the running process predates these changes).
  - Notes: When inserting the page-heroes loop, I deliberately ran it AFTER the legacy services-title block. The legacy block unconditionally overwrites `pages.services.hero.title` with the services-section title; running the override loop after means a dealer's explicit `servicesHeroTitle` always wins, while leaving the safe fallback for dealers who don't touch the field. The "Full Address" single-line input was demoted from "required pattern that must include city+postcode" to "display string; auto-joined from structured fields when blank" — small UX win for dealers who only fill the structured fields.

- 2026-05-10: Update Brand button — surface server validation `details` in the alert + log them server-side (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `templates/update.html` (JS submit handler), `app.py` (`update_brand` route — both the full-config and form-payload 400 branches).
  - Reason: After the previous fix (commit `d88a137`) cleared the silent native-validation block, the form began posting and the server returned `400 {"error":"Validation failed","details":[...]}`. The JS only displayed `result.error` ("Validation failed") and dropped `result.details`, so the user still saw a generic failure. For columbus-vehicles-preview specifically, the stored brand has empty `location.address.{city,postcode}`, `location.email`, `location.phone` — `validate_brand` correctly rejects, but the missing-field list never reached the alert. Same blindness server-side (Flask access log shows the 400 but no `[UPDATE]` line on validation paths).
  - Applied: JS handler joins `result.details` (array) or appends it (string) onto the alert; Python route prints `[UPDATE] Validation failed for <slug> (full config|form): [...]` before returning 400 in both code paths.
  - Verified: code-level only; restart Flask + hard-refresh `/update/<slug>` to pick up the changes. Next 400 will name the missing fields in the on-page alert and the Flask log.
  - Notes: This exposes — but does not fix — the underlying data gap that `/new-theme` produces (location fields blank). Theme-building scope per `36d482d` doesn't include preview metadata, so dealers need to fill phone/email/address/postcode in the editor before "Update Brand" will save. Worth deciding whether `/create` should require those fields up front, or whether the editor should mark them as required-on-save with inline highlighting.

- 2026-05-10: Added `ele-car-sales-bespoke` theme — bespoke for ELE Car Sales, Shotts, Lanarkshire (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app/themes/ele-car-sales-bespoke/*` (full theme contract — 39 scaffolded files + 35 Phase-8 redesigns/additions); `public/themes/ele-car-sales-bespoke/images/*` (7 archetype-default JPEGs); `tools/.theme-dna/ele-car-sales.json`; `tools/.logo-colors/ele-car-sales.json`; `tools/.theme-images/ele-car-sales-bespoke.json`; registries + `theme/theme-manifest.json` regenerated via `npm run theme:sync`.
  - Reason: Prospect preview for ELE Car Sales (used-car dealer, Shotts, North Lanarkshire). Generated via `/new-theme`. Logo monochrome (white wordmark on black), dealer site Cloudflare-blocked, Autotrader 403 — proceeded logo-only with user-supplied primary `#c8102e` (classic UK-dealer red) and location derived from the Autotrader URL hint. Modern archetype based on bold uppercase sans wordmark.
  - Notes: Fonts = DM Sans (heading) + Inter (body) via Google Fonts. Primary `#c8102e`, accent `#1a1a1a`, white-on-primary contrast 5.88:1 (AA). 4 of 7 image slots curated for modern, 3 fell back to classic archetype pool (about, sellYourCar, recentlySold) — flag in dashboard if operator wants to swap per-brand. Type-clean against the springalls-classic baseline (only residual error is the pre-existing `AppPageConfig` validator quirk affecting every theme's `pages/used-cars/[slug]/page.tsx`). Audit: 0 blockers / 61 advisories (kept-inventory CSS legacy hex codes, raw `<img>` in inventory pages, error/success color literals in form modules — all flagged for future hardening). No dealer-specific copy was fabricated; all phone/email/hours fall back to `getBrandContactInfo(brand)` with neutral placeholders.

- 2026-05-10: brandstudio — rip dead preview-coupling tools + slim env-check + rollback to theme-only scope (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: deleted `tools/register_preview_brand.py` (372 lines); deleted `tools/smoke-test-preview.mjs` (208 lines); slimmed `tools/check-skill-env.mjs` (248 → 119 lines); slimmed `tools/rollback-theme.mjs` (174 → ~125 lines, dropped `deleteBrandRow` + `--keep-brand` + `--brand-slug`); SKILL.md (deleted Appendix A, deleted Phase 10d, trimmed Phase 0.5 + Phase 7.5 references, updated Tool reference, annotated Pitfalls catalogue rows 1 + 11 with "tool removed" notes).
  - Reason: Difatha's directive: "yes rip any used codes". After hardening the skill scope to theme-only (commit `36d482d`), the brand-registration + preview-smoke-test tools had no callers on the canonical path. They were only referenced by Appendix A (which was itself a vestigial dev-only convenience). Since the dashboard's `/create` flow is the canonical preview-creation path and has its own implementation (Flask routes + `preview_store.PreviewStore` + `maybe_start_linux_brand_automation`), the skill-side CLI was duplicate functionality that had to be maintained without any user. Cutting it now while the boundary is fresh prevents drift.
  - Applied:
    - **Deleted** `tools/register_preview_brand.py` — was the skill's MySQL writer + automation invoker. Last caller (Appendix A) is now gone. Dashboard's `/create` covers the same flow with proper auth.
    - **Deleted** `tools/smoke-test-preview.mjs` — depended on a registered brand + running Next dev. Both were Appendix-A-only concerns. The 5 checks (`brand-record-fetchable`, `brand-themeId-correct`, `brand-images-populated`, `theme-folder-exists`, `preview-url-responds`) are no longer relevant for the theme-building skill — `audit-theme.mjs` covers theme-only correctness, and the dashboard handles preview verification.
    - **Slimmed** `tools/check-skill-env.mjs` — was 7 gates including `mysql-env`, `mysql-reachable`, `flask-running`, `lvhme-resolves` (all Appendix-A deps). Now 3 gates: `tools-present`, `node-deps` (sharp), `theme-sync-clean`. Halved the file.
    - **Slimmed** `tools/rollback-theme.mjs` — dropped `deleteBrandRow()` (no skill-created brand rows to delete anymore), `--keep-brand` flag, `--brand-slug` arg, and the embedded Python script that imported `preview_store`. Cleanup is now purely theme-files: `app/themes/<id>/`, `public/themes/<id>/`, the 3 cache JSONs under `tools/.theme-*`, then `theme:sync` rebuilds registries. Brand cleanup is now dashboard-only via `/update/<slug>`.
    - **SKILL.md**: deleted "Appendix A — OPTIONAL local preview" entirely (~85 lines); deleted "Phase 10d — End-to-end smoke test" entirely (~40 lines); trimmed Phase 0.5 description to match the new 3-gate env check; updated Phase 7.5 to drop the "consumed by register_preview_brand" mention; updated the Tool reference table (removed 2 deleted rows, updated check-skill-env / rollback-theme rows). Pitfalls catalogue rows 1 + 11 (which referenced register_preview_brand.py) preserved per the catalogue's "rows are institutional memory" rule, with inline annotations noting the tool was removed.
  - Verified: `node tools/check-skill-env.mjs` → all 3 gates green; `node tools/rollback-theme.mjs --theme-id nonexistent --dry-run` → 5 cleanup steps + theme-sync, no MySQL touch. `audit-theme.mjs` unchanged (still passes 0 blockers on columbus / springalls).
  - Notes: Net deletion ≈ 580 lines of code + ~165 lines of skill prose. The skill now has a single-purpose, no-side-effects shape: produce theme files → audit → ship via git. Operator's preview workflow (dashboard's `/create` form) is unchanged — it never depended on the deleted tools.

- 2026-05-10: /new-theme skill — clarify scope to "theme building only"; demote Phase 9.5 + Phase 10d to Appendix A (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `.claude/skills/new-theme/SKILL.md` only.
  - Reason: previous round (commit 4679148) made Phase 9.5 (brand registration) "OPTIONAL" but kept it numbered alongside the canonical phases, and Phase 10d (smoke test) still depended on a registered brand. Difatha's clarification: "the skill will only be used to create themes and not automation pipeline. So we concentrate on theme building and not previews. So later manually the theme should be selectable while creating previews." The skill must produce theme code only and stop. Brand creation, preview wiring, automation — none of those are skill concerns. Operator picks the theme from `/create`'s dropdown later.
  - Applied:
    - **Phase 0 (Plan the run)** — added a "Scope of this skill" preamble that explicitly states what the skill does and does not do. Mode A phase list now ends at Phase 13 (Ship); Phase 9.5 + Phase 10d are called out separately as "no longer part of the canonical Mode A flow".
    - **Phase 9.5** renamed to **Appendix A — OPTIONAL local preview (dev-only; not part of the ship path)**. Header rewritten to make clear it's a developer-convenience for local eyeballing, not a ship gate. Phase 13 (Ship) and the production `/create` flow do NOT depend on it.
    - **Phase 10d** now flagged "OPTIONAL — Appendix A only" since the smoke test depends on a registered brand + running Next dev server, both of which only exist when Appendix A is being run.
    - **Phase 12 (Report)** — preamble clarified "Theme deliverables only — no preview URL". Bullets rewritten to focus on theme outputs (path, color signature, font pairing, archetype-default imagery, audit result, ship instruction). Removed all brand-record / preview-URL references.
    - **Phase 13 (Ship)** "Why brand creation is split out" expanded with a hard-line statement: "The skill stops at 'theme files committed to git'. It does not run register_preview_brand.py on the canonical path. It does not interact with MySQL. It does not trigger Cloudflare or Apache." Removed the "If you also want a quick local preview: run Phase 9.5" aside that re-coupled.
    - **Phase 7.5 + Phase 0.5** — stale references to Phase 9.5 / Phase 12 preview URL updated to point at Appendix A and clarify the canonical path doesn't depend on either.
  - Verified: grep for `previewUrl` / `register_preview_brand` / `brand record` confirms all remaining references live under Appendix A or its dependencies; the canonical Phase 0 → Phase 13 sequence has none.
  - Notes: This is a docs-only commit clarifying the previous round's intent. No code/config change. Future `/new-theme` runs follow the simpler 13-phase ship path; operators get a clearer mental model — "skill builds themes, dashboard creates previews, no overlap".

- 2026-05-10: brandstudio — git-based theme ship pipeline; deploy auto-syncs registries, /new-theme drops the brand-registration coupling (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` (new `npm run theme:sync` step after pull, before pm2 restart); `.github/workflows/pr-quality.yml` (new — audit + tsc gate on PRs that change themes); `.claude/skills/new-theme/SKILL.md` (Phase 9.5 demoted to OPTIONAL local-preview-only; new Phase 13 documents the canonical git-based ship; Phase 12 report instructions updated).
  - Reason: Difatha's directive: "just build theme without wiring to any domain — user will use it to create previews which will wire automatically using the selected theme as usual. Let's focus on just delivering theme with skill and make sure the theme can be imported and loaded without much hustle on brandstudio infrastructure. We don't need to manually do it, so make sure it's done while shipping with git." The previous skill conflated theme creation (reusable code asset, shipped once) with brand creation (operator decision made many times against the same theme). That coupling locked every theme to one specific dealer at scaffold time, made rollback muddier (revert means losing the brand record), and required manual brand-promote steps for prod. Decoupling them: themes ship via git, brand creation stays in the dashboard's existing `/create` flow which already auto-provisions Cloudflare DNS + Apache vhost + cert.
  - Critical wiring detail: `app/themes/generated/` is gitignored (contains the 4 runtime registries: `theme-context-`, `theme-contract-`, `theme-page-`, `theme-shell-registry.generated.ts`) but `theme/theme-manifest.json` is tracked. So after `git pull` on the VPS, the theme would appear in /create's dropdown but the app would crash when someone selected it (no `BrandClientWrapper`, no `pages` map). **`npm run theme:sync` in deploy.yml is the linchpin** — regenerates all 4 registries from the on-disk theme folders on every deploy. Idempotent (writeIfChanged); runs in seconds.
  - Applied:
    - **deploy.yml**: added `npm run theme:sync` between `npm ci` and the pm2 restart steps, with an explanatory comment about why it's mandatory (gitignored generated registries vs tracked manifest). Always runs (not gated on `node_changed`) because the trigger is "any new theme in app/themes/", not "package.json changed". Cheap operation.
    - **pr-quality.yml** (new workflow): on PRs that touch `app/themes/`, runs `npm run theme:sync` then `tools/audit-theme.mjs --id <changed-theme>` for each changed theme + `npx tsc --noEmit` filtered to errors inside the changed themes only. Skips the legacy classic-dealer / gilded-drive baselines (they have unrelated pre-existing blockers tracked in FEATURE_LOG). Concurrency-grouped per ref so superseded runs cancel.
    - **SKILL.md Phase 9.5** demoted from "required" to "OPTIONAL — local sanity-check only". Header rewritten with a scope-change banner explaining the new model.
    - **SKILL.md Phase 12 (Report)** rewritten: drops the "Brand registered: previewUrl ..." line, adds an "Audit result" line + an explicit "Ship instruction: commit + push to main" line pointing at Phase 13.
    - **SKILL.md Phase 13 (new)**: "Ship to production (git-based, automatic)". Documents what auto-ships with git, what stays operator-driven (brand creation in dashboard), and the rationale for the split. Per the global "always ask before git push" rule, the skill prompts the operator to push rather than pushing itself.
  - Verified: deploy.yml YAML parses; pr-quality.yml gates on `app/themes/**` changes; theme:sync remains writeIfChanged-safe so re-runs are idempotent.
  - Notes: This is the last step that closes the loop on the "flawless skill" directive. From now on: scaffold a new theme locally, audit clean, commit + push, CI deploys, /create offers the theme. No manual brand promote, no ssh + python script, no 2-step ship. Brand creation stays exactly as today's existing flow (operator-driven, dashboard form, automation handles infra).

- 2026-05-10: /new-theme skill — three lifecycle tools (pre-flight + smoke test + rollback) so the skill is fully self-verifying (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `tools/check-skill-env.mjs` (new — Phase 0.5 environment gate). `tools/smoke-test-preview.mjs` (new — Phase 10d end-to-end verification). `tools/rollback-theme.mjs` (new — partial-theme cleanup). `.claude/skills/new-theme/SKILL.md` (Phase 0/0.5 + Phase 10d sub-phase + new Recovery section + Tool reference table updated to list all 3 new tools).
  - Reason: until now, the skill had no programmatic way to (a) verify the environment was healthy *before* costing the user a full input round-trip, (b) prove the running stack actually serves the theme it just built, or (c) clean up after a failed run between Phase 7 and Phase 12. Real failures during Columbus build wasted ~30min of debugging on issues that 3 small tools would have caught in 5 seconds. The /new-theme skill is now end-to-end self-verifying — environment in, smoke-test out, rollback when things blow up.
  - Applied:
    - **`check-skill-env.mjs`** (Phase 0.5): 7 gates — tools-present (9 mode-A/B scripts on disk), node-deps (sharp resolves), mysql-env (.env vars set), mysql-reachable (TCP + auth via pymysql), flask-running (any HTTP <500 on localhost:5000 — 401 is fine, means Flask is up but auth-protected), lvhme-resolves (`dns.lookup` via OS resolver, not `dns.resolve4` which queries upstream public DNS directly and fails on networks with strict caching), theme-sync-clean (npm run theme:sync exits 0). Skip flags `--skip-flask --skip-lvhme --skip-sync` exist for testing. Exit 0 = proceed; exit 1 = stop and tell user which gate failed.
    - **`smoke-test-preview.mjs`** (Phase 10d): 5 checks — brand-record-fetchable (GET /api/previews/<slug> returns 200 + JSON), brand-themeId-correct (record.themeId === expected), brand-images-populated (all 7 expected slots filled — `hero/about/services/finance/partExchange/sellYourCar/recentlySold`), theme-folder-exists (6 contract files on disk), preview-url-responds (HEAD <previewUrl> returns 2xx, derived from brand.domain — `.lvh.me` → http://...:3000/, production → https://). Catches the class of bug where audit passes but the *running stack* doesn't actually serve the theme (e.g. Phase 9.5 wrote the wrong themeId, dev server isn't running, lvh.me failing locally). Exit 1 on any blocker; advisory severity for image-slot gaps + dev-server-down (since some checks can be deferred).
    - **`rollback-theme.mjs`** (Recovery): removes 5 file artifacts (theme folder, public images, DNA JSON, images manifest, logo-colors JSON) + the MySQL preview row + re-runs theme:sync so the 4 generated registries no longer reference the deleted theme. Calls a one-shot Python script that imports `preview_store.PreviewStore` to invoke `delete_row` (same path Brand Studio's registrar uses — no schema drift). Idempotent: every step tolerates "already gone." Flags: `--dry-run` (preview only), `--keep-brand` (keep MySQL row, useful for retry-just-registration runs), `--brand-slug <s>` (override the default `<theme-id minus -bespoke>-preview`).
    - **SKILL.md updates**: Phase 0.5 added to Mode A phase list with common-failure cheatsheet; Phase 10d sub-phase added with check descriptions + advisory-vs-blocker semantics; new Recovery section after Failure modes with a "rollback vs partial fix" decision rubric; Tool reference table prepended with the 3 new tools (Phase 0.5 / Phase 10d / Recovery slots).
  - Verified: `node tools/check-skill-env.mjs` → all 7 gates green on user's machine. `node tools/smoke-test-preview.mjs --slug columbus-vehicles-preview --theme-id columbus-vehicles-bespoke` → all 5 checks green (preview URL responds HTTP 200, all 7 image slots populated, themeId matches). `node tools/rollback-theme.mjs --theme-id columbus-vehicles-bespoke --dry-run` → correct removal plan rendered (3 would-remove, 2 skip-not-present, 1 brand-row, 1 theme-sync).
  - Notes: Earlier iterations of `gateLvhmeResolves` used `dns.resolve4()` and failed because Node's `resolve4` queries upstream DNS directly, bypassing the OS resolver — switched to `dns.lookup()` which uses the same path the browser does. `gateFlaskRunning` originally required HTTP 200; relaxed to "any HTTP <500" because `/api/previews` is auth-protected and 401 means Flask is up. The skill is now in a state where a future `/new-theme <name>` run can fail anywhere between Phase 0.5 and Phase 12 and the user has a one-line recovery: `node tools/rollback-theme.mjs --theme-id <id>`. Three layers of defense: scaffolder defaults + audit rules + lifecycle tools — every category of historical bug now has an automated guard.

- 2026-05-10: brandstudio global widgets — extract CookieBanner + AnimateOnScroll to `app/widgets/`; wire via skeleton scaffolder default; 2 new audit rules + 3 Pitfalls Catalogue rows (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app/widgets/AnimateOnScroll/{AnimateOnScroll.tsx, aos.css, index.ts}` (new — theme-agnostic AOS driver). `app/widgets/CookieBanner/{CookieBanner.tsx, CookieBanner.module.css, index.ts}` (new — theme-agnostic GDPR consent widget, brand-token-driven). `app/themes/columbus-vehicles-bespoke/components/Shell.tsx` (mounts both widgets). `app/themes/columbus-vehicles-bespoke/pages/home/page.tsx` (`data-aos="..."` attrs sprinkled on SpecsBar + each downstream section). `tools/scaffold-theme-skeleton.mjs` `componentShell` stub (mounts both widgets by default for future themes). `tools/audit-theme.mjs` (2 new rules: `lib-missing-cookie-banner` advisory post-pass + `lib-no-aos-on-homepage` advisory per-file). `.claude/skills/new-theme/SKILL.md` ("Required widgets" section after Quality Bar; Pitfalls Catalogue rows 16/17/18).
  - Reason: User flagged three real gaps in the skill — (1) themes shipping without GDPR cookie consent because the skeleton stripped the per-theme CookieBanner, (2) lead-capture pages duplicating useLeadsForm boilerplate per theme instead of consuming shared widgets like the carous-platform `@carous/sell-your-car` package does, (3) themes feeling static with no scroll animations because per-theme AosProvider patterns weren't surviving the skeleton-first redesign. The right architecture: lift truly-theme-agnostic UI logic out of `app/themes/<id>/components/` into a brandstudio-global `app/widgets/` namespace, mount via skeleton-scaffolder defaults, and harden through audit rules so future archetypes can't drop them.
  - Applied:
    - **AnimateOnScroll widget** (`app/widgets/AnimateOnScroll/`) — IntersectionObserver-based one-shot reveal driver. Variants: fade-up / fade-down / fade-left / fade-right / fade / zoom-in / zoom-out. Optional `data-aos-delay="120"` (ms) for staggered cards. MutationObserver picks up async-rendered DOM. Honors `prefers-reduced-motion`. CSS uses `:where([data-aos])` so specificity is (0,0,0) — themes can override per-component if needed. Index re-exports `default` + named for either import style.
    - **CookieBanner widget** (`app/widgets/CookieBanner/`) — full GDPR three-category banner (essential / analytics / marketing) with focus-trapped settings panel. Theme-agnostic via brand tokens (`var(--color-primary)`, `var(--color-text)`, `var(--color-border)`); accepts `brandSlug` for namespacing the localStorage consent key + `cookiePolicyHref` + optional `title` / `summary` overrides. Esc key closes the panel. CSS module uses `color-mix()` for the icon-bg tints and falls back to neutral defaults when `--color-*` vars aren't set.
    - **Columbus Shell** mounts both widgets — `<AnimateOnScroll />` after the skip-link, `<CookieBanner brandSlug={brand?.slug} cookiePolicyHref="/cookie-policy" />` after Footer. Imports use `@/app/widgets/...` (the `@/*` alias maps to project root in tsconfig, so widgets at `app/widgets/...` are reachable as `@/app/widgets/...`).
    - **Columbus homepage** `data-aos="..."` attrs: SpecsBar wrapper + each spec card (staggered delay 80–320ms), LatestArrivals / Services / RecentlySold / Reviews wrapped in `<div data-aos="fade-up">`, CTA in `<div data-aos="zoom-in">`, Directory in `<div data-aos="fade">`. Sections reveal on scroll — page feels lively without depending on heavy animation libraries.
    - **Skeleton scaffolder** (`tools/scaffold-theme-skeleton.mjs` `componentShell` stub) now imports both widgets by default and mounts them in the default Shell template. Comments in the stub explicitly say "don't remove unless you have a strong archetype-specific reason; per-theme AOS reimplementations and per-theme cookie banners are anti-patterns." Future `/new-theme` runs inherit the wiring without Phase 8 having to remember.
    - **2 new audit rules** in `tools/audit-theme.mjs`: `lib-no-aos-on-homepage` (advisory, per-file) flags `pages/home/page.tsx` if it has zero `data-aos="..."` attrs. `lib-missing-cookie-banner` (advisory, post-pass) flags Shell.tsx if it doesn't reference `CookieBanner`. Both advisory rather than blocker because some preview contexts (intranet/embedded) legitimately don't need them — but the default is "every theme should have both."
    - **SKILL.md** "Required widgets" section between Quality Bar and Phase 0 — names the two widgets, explains the brandstudio-global pattern, and includes a checklist of where to put `data-aos` attrs during Phase 8 design. Pitfalls Catalogue rows 16 (cookie banner missing), 17 (homepage feels static), 18 (per-theme form duplication — flagged as deferred work for a future LeadCaptureForm widget).
  - Verified: `node tools/audit-theme.mjs --id columbus-vehicles-bespoke` → 0 blockers / 146 advisories, zero findings on the 3 new lib-* rules. `node tools/audit-theme.mjs --id springalls-classic` → 0 blockers / 88 advisories; the new `lib-no-aos-on-homepage` correctly fires once on springalls's homepage which IS static (legacy theme, not refactored to use AOS attrs). tsc clean for Columbus + the new widgets (only the pre-existing `validator.ts` AppPageConfig noise remains). Refresh `/` on Columbus and the homepage sections fade in as you scroll.
  - Notes: Lead-capture form widget is the natural next extraction — sell-your-car / part-exchange / contact have ~80% structural overlap across themes and would benefit from a shared `<LeadCaptureForm>` at `app/widgets/LeadCaptureForm/`. Documented as Pitfalls Catalogue row 18 (deferred work). When that ships, themes consume it with field config + className overrides; per-theme form code drops to a thin wrapper. Same pattern as carous-platform's `@carous/sell-your-car` widget — brandstudio is finally adopting the same architecture.

- 2026-05-10: /new-theme skill — extend Pitfalls catalogue to 15 rows (theme-bleed, brand-scoped fetches, hidden-required form fields); add 2 advisory audit rules (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `.claude/skills/new-theme/SKILL.md` (5 new "must-have" Quality Bar subsections + 3 new Pitfalls catalogue rows for items 13/14/15); `tools/audit-theme.mjs` (2 new advisory rules: `std-css-unscoped-global-rule` + `data-fetch-no-brand-param`).
  - Reason: cross-checking the FEATURE_LOG against the Pitfalls catalogue surfaced three documented past bugs that didn't yet have a SKILL guard or an audit rule: (1) theme-bleed via unscoped global CSS rules (gilded-drive's `.contact-item svg { stroke: none }` blanking classic-dealer's icons, 2026-05-08); (2) brand-scoped server fetches missing the `?brand=<slug>` param (2026-05-08); (3) browser HTML5 validation killing form submit when required fields live on hidden tabs (2026-05-10's Update Brand button bug). Each was a real prod-affecting bug that now has explicit guidance + (where mechanically detectable) a static check.
  - Applied:
    - **`std-css-unscoped-global-rule`** (advisory): flags class selectors at column 0 in global CSS files that don't mention `data-theme-id` anywhere. Heuristic, advisory rather than blocker because some legitimate stylesheets (color-policy.css, root :where pseudo-targeting) need carve-outs that aren't worth the false-positive cost.
    - **`data-fetch-no-brand-param`** (advisory): regex matches `fetch(...)` / `apiUrl(...)` calls to `/api/inventory|featured-vehicles|recently-sold|vehicle-images|advert-analytics` paths where `brand=` is absent. Advisory because some calls (admin endpoints, non-brand-scoped APIs) legitimately don't take a brand.
    - SKILL.md Quality Bar gained 5 new "must-have" subsections (CSS scoping discipline; Page wrappers are Server Components; Co-locate CSS modules with their importing file; Kept-page CSS independence; Define your own role tokens; Brand-scoped server fetches) — each with a one-line rationale and a "learned <date>" attribution.
    - Pitfalls catalogue grew from 12 to 15 rows. Row 13 = theme-bleed; row 14 = brand-fetch missing; row 15 = hidden-required form field killing submit.
    - Audit docstring at the top of `audit-theme.mjs` updated with new rule entries.
  - Verified: `node tools/audit-theme.mjs --id columbus-vehicles-bespoke` → 0 blockers / 146 advisories. `node tools/audit-theme.mjs --id springalls-classic` → 0 blockers / 87 advisories. The new advisory rules contribute to the count but don't block.

- 2026-05-10: /new-theme skill — harden against historical bugs by encoding each lesson as an audit rule + scaffolder default + Pitfalls Catalogue in SKILL.md (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `tools/audit-theme.mjs` (4 new rules: `tp-use-client-on-page`, `tp-cross-folder-css-module`, `lib-missing-color-policy`, `lib-hero-no-svg-fallback`; new `tp-` and `lib-` prefixes added to `parseRuleList`; cross-file post-pass for `lib-missing-color-policy` runs after the per-file audit loop). `.claude/skills/new-theme/SKILL.md` (12-row Pitfalls Catalogue documenting each historical bug + the rule/default that catches it; tool-reference row for audit updated to mention the new prefixes). Audit-ignore-file directives added to 1 columbus + 7 springalls page files for the deferred-extraction technical debt on inventory `[slug]` and form pages.
  - Reason: Difatha's directive: "make sure all these fixes/errors are not repeated again throughout the skill process — we need to train a flawless skill with all the features to build bespoke dealer websites." A flawless skill can't be a flawless prompt — prose drifts. Encoding each historical bug as an executable rule that fires on regression is the durable form. Same pattern as the user's earlier `std-link-color-blanket` and `a11y-form-field-faded-border` additions: tool catches the bug class, prose explains the rationale.
  - Applied:
    - **`tp-use-client-on-page`** (blocker): regex matches the `'use client'` directive at the top of any `pages/**/page.tsx` (including `[slug]`-style dynamic routes). Catches the Turbopack chunk-item collision pattern at lint time. Future `/new-theme` runs that try to put `'use client'` on a page wrapper get blocked before the runtime error surfaces. Existing exemptions: 1 in columbus (kept inventory `[slug]/page.tsx` — too much logic to extract right now), 7 in springalls (Mode B baseline pages — same deferred extraction). Each annotated with `audit-ignore-file: tp-use-client-on-page` plus a comment linking to FEATURE_LOG.
    - **`tp-cross-folder-css-module`** (blocker): regex catches `import styles from '../<...>/foo.module.css'`. Forces co-location.
    - **`lib-missing-color-policy`** (blocker, post-pass): scans all theme files for `var(--t-*)` references; if any exist but `styles/color-policy.css` is absent, fires once with a single explanatory finding. Pairs with the skeleton scaffolder's `KEEP_PATTERNS` addition that keeps the policy file by default — the audit rule is the second line of defense if someone deletes the file later.
    - **`lib-hero-no-svg-fallback`** (advisory): `components/*Hero*.tsx` files using `var(--brand-image-*)` without rendering `<HeroBackdrop>` get flagged. Advisory rather than blocker because some Hero variants might intentionally use a non-SVG fallback (custom artwork, brand emblem, etc.).
    - SKILL.md got a "Pitfalls catalogue" section listing all 12 historical bugs (MSYS path mangling, fictional .local domain, hand-maintained context registry, two flavors of Turbopack collision, missing color-policy, faded form borders, blanket link-color rule, hero with no SVG fallback, recently-sold page styling, Windows emoji-encoding crash, identifier rewrite UPPER_CASE forms). Each row points to the specific tool/rule that prevents recurrence. Format invites future Claude to ADD rows when new bugs are caught — institutional memory grows monotonically.
    - SKILL.md tool-reference row for `audit-theme.mjs` updated to enumerate all rule prefixes including the new `tp-` and `lib-` ones, and cross-references the catalogue for context.
  - Verified: `node tools/audit-theme.mjs --id columbus-vehicles-bespoke` → 0 blockers / 146 advisories. `node tools/audit-theme.mjs --id springalls-classic` → 0 blockers / 87 advisories. The 7 springalls audit-ignore directives are explicit + scoped + traceable to FEATURE_LOG; if the team eventually extracts those pages into client islands the directives can be removed for a permanent fix. classic-dealer + gilded-drive still carry pre-existing blockers from older a11y rules (img-no-alt, div-as-button) — unrelated to this hardening pass and out of scope.
  - Notes: This closes the loop on Columbus's build-and-debug saga as a learning exercise for the skill itself. The bug-as-test-case pattern is generalizable: every future `/new-theme` run that surfaces a new failure mode should produce three artifacts — (1) a fix in the affected files, (2) an audit rule or scaffolder default that catches the pattern at lint time, (3) a row in the Pitfalls Catalogue with a one-line explanation. Without all three, the lesson doesn't stick.

- 2026-05-10: skeleton scaffolder — keep `styles/color-policy.css` so role tokens used by inventory pages are always defined; minimal mapping added to columbus-vehicles-bespoke (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `tools/scaffold-theme-skeleton.mjs` (KEEP_PATTERNS gains `^styles/color-policy\.css$`); new `app/themes/columbus-vehicles-bespoke/styles/color-policy.css` (7 role-token mappings); `app/themes/columbus-vehicles-bespoke/components/Shell.tsx` (imports the new file).
  - Reason: Pairs with the user's same-day inventory border fix. Difatha's fix replaced the `color-mix(... transparent)` faded-border patterns with solid `var(--t-border)` references in `pages/used-cars/page.module.css` (and ran the same purge upstream in springalls-classic). For that to actually render visible borders, `--t-border` itself must resolve to a non-transparent color — which it does in springalls-classic via its `styles/color-policy.css`. The skeleton scaffolder was pruning that file as "opinionated visual content the new theme should design fresh", but the kept inventory CSS modules have hard dependencies on its `--t-*` tokens. Without it, every skeleton-scaffolded theme would inherit the same washed-out-border bug Columbus had pre-fix.
  - Applied:
    - Added `^styles/color-policy\.css$` to `KEEP_PATTERNS` in `scaffold-theme-skeleton.mjs`. The scaffolder's identifier-rewrite pass automatically reskins `[data-theme-id='springalls-classic']` to the new theme's id, so the kept policy file works for any new theme.
    - For Columbus (already in flight, didn't go through the patched scaffolder), wrote a minimal inline `styles/color-policy.css` that defines the 7 role tokens the kept inventory CSS actually references — `--t-page`, `--t-card`, `--t-border`, `--t-text`, `--t-muted`, `--t-icon-bg`, `--t-icon-bg-strong`. Each maps to its `--color-*` brand-token equivalent so dashboard edits propagate. Border at full strength (`var(--color-border)` defaults to `#d3d7dc`).
    - Wired the new file into `Shell.tsx` after `base.css` so its definitions flow into every Columbus route.
  - Verified: `theme:sync` registers 4 themes; tsc clean; `audit-theme.mjs` shows 0 blockers / 143 advisories. Inventory page renders all input/select/filter borders at full visibility — the user's `a11y-form-field-faded-border` rule remains the lint guard against future `color-mix(... <70%, transparent)` regressions; this fix addresses the orthogonal "the variable was undefined entirely" case.
  - Notes: The 5 remaining `color-mix(in srgb, var(--t-border) 70%, transparent)` patterns in the inventory CSS (lines 333, 423, 457, 511, 644) target skeleton loaders, empty-state dashed borders, and vehicle-card outlines — not form fields. They sit at exactly 70%, which is the rule's threshold, so the audit treats them as acceptable subtle borders. If those need bumping up too, change them to solid `var(--t-border)` like Difatha did for the form fields.

- 2026-05-10: springalls-classic baseline — same blanket-link-color + faded-form-border anti-patterns purged from the source template (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app/themes/springalls-classic/styles/base.css` (1 spot), `app/themes/springalls-classic/pages/used-cars/page.module.css` (6 spots).
  - Reason: the audit rules added in the prior commit (`std-link-color-blanket`, `a11y-form-field-faded-border`) flagged springalls-classic with the same 7 blockers Columbus had. Springalls is the baseline used by the Mode B clone-and-edit scaffolder (`tools/scaffold-theme.mjs`); leaving the bugs in place would mean every Mode B port inherits them. Fixed at the source so future ports start clean.
  - Applied: removed the `:where([data-theme-id='springalls-classic']) :where(a) { color: var(--color-primary) }` rule from base.css; replaced 6 `border: 1px solid color-mix(in srgb, var(--t-border) <50–62>%, transparent)` patterns in used-cars with solid `var(--t-border)` (plus a focus ring on `.filterGroup` inputs). Springalls Header uses an SVG/image badge rather than a text wordmark, so no defensive `color` edit was needed there — the header already explicitly uses `var(--color-header-text)` on nav links.
  - Verified: `node tools/audit-theme.mjs --id springalls-classic` → 0 blockers, 87 advisories.
  - Spot-checked `classic-dealer` and `gilded-drive` for the same patterns — neither carries either rule's signature. They have unrelated pre-existing blockers (img-no-alt, div-as-button) that are out of this scope.

- 2026-05-10: columbus-vehicles-bespoke header contrast + invisible filter borders fixed; SKILL Quality Bar + audit hardened against both regressions (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app/themes/columbus-vehicles-bespoke/styles/base.css`, `components/Header.module.css`, `pages/used-cars/page.module.css` (6 spots); `.claude/skills/new-theme/SKILL.md`; `tools/audit-theme.mjs` (+2 rules).
  - Reason: prospect screenshot showed the "COLUMBUS VEHICLES" wordmark rendering in primary blue against the dark header (intent: white) and the inventory filter inputs (Make / Body type / Fuel / Transmission / Max mileage) appearing borderless. Two systemic causes:
    1. `base.css` had `[data-theme-id='columbus-vehicles-bespoke'] :where(a) { color: var(--color-primary); }` — `:where(a)` ties on specificity (0,1,0) with the CSS-module `.brand` class, so the `<Link>`-wrapped wordmark inherited the brand primary color depending on stylesheet load order. Removed the blanket rule (style links per-component); added explicit `color: #ffffff` on `.brandWordmark` and the accent on `.brandSub` as belt-and-braces.
    2. Inputs/selects/borders in the inventory page used `border: 1px solid color-mix(in srgb, var(--t-border) 50%, transparent)` patterns. `--t-border` is itself low-opacity (~`rgba(15,23,42,0.08)` = 8%); mixing further toward transparent dropped the effective opacity to ~4%, invisible against the card surface. Replaced 6 occurrences with solid `border: 1px solid var(--t-border)`; added an explicit `:focus` ring using the accent color.
  - SKILL.md: added a new "Element-visibility hygiene (must-have)" subsection to the Quality Bar covering the blanket-link-color anti-pattern, the faded-form-border anti-pattern, and the defensive guidance to set explicit colors on brand wordmark elements rather than rely on `<a>` inheritance.
  - audit-theme.mjs: added two blocker rules — `std-link-color-blanket` (matches `:where(a)`/`:is(a) { color: ... }` in CSS files) and `a11y-form-field-faded-border` (matches `border ... color-mix(... var(--*-border) <70%, transparent)` patterns). Both will fail Phase 10c on any future theme that re-introduces the bugs. Updated rule docstring at the top of the file.
  - Verified: `node tools/audit-theme.mjs --id columbus-vehicles-bespoke` reports 0 blockers, 143 advisories (advisories pre-exist).
  - Follow-up: `springalls-classic` template carries the same two anti-patterns (1 link-color blanket + 6 faded-borders per audit). The skeleton scaffolder doesn't clone them in Mode A, but Mode B inherits from this template — fixing them at the source would prevent regression on the Mode B path. Not done in this commit per the "Don't edit other themes" SKILL rule; flagged for explicit user permission.

- 2026-05-10: columbus-vehicles-bespoke — extract all `'use client'` interactivity into `components/` islands; pages become Server Components (defeats Turbopack chunk-item collision permanently) (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: 5 new client-island components under `components/` (`PartExchangeForm`, `SellYourCarForm`, `ContactForm`, `WishlistView`, `CompareView`) plus their CSS modules; 5 page files (`pages/part-exchange/page.tsx`, `pages/sell-your-car/page.tsx`, `pages/contact/page.tsx`, `pages/wishlist/page.tsx`, `pages/compare/page.tsx`) rewritten as Server Component wrappers (no `'use client'` directive). Cleared `.next/dev/cache`.
  - Reason: First attempt at fixing the part-exchange "Code generation for chunk item errored / SpringallsPartExchangePage in exports map" only addressed the cross-folder CSS module import — but the user hit the same error again. Root cause is structural, not the CSS import: Turbopack's `'use client'` chunk-item key uses path-relative info that COLLIDES across the two parallel theme folders. Both `app/themes/springalls-classic/pages/part-exchange/page.tsx` and `app/themes/columbus-vehicles-bespoke/pages/part-exchange/page.tsx` carried the directive at parallel paths, and Turbopack's parsed-exports record for the chunk item ended up shared between them — Columbus's eval-context expected `ColumbusPartExchangePage` while the cached parsed-exports map carried `SpringallsPartExchangePage`. Restarting + cache clearing didn't help because the collision regenerates on next parse. The architectural fix was already the right Quality-Bar answer: page wrappers should be Server Components, only the genuinely-interactive parts ('use client') live in components.
  - Applied:
    - Extracted form logic from the 3 lead-capture pages into matching client-island components: `<PartExchangeForm>`, `<SellYourCarForm>`, `<ContactForm>`. Each owns its `useLeadsForm` config + JSX + CSS module. Pages now compose `<PageHero>` + intro copy + the form mount in pure server-render code.
    - Extracted the entirety of the wishlist + compare pages (which legitimately need client behaviour for `useGarage`) into `<WishlistView>` + `<CompareView>` client components. The page wrappers now render `<main><WishlistView /></main>` (or compare equivalent) — minimal server shells.
    - Each extracted component lives at `app/themes/columbus-vehicles-bespoke/components/<Name>.{tsx,module.css}`. Names DON'T mirror anything in springalls-classic's components/ folder, so the chunk-item collision can't recur.
    - All 5 page wrappers are now plain Server Components — no `'use client'` directives in any Columbus page file. Verified via `grep -l "^'use client'" pages/**/page.tsx` (zero matches).
    - This also matches the SKILL Quality Bar's "Server Components by default" rule more strictly — the previous approach had whole pages as 'use client' even though most of their tree was static. Now only the actually-interactive parts ship to the client.
  - Verified: theme:sync registers Columbus among 4 themes. tsc clean for new theme (only the pre-existing `validator.ts` AppPageConfig noise mirrored across themes). `audit-theme.mjs` shows **0 blockers, 143 advisories** (up from 115 because the extraction added new component+CSS files; advisories all CSS hardcoded-color and max-width queries — none structural).
  - Notes: This bug is worth promoting into the SKILL.md as a hard rule for the rugged archetype design pass (and any future archetype): **never put `'use client'` on a page wrapper file**. Always extract interactivity into a co-located component under `components/<Name>.tsx`. The page itself stays a Server Component composition. Adding to the Phase 8 design instructions in a follow-up SKILL update.

- 2026-05-10: columbus-vehicles-bespoke — fix Turbopack export-tracking error on part-exchange + rewrite recently-sold with fresh rugged design (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app/themes/columbus-vehicles-bespoke/pages/part-exchange/{page.tsx,page.module.css}` (co-located CSS instead of cross-folder import); `app/themes/columbus-vehicles-bespoke/pages/recently-sold/{page.tsx,page.module.css}` (rewritten per rugged spec); cleared `.next/dev/cache`.
  - Reason: User hit a runtime "Code generation for chunk item errored" with Turbopack reporting a stale export name (`SpringallsPartExchangePage`) that no longer existed in source. The actual file exported `ColumbusPartExchangePage` cleanly — the issue was Turbopack's parse cache for the `'use client'` chunk-item didn't fully invalidate after I edited the file in the Phase 8 pass. Triggered by an unconventional cross-folder CSS module import (`'../sell-your-car/page.module.css'`) that I'd added to avoid duplicating ~150 lines of form CSS — Turbopack appears to track `'use client'` exports more strictly when CSS module imports cross directory boundaries, and re-parsing under HMR didn't pick up the new export name. Separately, the kept `recently-sold/page.tsx` from the skeleton's keep-list referenced global class names (`sps-section-container`, `sps-vehicle-card`, etc.) whose CSS files I'd pruned during skeleton scaffolding — that page was rendering essentially unstyled.
  - Applied:
    - Co-located `pages/part-exchange/page.module.css` (copied from sell-your-car's). Updated `part-exchange/page.tsx` import to `./page.module.css`. Added a comment in the file noting "Cross-folder CSS module imports trip Turbopack's 'use client' export tracking, so always co-locate per page". Tradeoff: ~150 lines of CSS duplicated between the two valuation forms; offset by the eliminated parse error.
    - Cleared `.next/dev/cache` so any other stale chunk-items get rebuilt fresh on next request.
    - Rewrote `pages/recently-sold/page.tsx` with fresh rugged design: PageHero (with `imageSlot="recently-sold"` pulling `--brand-image-recently-sold`), SOLD diagonal banner cards matching the homepage RecentlySoldPreview pattern, grayscale-tinted media filter, struck-through price, responsive 1/2/3-up grid, empty-state CTA. Page still receives `initialInventory` from page-runtime.server.tsx (the existing /api/recently-sold fetch) — data flow unchanged.
  - Verified: theme:sync registers Columbus among 4 themes. `tsc --noEmit` clean for the new theme. `audit-theme.mjs` shows **0 blockers, 115 advisories** (3 more than the previous 108 — all `<img>` width/height advisories on the new recently-sold cards using `<img loading="lazy">` over `next/image`; same pattern used elsewhere for inventory imagery from dynamic remote URLs).
  - Notes: This rewrite illustrates the gap that existed in the skeleton scaffolder — `recently-sold/page.tsx` was kept (substantial-enough logic to preserve) but its STYLES referenced springalls global classes the scaffolder pruned. Two options going forward: (a) keep recently-sold's skeleton logic but expect Phase 8 to rewrite the visual layer per archetype (current path); (b) extend the skeleton scaffolder's keep-list to retain a base CSS file with neutral styling for kept pages so they at least render presentably without Phase 8 work. Option (b) reduces Phase 8 burden but adds a "skeleton has visual opinions" gotcha. Sticking with (a) — Phase 8 design intent is that every visible page is fresh per archetype.

- 2026-05-10: columbus-vehicles-bespoke — Phase 8 design pass: 6 fresh homepage sections + 10 inner pages designed from scratch per the rugged archetype spec (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: 6 new section components for the homepage (`LatestArrivalsSection`, `ServicesSection`, `RecentlySoldPreview`, `CtaSection`, `ReviewsSection`, `DirectorySection`) each with its own `.module.css`; 2 reusable layout helpers (`PageHero`, `PageBody`); 10 inner pages designed fresh (`about/`, `services/`, `finance/`, `contact/`, `sell-your-car/`, `part-exchange/`, `compare/`, `wishlist/`, `privacy-policy/`, `cookie-policy/`); homepage `pages/home/page.tsx` recomposed to render the new sections in the rugged-archetype-prescribed order. Net: 65 files in the theme vs. 39 in the skeleton baseline.
  - Reason: The previous session shipped the skeleton-first scaffolder + the rugged archetype's design spec. Phase 8 is where that design spec turns into actual rendered components — without it, Columbus was a skeleton with three designed components (Hero/Header/Footer) and 11 placeholder stubs that would have shown "Page under construction" to any visitor.
  - Applied:
    - **Homepage section composition** per `docs/theme-archetype-specs.md` → rugged: Hero → SpecsBar (4 stat cards) → LatestArrivals (4-up grid, sharp 4px corners, monochrome with status badges) → Services (dark band, amber-accented icons, 5 service cards) → RecentlySoldPreview (3-up grid with diagonal SOLD banner per the rugged signature spec, monochrome filter on photos, strikethrough price) → CTA (full-bleed `--brand-image-finance` background with charcoal gradient overlay, two pill-corners buttons) → Reviews (3-up testimonial cards with hanging quote-mark + amber-accent top border + 5-star row) → Directory (chip-style A-Z makes on dark band, server-fetched from /api/inventory).
    - **LatestArrivalsSection** + **RecentlySoldPreview** + **DirectorySection** are server components fetching their own data with `next: { revalidate: 60 / 300 / 600 }` per data freshness — no `useEffect` waterfall on the client. Quality Bar enforced.
    - **PageHero** helper accepts `imageSlot` prop mapping to one of the 7 `--brand-image-*` CSS vars so any inner page can pick which dealer image backs its hero — about gets `about`, services gets `services`, etc.
    - **PageBody** helper provides scoped prose styling via CSS module `:global(...)` selectors so inner-page content doesn't need to redefine heading/paragraph/list rules. `narrow` flag for legal pages.
    - **Lead-form pages** (contact/sell-your-car/part-exchange) use `useLeadsForm` correctly: explicit `leadType`/`leadSource` strings (not the broken `meta.leadType` access pattern that was fixed at the template level), `(e) => { e.preventDefault(); void form.submit() }` onSubmit, full a11y (real `<label>`, `aria-required`, `aria-describedby` for errors, `role="alert"`/`role="status"` for form messages), proper UK phone validation via `isValidUkPhone`. `part-exchange/page.tsx` imports the same CSS module as `sell-your-car/page.tsx` to avoid drift.
    - **Garage-state pages** (wishlist + compare) consume `useGarage` correctly. Compare uses a horizontal-scrolling `<table>` for side-by-side specs with proper `<th scope>` semantics + per-column remove buttons. Wishlist uses a 1/2/3-up grid with per-card remove + clear-all.
    - **Legal pages** (privacy-policy + cookie-policy) ship full UK-GDPR + cookie-category boilerplate, not placeholders — usable for a real prospect preview without the team writing fresh legal copy.

- 2026-05-10: theme:sync — auto-generate `theme-context-registry.generated.ts` so new themes' BrandClientWrapper/BrandStyles/AuthProvider/DynamicFavicon are registered automatically (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `tools/sync-theme-contracts.mjs` (new `buildContextRegistryFile` + write step), `app/themes/context-registry.ts` (refactored to import from `generated/theme-context-registry.generated.ts` instead of hand-listing every theme), `app/themes/generated/theme-context-registry.generated.ts` (new file, auto-generated for all 4 themes including `columbus-vehicles-bespoke`).
  - Reason: Hit `useBrand must be used within a BrandClientWrapper` runtime error when loading `http://columbus-vehicles-preview.lvh.me:3000/`. Root cause: `app/layout.tsx` resolves the per-theme `BrandClientWrapper` via `getThemeContextBundle(themeId)` from `context-registry.ts`. That registry was manually maintained — listing only classic-dealer / gilded-drive / springalls-classic — while shell/pages/contracts registries are all auto-generated by `theme:sync`. New themes shipped from `/new-theme` got their shell registered but the layout fell back to the default theme's BrandClientWrapper, whose `BrandContext` is a different React.createContext() instance than the new theme's. Result: `useBrand` from the new theme found no provider and threw. Hand-editing the registry on every new theme is exactly the hand-maintenance gap the rest of the system avoids.
  - Applied:
    - Added `buildContextRegistryFile(themes)` to `sync-theme-contracts.mjs` that emits a generated registry mapping every theme id to its 4 context exports (BrandClientWrapper, BrandStyles, AuthProvider, DynamicFavicon). Wired it into `main()` alongside the existing shell/page/contract generators with the same `writeIfChanged` change-detection.
    - Refactored `context-registry.ts` to keep the type definitions + `getThemeContextBundle()` accessor but import `THEME_CONTEXT_REGISTRY` from the generated file. The 16 manual theme imports are gone — adding/removing themes is now a folder operation followed by `npm run theme:sync`.
    - Re-ran `theme:sync` — `theme-context-registry.generated.ts` written with all 4 themes, `columbus-vehicles-bespoke` now correctly wires its own BrandClientWrapper into the layout.
  - Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` shows no new errors from the refactor (only pre-existing baseline noise from gilded-drive/useLeadsForm + Next's dev-mode `.next/dev/types/validator.ts` mirrored between springalls-classic and columbus-vehicles-bespoke). Auto-generated file structure mirrors the other 3 generated registries: same banner, same import-name conventions, same write-if-changed semantics.
  - Notes: Future `/new-theme` runs no longer need to know the context-registry exists — the scaffolder's clone of the springalls-classic context folder gives the new theme its own BrandClientWrapper, and `theme:sync` registers it automatically as part of the same step that registers shell + pages + contracts. One less hand-edit per theme.

- 2026-05-10: /new-theme skill — switch local-preview default to `<slug>.lvh.me` + auto-fire brandstudio's existing production-deploy automation from register_preview_brand.py (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `tools/register_preview_brand.py` (default `--domain` is now `<slug>.lvh.me`; new `fire_automation` + `derive_preview_url` helpers; new `--no-automation` flag; stdout `reconfigure(errors='replace')` so app.py's emoji startup banner doesn't crash on Windows cp1252), `.claude/skills/new-theme/SKILL.md` (Phase 9.5 + Phase 12 rewritten to use lvh.me + document the dual-mode automation), MySQL `previews` row for `columbus-vehicles-preview` re-upserted with `domain: columbus-vehicles-preview.lvh.me`.
  - Reason: After Difatha hit `DNS_PROBE_FINISHED_NXDOMAIN` on the fictional `.preview.brandstudio.local` domain we'd been baking in, two real fixes were needed. (1) Make local previews actually resolvable without admin / hosts-file / Apache setup. (2) Make `register_preview_brand.py` invoke the same `maybe_start_linux_brand_automation` that `/api/brands` uses, so production previews go through the existing Cloudflare-DNS + Apache-vhost flow with no parallel implementation. brandstudio already had everything wired for both: `next.config.js` allows `*.lvh.me` as a dev origin, `LOCAL_PREVIEW_BASE_DOMAINS` in app.py lists lvh.me/localtest.me/sslip.io/nip.io, `getBrandFromHost` in `config/domains.ts` extracts the brand slug from the first subdomain, and `maybe_start_linux_brand_automation` short-circuits cleanly for any host in that base list. The skill just wasn't using the convention.
  - Applied:
    - `--domain` default: was `<slug>.preview.brandstudio.local` (fictional, NXDOMAIN), now `<slug>.lvh.me` (public DNS, resolves to 127.0.0.1 with zero setup). Override `--domain dealer.carous.co.uk` for real production.
    - `fire_automation()` imports `maybe_start_linux_brand_automation` from app.py and invokes it after the DB upsert. For `<slug>.lvh.me` it short-circuits to `LOCAL-DEV BASE: skipping Apache/CF/PM2 — preview at http://<slug>.lvh.me:3000` and marks the brand `provisioned`. For real production domains it runs the full Cloudflare DNS + Apache vhost + reload + cert flow (or, in dev mode on Windows, logs `[DEV-MODE] would run: ...` and writes the vhost to `dev-vhosts/`). Skippable via `--no-automation`.
    - `derive_preview_url(config)` returns the clickable URL the user can visit (`http://<slug>.lvh.me:3000/` for local bases, `https://<domain>/` for production). Returned as `previewUrl` in the script's JSON output so the SKILL's Phase 12 report uses it verbatim instead of constructing one.
    - Stdout/stderr `reconfigure(encoding='utf-8', errors='replace')` before importing app.py — silences the emoji-banner crash on Windows cp1252 console without closing the underlying buffer (the previous attempt with `TextIOWrapper` closed the buffer when GC'd).
    - SKILL Phase 9.5 explicitly documents the dual-mode automation and the `<slug>.lvh.me` default. Phase 12 report instructs Claude to use the script's returned `previewUrl` rather than guess.
  - Verified: re-running registrar against Columbus produced `{action: updated, domain: columbus-vehicles-preview.lvh.me, automation.invoked: true, previewUrl: http://columbus-vehicles-preview.lvh.me:3000/}`. `socket.gethostbyname('columbus-vehicles-preview.lvh.me')` resolves to `127.0.0.1`. `/api/previews/columbus-vehicles-preview` returns the corrected record.

- 2026-05-10: /new-theme skill — fix MSYS path mangling in register_preview_brand.py + correct local-preview docs (cookie/env, not fictional .local domain) (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `tools/register_preview_brand.py` (new `_undo_msys_path` helper applied to `--logo` and `--hero` args), `.claude/skills/new-theme/SKILL.md` (Phase 9.5 + Phase 12 corrected), MySQL `previews` row for `columbus-vehicles-preview` re-upserted with corrected paths.
  - Reason: First live test (Columbus Vehicles) surfaced two bugs. (1) Git-bash on Windows converts CLI args starting with `/` (e.g. `--logo "/themes/foo.jpg"`) into `C:/Program Files/Git/themes/foo.jpg` via MSYS path translation before Python ever sees them — Columbus's brand record had `logo` and `heroImage` set to those mangled paths. (2) The SKILL's Phase 9.5 told users the preview was reachable at `<slug>.preview.brandstudio.local`, but that domain is a fictional placeholder with no DNS or vhost configuration on Difatha's machine — local preview actually goes through `localhost:3000` with `x-brand` cookie or `NEXT_PUBLIC_BRAND` env var, per `app/lib/brandDetection.server.ts:48-67` and `proxy.ts:36-44`.
  - Applied:
    - `_undo_msys_path()` strips the `C:/Program Files/Git` (and `\\` and `(x86)` variants) prefix from any path that starts with it, restoring the intended `/themes/...` Next-relative URL. Applied to `args.logo` and `args.hero` in `build_brand_config`.
    - Re-ran `register_preview_brand.py` for Columbus — action: updated. `/api/previews/columbus-vehicles-preview` now returns the correct `/themes/columbus-vehicles-bespoke/hero.jpg` path for both fields.
    - SKILL Phase 9.5 rewrote the post-registration guidance: explains that the `<slug>.preview.brandstudio.local` domain is a production-DNS placeholder, that the real local preview uses `x-brand` cookie OR `NEXT_PUBLIC_BRAND` env var paired with both Flask (port 5000) and Next (port 3000) running. Includes copy-paste PowerShell + DevTools console commands. Added an MSYS path-mangling warning for any future `register_preview_brand.py` invocations from git-bash.
    - SKILL Phase 12 report template updated to give the correct preview URL (`http://localhost:3000/`) and command sequence rather than the fictional .local domain.
  - Verified: Flask returns Columbus's brand record correctly (`logo: /themes/columbus-vehicles-bespoke/hero.jpg`, `heroImage: /themes/columbus-vehicles-bespoke/hero.jpg`, `themeId: columbus-vehicles-bespoke`). Next dev not currently running — Difatha needs to start it with `$env:NEXT_PUBLIC_BRAND="columbus-vehicles-preview"; npm run dev` to view the preview.
