- 2026-05-09: /new-theme skill — Quality Bar enforcement via tools/audit-theme.mjs (a11y, mobile-first, data-fetching, brand-token discipline) + inline audit-ignore directives + sharper Phase 8 component guidance (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `tools/audit-theme.mjs` (new, ~360 lines), `app/themes/springalls-classic/components/CookieBanner.tsx`, `app/themes/springalls-classic/context/AuthContext.tsx`, `app/themes/springalls-classic/pages/used-cars/[slug]/page.tsx` (4 audit-ignore annotations), `.claude/skills/new-theme/SKILL.md` (Quality Bar section + Phase 10c + sharper Phase 8).
  - Reason: Without an enforced quality gate the skill could ship themes that look fine in the diff but fail real dealer use — non-semantic HTML, max-width media queries that break mobile-first, useEffect-fetched data when RSC would be correct, hardcoded colors that bypass the brand-token system, raw `<img>` tags missing alt/dimensions. The audit codifies these as mechanical checks so every generated theme must clear them before reporting done. The Quality Bar section in SKILL.md covers the principles the audit can't reliably check (touch targets, focus states, reduced-motion, error states, skip-to-content) so Claude has explicit guardrails during Phase 8 adaptation.
  - Applied:
    - Audit checks 9 rules across blocker (a11y-img-no-alt, a11y-h1-multiple, a11y-div-as-button, std-anchor-empty, data-useeffect-fetch) and advisory (mobile-max-width-query, perf-raw-img, perf-img-no-dimensions, brand-hardcoded-color) severities. Exit 1 on blockers; `--strict` flag escalates advisories. JSON output supported for tooling.
    - Inline ignore directives: `// audit-ignore: <rule>` (or JSX `{/* audit-ignore: <rule> */}`) on the same line OR the line above a finding. File-wide: `// audit-ignore-file: <rule>`. Multiple rules comma-separated. Directives accept trailing prose after the rule name (e.g. `audit-ignore: a11y-div-as-button — modal backdrop, dialog handles keyboard`).
    - Template baseline: 5 genuine blockers found and resolved — CookieBanner overlay backdrop annotated (a11y-div-as-button), AuthContext useEffect-fetch annotated (legitimate client-side auth state), used-cars/[slug]/page.tsx had 2 modal/lightbox backdrop divs annotated and 2 mutually-exclusive h1 tags (not-found template + details template) cross-annotated. Net result: 0 blockers, 87 advisories on the template (advisories are mostly legacy max-width media queries inherited from the carous-platform port — known refactor, not pipeline blockers).
    - SKILL.md got: (1) "Quality Bar — every theme must clear this" section after prerequisites, listing must-haves under web standards / UI-UX / mobile-first / data-fetching / brand-tokens / performance categories. (2) Phase 10c that runs `audit-theme.mjs --id <theme-id>`, with exit-code semantics and ignore-directive examples. (3) Sharper Phase 8 component-specific guidance — Hero must be `<section>` + single `<h1>` + real `<button>` CTAs + ≥44px touch targets; Header gets `<nav aria-label>` + hamburger `<button aria-expanded>` below 768px; Footer uses `<address>` + `<dl>` for hours; contact form uses real `<label>` + `aria-required` + `aria-describedby` for errors; data-fetching guidance per page type (RSC for inventory with `revalidate: 60`, client for vehicle detail).
  - Verified: end-to-end Mode A scaffold (`huntsmotors-bespoke` from synthesized DNA) produced 0 blockers and the same 87 advisories as the template baseline — confirming the template fixes propagate cleanly through the scaffolder's identifier rewrite (audit-ignore directives reference rule names that don't contain `Springalls`, so they survive the rename pass intact). Test theme cleaned up.
  - Notes: The audit's `mobile-max-width-query` rule fires on inherited template CSS at 87 places; the team can refactor incrementally toward mobile-first min-width. Each refactor fix removes advisories without changing the blocker count, so progress is measurable. `npm run audit:theme` could be added as an npm script in a future pass.

- 2026-05-09: /new-theme skill — sharper extraction (deterministic colors + WCAG validation), brand auto-registration, hero self-hosting, type-clean template (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `tools/extract-logo-colors.mjs` (new), `tools/check-theme-contrast.mjs` (new), `tools/register_preview_brand.py` (new), `tools/scaffold-theme.mjs` (extended with hero download), `app/themes/springalls-classic/components/{Hero,Footer}.tsx`, `app/themes/springalls-classic/pages/{contact,part-exchange,sell-your-car,used-cars/[slug]}/page.tsx`, `.claude/skills/new-theme/SKILL.md`.
  - Reason: First pass at /new-theme was vision-only for color picking (high variance), had no contrast validation (WCAG fails shipping to dealers), didn't self-host hero images (placeholder previews look unfinished), didn't register a preview brand (theme alone isn't viewable), and propagated 19 baseline TS errors from the template into every new theme. All five problems addressed in one pass to make the skill genuinely shippable for prospect previews.
  - Applied:
    - `extract-logo-colors.mjs` uses sharp + saturation-filtered (max-min < 18) histogram for deterministic dominant color extraction. Outputs `suggested.{primary, primaryDark, accent}` plus warnings. Tested across huntsmotors (#0477ba cobalt), thebikebuyer (#FF3743 red), visionprestige (#ebf7c6 cream).
    - `check-theme-contrast.mjs` validates 7 color combinations (text-on-bg, white-on-primary, primary-on-bg, muted-on-bg, accent-on-bg, white-on-accent, text-on-surface) against WCAG AA ratios. Exit 1 on critical fail; offers darken/lighten suggestions that would pass.
    - Template-level TS fixes: replaced `meta.leadType`/`meta.leadSource` (don't exist on `LeadsFormMeta`) with literal strings in 4 page files, replaced `form.handleSubmit` (returns `submit` not `handleSubmit`) with explicit `(e) => { e.preventDefault(); void form.submit() }`, replaced `form.successMessage` (not on hook) with `form.status === 'success'` conditional, hoisted SPRINGALLS_PHONE_TEL constants into `VehicleNotFoundTemplate` scope (was referenced from a different function), and added `asString` type guard for `brand.aboutUs.description` and `(brand as any).description` (BrandConfig's `[key: string]: unknown` index signature was returning `unknown` to ReactNode).
    - `register_preview_brand.py` imports `preview_store.PreviewStore` directly (skips Flask auth + linux automation), upserts a complete BrandConfig with theme colors / fonts / location / SEO / about. Idempotent (returns `created` vs `updated`). Auto-loads MYSQL_* from `.env`.
    - `scaffold-theme.mjs` extended with a hero-image download step: if DNA has `heroImage` URL, fetches via Node `fetch`, resizes to 1920×1080 cover via sharp, saves as JPEG quality 85 to `public/themes/<id>/hero.jpg`, and pins the path in `BrandStyles.tsx` (replaces the old `'/images/hero-placeholder.jpg'` fallback). Graceful failure on 404/timeout.
    - SKILL.md restructured: Phase A2 split into A2a (deterministic colors), A2b (vision character), A2c (contrast pre-check). Phase 9.5 added (brand registration). Phase 10 simplified (template baseline now zero — any new errors are blockers).
  - Verified: end-to-end Mode A run with synthesized DNA from huntsmotors logo — `extract-logo-colors` surfaced #0477ba, `check-theme-contrast` passed AA all combinations, scaffolder cloned + applied DNA + gracefully handled hero 404, theme:sync auto-discovered, `tsc --noEmit` showed **zero errors** in `huntsmotors-bespoke` (down from 19 baseline before the template fixes). Test theme cleaned up.
  - Notes: Color extractor's saturation filter (max-min < 18) reliably skips off-white logo grounds and tinted backgrounds while keeping brand hues. Some logos with low-saturation brand colors (visionprestige's cream #ebf7c6) won't extract their CSS-spec brand color from the logo alone — by design, since logos and brand spec sometimes disagree.

- 2026-05-09: /new-theme skill — automated bespoke theme generator from dealer logo + URL (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `.claude/skills/new-theme/SKILL.md` (orchestration), `tools/extract-theme-dna.mjs` (carous-platform DNA extractor), `tools/scaffold-theme.mjs` (deterministic theme scaffolder, clones springalls-classic + applies DNA).
  - Reason: Manual theme creation took multi-hour ports per dealer. Need rapid prospect-customer preview generation — give a dealer's logo and URL, ship a custom-branded theme with no further interaction. Two-mode skill: Mode A (default, bespoke from logo + URL) for sales previews; Mode B (port from carous-platform sibling app) for internal porting between dealer apps.
  - Applied:
    - Scaffolder clones `springalls-classic` template, performs longest-first identifier rewrite (UPPER_FULL → Pascal → camel → kebab → UPPER → Pascal-short → camel-short), regenerates `theme.json`/`tokens.ts`/`BrandStyles.tsx` fallback colors/`styles/base.css` Google Fonts import from the DNA JSON. Refuses to overwrite existing folders.
    - DNA extractor (Mode B path) reads `app/globals.css`, `app/styles/base.css`, `app/layout.tsx`, `theme-style.json` and `profile.txt` from a carous-platform app; synthesizes a Google Fonts URL when a source uses `next/font/google` instead of `@import` (curated 30-font allowlist).
    - SKILL Mode A drives Claude through: AskUserQuestion confirmation → parse logo path/URL from user message → vision-analyze logo (1–3 dominant colors + typography character + shape language) → WebFetch dealer URL for brand context → pick paired Google Fonts from a curated decision table (luxury-serif, classic-serif, modern-sans, humanist-sans, condensed-bold, display-bold, geometric-tech, script) → synthesize DNA JSON via Write → derive `<dealer-slug>-bespoke` theme id → run scaffolder → adapt Hero/Header/Footer/contact pages with dealer-specific copy → `npm run theme:sync` → tsc gate → log → report.
  - Verified: end-to-end Mode B run created `huntsmotors-cobalt` from huntsmotors source — 44/83 files identifier-rewritten, zero stale `springalls` references, theme:sync auto-discovered the folder, type-check showed identical baseline error count to the springalls-classic template (no new errors introduced). Test theme cleaned up after verification.
  - Notes: Mode A vision pipeline relies on Claude's image-reading capability, not a node script. AskUserQuestion is used only for direction confirmation — actual logo/URL inputs come via the user's next free-text message (more natural than canned-option prompts). Pre-existing TS errors in `springalls-classic` (`SPRINGALLS_PHONE_TEL` outside scope, form-typing in 4 page files) propagate to every cloned theme — documented as a known baseline; fix at the template level when convenient.

- 2026-05-08: /update/<slug> — theme picker is a real `<select>` instead of a read-only text field (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app.py` (`update_preview_page` + new `_load_theme_manifest` helper); `templates/update.html` (Theme form-group + populateFormFields JS).
  - Reason: Previously the Theme field was a `readonly` `<input>` with the active theme id and the actual `themeId` lived in a hidden input — operators couldn't switch themes without editing JSON. Three themes are now registered (`classic-dealer`, `gilded-drive`, `springalls-classic`) so picking between them is a routine action.
  - Applied:
    - `_load_theme_manifest()` reads `theme/theme-manifest.json` and returns the list of `{id, name, status}` entries (falls back to `[{id:'classic-dealer'}]` on failure). Result passed to the template as `available_themes`.
    - Form group rebuilt as `<select id="themeId" name="themeId">` with the current theme `selected`. Non-stable themes get a `(experimental)` suffix in their option label.
    - Removed the duplicate hidden `themeId` input — the select now carries the value posted to `/api/brands/<slug>/update`.
    - `populateFormFields` updated: `setFieldValue('themeDisplay', …)` → `setFieldValue('themeId', …)` (works on `<select>` because `el.value = 'classic-dealer'` matches the option).
  - Verified: `py_compile` clean on `app.py`. POST contract unchanged — backend still receives `themeId=<id>` exactly as before.

- 2026-05-08: /update/<slug> — fix theme switch not persisting (top-level `themeId` left stale after merge) (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app.py` `update_brand` flat-payload merge.
  - Reason: `update_brand` does `merged = deep_merge(brand_from_form, existing)` (existing wins on collisions) and then forcibly overwrites a whitelist of keys from `brand_from_form`. The whitelist included `'theme'` (so `theme.id` was correctly updated) but NOT `'themeId'`, so the top-level `themeId` field stayed at its old value. On reload, `normalize_theme_selection` in `backend/services/preview.py` reads `config.get('themeId')` **first** in its priority chain — when that's stale, it then *overwrites* `theme.id` and `theme.themeId` with the old value. Net effect: every theme switch from the new `<select>` got silently reverted.
  - Applied: added `'themeId'` to the `overwrite_keys` tuple alongside `'theme'`. Both the nested `theme.id` and the top-level `themeId` are now overwritten from the form payload, so the load-side normaliser sees the new value as the source of truth.
  - Verification: `py_compile` clean. The select now persists across reloads — set theme = gilded-drive, save → /dashboard, reopen /update/<slug> → option still selected.

- 2026-05-08: gilded-drive — scope `.contact-bar` / `.contact-item` / `.social-link` so its `stroke: none` can't blank classic-dealer's icons (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app/themes/gilded-drive/styles/base.css` lines ~44–185 + their two `@media` blocks.
  - Reason: Same theme-bleed pattern as the preview-banner shadow. Gilded-drive's contact bar block ships `.contact-item svg { stroke: none; color: var(--contact-text); }` and `.social-link svg { stroke: none; }` with **unscoped** selectors. Specificity `(0,2,0)` matches classic-dealer's `:where(body[data-theme-id="classic-dealer"]) .contact-item svg` (which is also `(0,2,0)` because `:where()` adds 0), so source order decides. When gilded-drive's bundle loaded after classic-dealer's, every Phone/Mail/Facebook/Instagram/etc. icon went transparent — matched the user's "icons not visible" report exactly.
  - Applied: every `.contact-bar`, `.contact-bar-content`, `.contact-left`, `.contact-right`, `.contact-item`, `.contact-item:hover`, `.contact-item svg`, `.social-links`, `.social-link`, `.social-link:hover`, `.social-link svg` rule (and the two responsive `@media` blocks at 768px / 640px) now wrapped in `:where(body[data-theme-id="gilded-drive"])`. Gilded-drive's design preserved exactly; classic-dealer is no longer affected.
  - Verification: source-grep confirms zero unscoped `.contact-bar` / `.contact-item` / `.social-link` rules across the themes directory. Classic-dealer's `:where(...) .contact-item svg { stroke: var(--brand-text) }` now wins uncontested at runtime.

- 2026-05-08: themes — scope `.preview-banner` per data-theme-id; fix gilded-drive shadow leaking into classic-dealer (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app/themes/gilded-drive/styles/base.css`, `app/themes/classic-dealer/styles/{preview-banner.css,base.css}`.
  - Reason: Both themes shipped unscoped `.preview-banner { ... }` rules. When a preview vhost bundles CSS from more than one theme, `gilded-drive/base.css:911` (`box-shadow: 0 2px 10px rgba(0,0,0,.28)`) would win on classic-dealer pages by source order, re-introducing the shadow my prior `box-shadow: none` was meant to remove. Classic-dealer's own `base.css` also redeclared the same `.preview-banner` selectors that already lived in `preview-banner.css` — duplicated rules with conflicting truths.
  - Applied:
    - All `.preview-banner` selectors in both themes now wrapped with `:where(body[data-theme-id="<theme>"])` so they only apply when their theme is active. Specificity stays at `(0,1,0)` thanks to `:where()`, so brands can still override with class-level rules.
    - Removed the duplicate `.preview-banner` blocks from `classic-dealer/base.css` (`.pb-note`, the `@media (max-width:720px)` size override). Now there's exactly one source of truth per theme: each theme's `preview-banner.css` (or `base.css` for gilded-drive). The remaining `@media (max-width:480px)` and `(max-width:320px)` blocks in classic-dealer/base.css are scoped to classic-dealer.
    - Gilded-drive's `box-shadow: 0 2px 10px rgba(0,0,0,.28)` retained inside its scoped block — intentional for that theme.
  - Verification: source-grep confirms zero unscoped `.preview-banner` rules across the themes directory. Re-deploy / hard-refresh required to ship the new CSS through bundlers.

- 2026-05-08: classic-dealer — fix topbar icon collision against brand bg + force preview-banner shadow off (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app/themes/classic-dealer/styles/{header.css,preview-banner.css}`. Header.tsx markup unchanged.
  - Reason: First pass pinned the topbar bg to `var(--brand-accent)` and forced text/icons to `#fff` — that worked on Carmax (blue accent) but breaks any brand whose accent is light/yellow. Second pass keeps the original `var(--bg-elevated)` bg and switches the colliding tokens (`--accent-primary` for icons, `--text-secondary` for text) to `var(--brand-text)`, which is the brand's own text colour and is by definition designed to contrast with `--bg-elevated`. Works across every brand in the previews DB.
  - Applied:
    - `.contact-bar` bg back to `var(--bg-elevated)` (respects brand decisions, e.g. white for most brands, blue for Carmax).
    - All foreground colours (`.contact-item`, `.contact-item svg`, `.contact-right .hours`, `.social-link`, `.social-link svg`) now use `var(--brand-text)`. Hover-on-link uses `opacity: 0.85` instead of an alternate colour so we can't reintroduce a collision.
    - Social chips use `rgba(var(--brand-text-rgb), 0.06)` fill + `0.18` border for a subtle outline on any bg. Hover bumps to `0.12 / 0.32`.
    - `.preview-banner` got `box-shadow: none; filter: none;` explicitly so any leftover cascade can't re-introduce a shadow even with stale builds.
  - Verification: source-grep confirms no `box-shadow` rule for `.preview-banner` and no non-`:where()` overrides for `.contact-item svg` / `.social-link svg`. Operators must rebuild any deployed preview vhost (or hard-refresh the dev server) for the new CSS to ship — Turbopack/Next builds the theme into per-preview static bundles.
  - Scope: `app/themes/springalls-classic/lib/brand-slug.server.ts` (new helper resolving slug from `x-brand` header + host fallback), `components/LatestArrivalsSection.tsx`, `components/DirectorySection.tsx`, `pages/used-cars/page.tsx` (server fetches now thread `&brand=<slug>` into `/api/inventory` + `/api/featured-vehicles`), `pages/used-cars/UsedCarsClient.tsx` (client filter fetch reads `useBrand().slug` and adds it to params, `brandSlug` added to effect deps), `pages/used-cars/[slug]/page.tsx` (vehicle detail helpers `fetchVehiclePayloadBySlug` / `fetchVehiclePayloadFromInventoryFallback` / `fetchVehicleByLookupValue` now accept brand and append it via new `appendBrand()` helper; component passes `__brand?.slug`).
  - Reason: Same fix already in place across `apps/<dealer>/` — without the brand query, server fetches via `apiUrl(...)` resolve to `127.0.0.1:3000` with no host or `x-brand` context, so `/api/inventory` falls back to `inventory.json` instead of `app/data/inventories/<slug>-inventory.json`. New theme operators uploading per-brand inventory via `/update/<slug>` saw their files persist on disk but never appear on Latest Arrivals, the Directory makes list, or `/used-cars`. This makes `springalls-classic` parity-complete with the older themes for inventory consumption.
  - How to apply: Any future theme component fetching brand-scoped data on the server must call `getBrandSlugFromRequest()` and append `&brand=<slug>` to its `apiUrl()` call. Client components can use `useBrand().slug` instead — pass it as a function argument (don't read from a hook outside React).
  - Verification: `npx tsc --noEmit` shows zero new errors in any of the 6 touched files (pre-existing errors are in unrelated themes/hooks).

- 2026-05-07: Wildcard-DNS local-preview wiring (lvh.me) end-to-end through `/settings` → automation → dashboard (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app.py` (`LOCAL_PREVIEW_BASE_DOMAINS` constant + four helpers, automation short-circuit, brand serialization, settings list payload, managed-domain row flag), `next.config.js` (`allowedDevOrigins` wildcard entries for `*.lvh.me` / `*.localtest.me` / `*.localhost.test` / `*.sslip.io` / `*.nip.io`), `templates/settings.html` (Local development panel with one-click "Use lvh.me" button + "local" chip on managed-domain rows), `static/modules/ui-components.js` (dashboard brand-row link uses `preview_url` when present and shows a "local" chip).
  - Reason: Brand Studio's automation thread previously only worked end-to-end against a real Linux + Apache + PM2 + Cloudflare stack. Even with the 2026-05-07 dev-mode pass, creating a brand locally produced an inspect-only `dev-vhosts/<host>.conf` file that nothing was actually serving — operators still had to manually edit their hosts file to land on the running Next.js dev server. This change makes "create brand → click preview → it just works" the default local UX, with zero hosts-file edits, zero admin prompts, and zero Apache.
  - Applied:
    - **Public wildcard-DNS bases**: `LOCAL_PREVIEW_BASE_DOMAINS = ('lvh.me', 'localtest.me', 'localhost.test', '127.0.0.1.sslip.io', 'nip.io', 'sslip.io')`. The default base is `lvh.me`, overridable via `LOCAL_PREVIEW_BASE_DOMAIN` env. All these services resolve `*.<base>` to `127.0.0.1` at the public DNS level — no hosts edits, no Bonjour, no admin.
    - **Helpers**: `_local_preview_base_host()`, `_is_local_preview_base_domain(d)`, `_is_local_preview_host(h)`, `_local_preview_url_for_host(h)` — returns `http://<host>:<NEXT_INTERNAL_PORT|PORT|3000>` if `h` is under any known wildcard base, else `''`.
    - **Automation short-circuit**: `maybe_start_linux_brand_automation` now bails early with `status='provisioned', step='local-dev-base', preview_url=<url>` when the brand's hostname is under a wildcard base. Apache vhost write, Cloudflare DNS upsert, and PM2 restart are all skipped — they're not needed because the browser resolves the host to 127.0.0.1 via public DNS, and `proxy.ts` already routes by host header.
    - **Brand serialization**: `serialize_preview_row` now includes `preview_url` and `preview_url_kind: 'local-dev'` on any brand whose `domain` is under a wildcard base. The dashboard table reads this and links the domain cell directly to the running Next.js dev server (with a small "local" chip), instead of `https://<host>` which would 404 in dev.
    - **Settings UI**: GET `/api/settings/domains` now returns a `local_preview` block (`{base_domain, next_port, url_pattern, is_dev_mode}`) and each row carries `is_local_preview_base`. Settings page renders a "Local development" panel above the managed-domains table when in dev mode. The panel shows the resolved URL pattern and a one-click "Use lvh.me for local previews" button that POSTs to the same `/api/settings/domains` endpoint to register `lvh.me` as an active managed domain. Rows for wildcard bases get a `local` chip so they're distinguishable from production domains.
    - **Next.js dev-origin allowlist**: `next.config.js`'s `allowedDevOrigins` now permits `*.lvh.me`, `*.localtest.me`, `*.localhost.test`, `*.sslip.io`, `*.nip.io` (with and without `:3000`) so the dev server stops emitting cross-origin warnings when assets are loaded from the wildcard host.
  - Verification: `nslookup fairfield.lvh.me` returns `127.0.0.1`. Helper unit-tests cover empty / case / trailing-dot / known-base / known-subdomain / non-base inputs. `python -m py_compile app.py` clean; `node -c next.config.js` parses. Settings panel toggles correctly between "no lvh.me yet" (button visible) and "lvh.me already managed" (button hidden) based on the rows returned.
  - Follow-up: The dashboard's brand-card link now opens the local preview directly. A future addition could expose a copy-URL button for sharing the local preview link in Slack / docs without needing dashboard access. Operators wanting offline-only previews (no public DNS lookup) can still set `LOCAL_PREVIEW_BASE_DOMAIN=localhost.test` and add `127.0.0.1 *.localhost.test` to their hosts file — the same wiring picks it up.

- 2026-05-07: Pin Flask backend to Python 3.12 + dev-mode automation pipeline for Windows (carous-platform Features-Fixes.md items 4 + 6) (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `start.bat` (rewritten with `py -3.12` pin + venv-version detection), `start.sh` (mirror pin + `BRANDSTUDIO_PY` override), `requirements.txt` (header comment), `README.md` (quick-start), `app.py` (dev-mode automation: `_is_brand_automation_dev_mode()`, `_resolve_apache_sites_dir()`, dev-aware `run_cmd` + `_pm2_wait_until_online` skip + delete-flow short-circuit), `.gitignore` (ignore `dev-vhosts/*.conf`), `dev-vhosts/{README.md,.gitkeep}` (new).
  - Reason:
    1. **(item 4)** `py -m pip install -r requirements.txt` failed on this Windows box with "Getting requirements to build wheel ... error". Root cause: default `py` is Python 3.14; Pillow 10.1.0 and cryptography 42.0.8 only ship wheels through 3.12, so pip falls back to source builds that need MSVC + libjpeg + Rust + OpenSSL (none installed). Result: brandstudio dashboard could not be brought up locally.
    2. **(item 6)** Brand-automation pipeline (Apache vhost / PM2 / Cloudflare provisioning thread, the heart of preview creation) was hard-coded to bail on Windows with `if platform.system() == 'Windows': return`. Operators couldn't exercise the dashboard's status-badge polling, the lifecycle state machine, the vhost rendering, or the CF DNS upsert without a Linux VPS. "Complete local testing" was impossible.
  - Applied:
    - **Python 3.12 pin**: `start.bat` and `start.sh` now invoke `py -3.12` / `python3.12` explicitly with a `BRANDSTUDIO_PY` env override. `start.bat` detects an existing `venv/` built with the wrong Python (parses `pyvenv.cfg`) and refuses to use it, telling the operator to `rmdir /s /q venv` and re-run. `requirements.txt` carries a header comment explaining the pin + the upgrade path (Pillow → 11.x, cryptography → 44.x for 3.13/3.14 wheels).
    - **Dev mode for the automation thread**: `_is_brand_automation_dev_mode()` returns true on Windows or when `BRAND_AUTOMATION_DEV_MODE=1`. In dev mode: (a) the local `run_cmd` inside `automate()` short-circuits to a `[DEV-MODE] would run: <cmd>` log line and returns 0 — apache2ctl/a2ensite/systemctl/pm2 calls all become no-ops; (b) the rendered vhost is written to `./dev-vhosts/<subdomain>.conf` (or `DEV_VHOSTS_DIR` override) instead of `/etc/apache2/sites-available/`; (c) `_pm2_wait_until_online()` is skipped (state machine immediately advances `pm2:online` with `pm2_status='dev-mode'`); (d) the `_async_cleanup` path inside `delete_brand` removes the dev vhost on delete and logs the would-be apache + pm2 calls; (e) `maybe_restart_pm2_next_linux` (called on every brand update) logs the would-be pm2 restart instead of bailing.
    - **What still runs unchanged**: Cloudflare DNS upserts (the `requests` library is platform-agnostic; turn off via `CLOUDFLARE_DISABLED=1` as in production); the `_automation` lifecycle state machine (writes to `preview_store`, fully cross-platform); the dashboard's status-poll endpoint `/api/previews/<slug>/automation-status`; the new pre-INSERT duplicate detection and spam detection (already cross-platform).
    - **Old-domain cleanup**: `_cleanup_old_domain_resources` (used by the domain-change update flow) logs the would-be CF delete + a2dissite in dev mode rather than running them. Operators can manually trigger by setting `BRAND_AUTOMATION_DEV_MODE=0` if they have a real Apache they want to clean.
  - Verification: bytecode-compiled `app.py` clean. Demonstrated end-to-end with a fresh `py -3.12 -m venv` + `pip install -r requirements.txt` + `python -c "import flask, PIL, cryptography, pymysql, dotenv, jwt, requests"` all 7 deps importing cleanly.
  - Follow-up: The full brand-creation orchestration now runs locally; what's still missing is a Windows-friendly Next.js prod-build path (currently `app.py` doesn't trigger a Next build on brand-create — that's by design, runtime-resolved). Bumping deps for Python 3.13/3.14 support deferred until a stakeholder asks for it.

- 2026-05-07: Add automation lifecycle tracking + domain-change re-provisioning (audit items 1 & 2) (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app.py`, `backend/services/preview.py`, `static/modules/ui-components.js`, `static/style.css`. No DB schema changes — automation state is embedded inside each preview's existing `config` JSON blob under the `_automation` key, which avoids any migration on either MySQL or SQLite.
  - Reason: Two of the audit's most operator-impacting findings:
    1. **No automation-status visibility.** Previously, when DNS poll timed out the brand-create thread aborted silently and the dashboard reported 201 success. Operators discovered "the brand is gone" only when a user complained.
    2. **Domain changes were silent.** `update_brand_put` only restarted PM2; it did not re-provision the new domain or remove the old vhost. Result: changing a brand's domain in the dashboard left the OLD vhost still serving the OLD hostname while the NEW domain 404'd.
  - Applied:
    - Added `_set_automation_state` / `_get_automation_state` helpers in `app.py` that read-modify-write the preview's `config._automation` block via `preview_store` directly (bypassing `upsert_preview`'s normalize_* pipeline so status ticks don't trigger color/services re-normalization on every step). Thread-safe via a dedicated `_automation_state_lock`. Status updates never raise out of the automation thread — wrapped in try/except.
    - Updated `strip_internal_fields` in `backend/services/preview.py` to preserve `_automation` so the status survives all dashboard edits (transient internal keys like `_created_at` / `_updated_at` are still stripped because they're re-derived on read).
    - Wired status writes into `maybe_start_linux_brand_automation` at every gate and every step: `pending` (queued) → `provisioning` (starting) → `apache:rendering-vhost` → `apache:configtest` → `apache:reload` → `apache:ok` → `dns:upserting` → `dns:ok` (or `dns:failed`) → `pm2:restarting` → `pm2:online` (or `pm2:not-online`) → `live` (or `failed:<reason>`). Status `skipped` is set with reason for Windows / disabled / no-host / non-dotted-host gates.
    - Added `GET /api/previews/<slug>/automation-status` endpoint returning the live `_automation` block. Designed to be polled every 3 s while a brand-create is in flight.
    - Added `_cleanup_old_domain_resources(old_host, slug=None)` helper that spawns a daemon thread, deletes the Cloudflare DNS record for the old hostname, then disables/removes the Apache vhost under the same `_automation_critical_lock` as create. Used when a brand's domain changes.
    - Wired domain-change detection into all three update paths: `update_brand_put` (PUT, JSON), `update_brand` (POST, JSON), and `update_brand` (POST, form-data). Each captures the OLD domain BEFORE strip/merge, compares to the NEW domain after merge, and on change: marks state as `pending` with `step='domain-change'`, fires `_cleanup_old_domain_resources` for the old host, and runs the full `maybe_start_linux_brand_automation` for the new domain. Same-domain updates keep the existing PM2-restart-only flow.
    - Dashboard badge: `static/modules/ui-components.js` now renders an `.automation-badge` pill in the brand-card header showing the lifecycle status, color-coded (live=green, pending/provisioning=amber+pulse, failed=red, skipped=gray). Title attribute shows step + message/error. Cards in `pending` / `provisioning` are auto-polled every 3 s via the new endpoint until they reach a terminal status; the `setInterval` clears itself when no in-flight cards remain.
    - Update responses (PUT/POST) now include a `domain_changed` boolean so client code can choose to refresh the page or show a re-provision banner.
  - Verification: `py_compile.compile` succeeds for `app.py` and `backend/services/preview.py`; `node --check` succeeds for `ui-components.js`.
  - Still on the list: cross-process file lock for gunicorn `-w N`, hyphen-handling decision for `*.carous.co.uk`, `/healthz` endpoint.

- 2026-05-07: Apply high-priority preview-pipeline fixes from audit (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app.py` only (lines for `maybe_start_linux_brand_automation`, `_pm2_wait_until_online`, `_automation_critical_lock`, `delete_brand`, `resolve_cloudflare_ssl_paths`).
  - Reason: Acted on the highest-bang-for-buck items from the 2026-05-07 audit. The old automation thread spent up to 60 s in an unnecessary proxy-OFF / wait-for-DNS / proxy-ON dance, ignored the proxy-ON flip's return value, had no concurrency control between competing brand-creates, didn't health-check PM2 after restart, and did all of `delete_brand`'s slow ops on the request thread.
  - Applied:
    - **Collapsed the proxy-toggle dance.** Removed STEP 1 (`proxied=False`), STEP 2 (DNS poll), STEP 3 (HTTP-only intermediate vhost). The dance existed for certbot HTTP-01 challenges, which are no longer used. New flow: write HTTPS vhost → apache reload → DNS upsert (proxied=True) → PM2 restart. Saves up to 60 s per provision and removes the most common failure point.
    - **Surfaced the proxy-ON upsert failure with retry.** Old code ignored the return of `upsert_dns_record(..., proxied=True)`. New code does one retry on transient failure and logs an explicit error if both attempts fail. Doesn't abort — the Apache vhost is in place, so an operator can fix DNS manually without losing the rest.
    - **Added `_automation_critical_lock` (`threading.Lock`)** around the apache vhost write / configtest / reload section AND around the PM2 restart. Prevents two concurrent brand-creates from racing `apache2 reload` and confusing each other's configtest output. Per-process only — multi-worker gunicorn setups still need a file lock, called out in code comments.
    - **Added `_pm2_wait_until_online()` health check.** Polls `pm2 jlist` after `pm2 restart` until the named app reports `online`, with a default 30 s timeout (`PM2_HEALTH_TIMEOUT` env var). Logs a clear WARN if the app doesn't come back online so operators know to check `pm2 logs`.
    - **Made `delete_brand` async.** Synchronous portion now just validates, snapshots the domain info, deletes local files, and removes the DB record (so the dashboard reflects the deletion immediately). DNS delete + Apache disable/remove + apache2 reload + PM2 restart now run in a daemon thread that holds the same `_automation_critical_lock`. Request returns ~50 ms instead of 5–30 s.
    - **Stopped using the bare slug as an Apache site candidate** during delete cleanup. The audit found this always logs warnings because real vhost files are named after dotted hostnames, not slugs.
    - **Hardened `resolve_cloudflare_ssl_paths`** to fall back to the shared default Cloudflare cert when the per-origin file doesn't exist on disk. Previously a brand-new origin would always fail `apache2ctl configtest` because the vhost referenced a non-existent cert path.
    - **Removed `render_vhost_http_only`** — dead helper after the dance was removed; the HTTPS vhost rendered by `render_vhost` already includes a port-80 → port-443 redirect.
  - Verification: `py_compile.compile(app.py, doraise=True)` succeeds. Linux Flask import not exercised on this Windows dev box (broken venv python.exe redirect — pre-existing, not caused by these changes).
  - Still on the list (deferred): preview `automation_status` column + dashboard surfacing, domain-change automation re-run on update, file-lock for cross-process worker safety, hyphen-handling decision, `/healthz` endpoint.

- 2026-05-07: Audit preview-creation pipeline + clear three layers of dead code (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app.py` (import block + `delete_brand` SSL block), `backend/services/automation.py` (header docstring), `backend/services/dns.py` (header docstring). The Linux automation thread itself was NOT changed in this turn — only dead/shadowed code was removed and the live behaviour is documented.
  - Reason: End-to-end audit of preview creation/update/delete found that `maybe_start_linux_brand_automation`, `maybe_restart_pm2_next_linux` and `validate_dns_after_automation` were each defined twice — once in `backend/services/*` (imported at the top of `app.py`) and once locally in `app.py`. Python name resolution makes the local copies authoritative, so the imports were misleading dead code that would silently swallow any "fix" applied to `backend/services/automation.py`. Same problem with `resolve_cloudflare_ssl_paths` and `_cloudflare_origin_name_for_hostname`. Separately, `delete_brand` ran a 47-line certbot revocation block that always logged "No SSL certificate found" because creation switched to shared Cloudflare origin certs (no per-domain Let's Encrypt cert exists to revoke).
  - Applied fixes:
    - `app.py` import block: removed the shadowed `maybe_*` and `validate_dns_after_automation` imports; left a comment pointing readers at the active local definitions.
    - `backend/services/automation.py` and `backend/services/dns.py`: docstrings now state explicitly that the file is shadowed by app.py and editing it has no effect.
    - `delete_brand`: deleted the dead certbot revocation block (line 3531–3577 pre-edit). Replaced with a 5-line comment that points at git history if certbot is ever reintroduced.
    - Verified all three files parse cleanly with `python -c "ast.parse(...)"` (Linux import behaviour unchanged because the imports were already shadowed).
  - Audit findings NOT applied (require operator approval — touch the live Linux automation):
    - **Critical:** No rollback on automation failure (DNS poll timeout leaves a half-provisioned brand). Recommend adding `automation_status` column on the `previews` row and surfacing it in the dashboard.
    - **Critical:** Domain-change updates skip automation entirely (`update_brand_put` only restarts PM2). Either auto-run automation on domain change or warn the operator + offer a one-click re-provision.
    - **High:** The proxy-OFF → wait-for-DNS → proxy-ON dance was needed for certbot HTTP-01 challenges. Certbot is gone. Collapse to a single proxied DNS upsert at the top of the thread — saves up to 60s per provision and removes a moving part.
    - **High:** Step 7 (proxy-on flip) silently ignores `upsert_dns_record`'s return; if it fails the record stays DNS-only and SSL breaks. Surface and retry.
    - **High:** `delete_brand` is synchronous — Apache reload + Cloudflare API + filesystem unlinks all run on the request thread. Move to a daemon thread like create.
    - **High:** No bulk-create serialization. Two simultaneous brand creates fight for `apache2 reload`, `pm2 restart` and certbot lock. Add a process-level lock around the apache+pm2 critical section.
    - **High:** No PM2 health verification after restart. Poll `pm2 jlist` for `online` status before declaring success.
    - **Medium:** In-memory dedup is per-process (won't catch parallel gunicorn workers); move to a file lock under `/tmp` or a DB row.
    - **Medium:** Hyphen normalization mismatch — automation strips hyphens from `*.carous.co.uk` subdomains but the brand's stored domain keeps them. DB lookup by host then misses. Pick one rule and enforce it at the form.
    - **Medium:** No automation status surfaced to operators in the dashboard.
    - **Low:** TTL=300 on Cloudflare records is irrelevant when proxied; set to 1 (auto).
    - **Low:** No `/healthz` endpoint for external monitoring.
  - Next step: pick the top 2–3 from the unapplied list to schedule for follow-up turns.

- 2026-05-07: Add `springalls-classic` contract theme ported from `apps/springallscarsalesltd` (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: new directory `app/themes/springalls-classic/` and registration in `app/themes/context-registry.ts`. The carous-platform monorepo was not modified.
  - Reason: Bring the Springalls Car Sales design into Brand Studio as a 100%-configurable theme (tokens + recipes + sections + pages contract) so any dealer brand can adopt that look from the dashboard without forking the app.
  - What landed (token-driven, 1:1):
    - Foundation: `theme.json`, `tokens.ts`, `recipes/index.ts`, `sections/index.tsx` matching the Brand Studio contract.
    - Context bundle: `BrandClientWrapper`, `BrandStyles` (drives ~20 CSS vars from `brand.theme.colors`/fonts), safe `AuthProvider` (only fires for `/dashboard/*`), `DynamicFavicon`.
    - Chrome converted 1:1 from springalls CSS modules to scoped `data-theme-id='springalls-classic'` global CSS, with all hardcoded brand strings replaced by `useBrand()` reads: `Header.tsx` (logo, location, opening hours, wishlist count), `Footer.tsx` (contact, socials, address, phone, email, opening hours, year), `CookieBanner.tsx` (per-brand storage key), `Shell.tsx` (Garage provider, WhatsApp FAB).
    - Inlined four `@carous/*` workspace dependencies as small in-theme files: `lib/seo.ts`, `lib/contact.ts`, `lib/api.ts`, `lib/uk-phone.ts`, `lib/vehicle-links.ts`. WhatsApp enquiry replaced by a minimal in-theme `WhatsAppEnquiry.tsx` that builds `wa.me/<phone>` from brand config (hides if no phone configured). `GarageContext` ported with brand-scoped localStorage keys for multi-tenant isolation.
    - Home page (`pages/home/page.tsx`): Hero (brand-driven title + lead, dynamic search bar) + TrustSignalsSection + LatestArrivalsSection (live-fetches `/api/inventory`) + ServicesSection (reads `brand.services`) + CtaSection + ReviewsSection (reads `brand.testimonials`) + DirectorySection (live-fetches available makes from `/api/inventory`).
    - All 12 contract page IDs registered in `pages.ts`: home, about, contact, services, sellYourCar, usedCars, vehicleDetail, recentlySold, compare, wishlist, privacyPolicy, cookiePolicy. About/Contact/Services pages are 1:1 brand-driven; usedCars and vehicleDetail render live inventory data; recentlySold/privacy/cookie are brand-aware static pages; sellYourCar/compare/wishlist render branded placeholders that link to live actions until those pages get their dedicated 1:1 conversions in a follow-up turn.
  - What's deferred to follow-up turns:
    - Pixel-1:1 conversion of the standalone `ServiceHighlightsSection`, `LatestArrivalsSectionClient` (full carousel + filters), `SimilarVehicles`, and the `compare`, `wishlist`, `sell-my-car`, `finance`, `part-exchange` page bodies. These currently render branded placeholders or simplified equivalents.
    - Asset migration for `images/springalls-hero-home-2026.png` etc. — the theme reads `--springalls-hero-image` from `brand.heroImage` so any brand can set its own hero. The original PNG is still in the springalls app and is not copied into brandstudio.
    - `useLeadsForm` hook port (the springalls hook) — the theme's contact page currently links to `/contact` rather than embedding a form; the hook can be inlined when the form is rebuilt.
  - Verification: `npm run dev` boots clean, theme:sync reports `3 theme(s) discovered`, `GET /` returns 200 with no compile errors. Theme is selectable from the Flask dashboard via `theme.id = 'springalls-classic'` on a brand record.

- 2026-05-07: Fix brand-site reload-after-a-moment bug (owner: Difatha)
  - Scope: `app/themes/context-registry.ts`, `app/layout.tsx`, `next.config.js`
  - Reason: Brand-site visitors reported a hard refresh (white flash, same URL, no console errors) firing 5–60s after page load, in both dev and deployed environments. Root causes traced to two issues: (1) `context-registry.ts` imported `./editorial-showroom/context/{...}` from a directory that does not exist in the repo, which made the layout module fail to compile in dev (and produced an inconsistent server bundle in prod that could trigger Next.js's silent deployment-mismatch hard-reload on `<Link>` prefetch); (2) `app/layout.tsx`'s `generateMetadata` injected `Date.now()` into the favicon URL on every server render, which combined with `dynamic = 'force-dynamic'` made the metadata non-deterministic across requests — another known trigger for Next.js's RSC payload mismatch hard-reload.
  - Notes:
    - Removed dead `editorial-showroom` imports + registry entry (only `classic-dealer` and `gilded-drive` exist on disk).
    - Stripped `Date.now()` cache-buster from the server-side favicon metadata (the `DynamicFavicon` client component still updates `<link rel=icon>` hrefs at runtime when the brand changes, so cache-busting remains where it belongs).
    - Removed bogus `dynamicMetadata = 'force-dynamic'` export — not a recognised Next.js export.
    - Replaced obsolete `experimental.turbo.excludeFiles` with empty `turbopack: {}` stub. The legacy `webpack.watchOptions.ignored` block is no longer relevant under Next 16's default Turbopack — Turbopack only re-runs HMR for files in the import graph, so writes to `app/data/**`, `*.db`, `public/images/**` (which Flask makes) don't trigger reloads.
    - Local verification: `npm run dev` now boots clean on Turbopack (no `Module not found`, no `Unrecognized key turbo`, no `webpack config without turbopack` error). `GET /` returns 200 with Flask down (renders the 404 brand-not-found page, which is the expected branchwhen the brand API is unreachable).
    - Production verification still pending: needs a deploy + the user reproducing on a live brand site for 5+ minutes idle to confirm the reload no longer fires.
